# Platform audit, round 2 — 2026-08-21 (session 256)

Round 1 (`platform-audit-2026-08-21.md`) was shallow in ways its own completeness critic
named: authz opened 5 files out of ~400 routes, validation never audited a single POST body,
`packages/protocol/` had no reviewer at all, and uploads / feeds / migrations / layout / docs /
learning / video / products / messaging were untouched. Round 2 went there.

113 agents. Verifiers were required to write down the **exact reachable input** or refute.

| | Round 1 | Round 2 |
|---|---|---|
| Raw findings | 128 | 99 |
| Survived refutation | 114 (89%) | 78 (79%) |
| Confirmed and attributable | 103 | 72 |

No P0 in either round.

## The headline

**`GET /api/image-proxy` serves attacker-controlled SVG same-origin with no CSP.** Anonymous,
no flag, no cookie. The only content gate is `contentType.startsWith('image/')`, and
`image/svg+xml` passes; the bytes come back with the upstream `Content-Type` and nothing else.
An SVG carrying `<script>` therefore executes on the instance origin. See the finding below
for the two-step reproduction.

## Fixed in this session

The self-audit dimension was pointed at this session's own uncommitted diff and found five
confirmed defects in it, including a P1 that was a half-fix of a round-1 P1. All five are fixed:

| Finding | Fix |
|---|---|
| **[P1]** Key locking wired only to the registration builder; stage submission templates still rekeyed on a label edit | Per-stage key snapshot threaded `useContestEditor` → `ContestEditor` → `ContestStagesEditor` → `ContestStageCard` → `FormTemplateEditor`, plus a source-level wiring guard (`lockedKeysWiring.test.ts`) because a declared-but-unpassed prop is indistinguishable from a working fix |
| **[P2]** Markdown import regenerated every key from its label, walking past the lock | Imported fields inherit the saved key when their label matches; the confirm dialog now names the answers that would be orphaned |
| **[P3]** `balanceTags` added an empty `<p></p>` per unclosed `<p>` — the exact case it was written for | Implied-end-tag table (`p`, `li`, `dt`/`dd`, `td`/`th`/`tr`, `option`, `thead`/`tbody`) so peers close rather than nest |
| **[P3]** The missing-answers summary could not focus an agreement, radio, address or file field | `focusField` queries a focusable control first; the id-on-a-`<span>` types fall back to scroll |
| **[P3]** An option value with surrounding whitespace validated as a condition but could never match | `.trim()` on option values and `equals` entries, so stored, compared and validated are one string |

Also fixed, from the `server:false` dimension: **the registration form wiped typed answers**
when the client-only saved-answers fetch landed. A late fetch may correct a placeholder; it may
not overwrite a person's input.

## Confirmed findings

### The contest funnel's messaging

#### [P2] The `?contest=` entry association lives only in setup memory and is lost on any reload

`layers/base/pages/u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/edit.vue:45`

`const contestFromQuery = (route.query.contest as string) || ''` is captured once in setup and never persisted anywhere. `handleStarterSubmit` (edit.vue:224) then does `history.replaceState({}, '', contentEditPath(...))`, and `contentEditPath` (layers/base/composables/useContentUrl.ts:20-22) returns a bare `/u/{u}/{type}/{slug}/edit` with no query string. The association therefore survives exactly as long as the JS page instance. There is no server-side counterpart: a repo-wide grep for `pending.entry|pendingContest|pending_contest` returns exactly one hit — the comment claiming it exists.

**Failure:** An entrant on the Entries tab clicks "Create a new project" in the Submit Entry dialog, which reads "Enters automatically when you publish it" (index.vue:533). They land on `/u/me/project/new/edit?contest=qualcommforamoreresilientamerica`, type a title, and hit the starter submit — the URL is rewritten to `/u/me/project/my-build/edit`, dropping the query. They close the tab and come back the next evening to finish (or just reload after an autosave hiccup). `route.query.contest` is now empty, `contestFromQuery` is `''`, and the guard at edit.vue:302 is false. They press Publish. The project publishes, no `POST /api/contests/{slug}/entries` is ever issued, no toast appears, and they are not entered — having been told in writing that publishing would enter them. This is the reported "people aren't sure how to submit" failure with a concrete mechanism.

**Reachable input:** Authenticated entrant, registered `full` for an active contest on deveco (features.contests ON). 1) GET /contests/qualcommforamoreresilientamerica, open Submit Entry, click the "Create a new project" tile, landing on GET /u/{me}/project/new/edit?contest=qualcommforamoreresilientamerica. 2) Type a title and trigger the starter submit, which issues POST /api/content and then `history.replaceState({}, '', '/u/{me}/project/my-build/edit')` — the query is gone from the URL. 3) Close the tab (or simply reload after an autosave). 4) GET /u/{me}/project/my-build/edit — a fresh page instance where `route.query.contest` is undefined, so `contestFromQuery === ''`. 5) Finish the build and press Publish: `doPublish` issues the content PATCH and succeeds, then the guard at edit.vue:302 is false, so no POST /api/contests/qualcommforamoreresilientamerica/entries is ever issued and no toast is shown. The project is published and not entered, contradicting the "Enters automatically when you publish it" copy the entrant was shown in step 1.

**Suggested fix:** Persist the intent rather than holding it in a ref: either write it into the draft (a `contestSlug` on the content row or its metadata jsonb, consumed by the publish route server-side), or at minimum keep `?contest=` in the replaceState URL so a reload re-reads it. Then correct index.vue:313-314, which currently documents a hook that does not exist.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified against source. `contestFromQuery` at edit.vue:45 is a setup-scope `const` read from `route.query.contest`; it is never written into `metadata`, never included in `buildSaveBody()` (useContentSave.ts), and has no server persistence. `handleStarterSubmit` (edit.vue:224) rewrites the URL via `history.replaceState` to `contentEditPath(...)`, which returns `/u/{u}/{type}/{slug}/edit` with no query string (useContentUrl.ts:20-22). The auto-enter guard at edit.vue:302 (`if (contestFromQuery && contentId.value && !isNew.value)`) is therefore false on any fresh page instance, so neither the POST to `/api/contests/{slug}/entries` nor its 403 "Register for the contest" toast fires — publishing produces no entry and no message. The claimed server fallback does not exist: the only writer is `layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/entries.post.ts`, reached only from the manual submit dialog and this client block; `packages/server/src/content/content.ts` contains zero contest references, and the only hit for pending-entry across layers/ and packages/ is the incorrect comment at `contests/[slug]/index.vue:313` claiming a "server pending-entry hook". No test pins the behaviour (`git log -S contestFromQuery` returns only the introducing commit bea00495). Downgraded from P1 to P2: `features.contests` defaults to false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:31) so only deveco is affected, and the entrant retains a working manual path — after publishing, the project appears in the same Submit Entry dialog's `submittableContent` picker on the contest page. The real harm is the broken written promise on the tile ("Enters automatically when you publish it", index.vue:533) combined with total silence on the failure, which matches the reported "people aren't sure how to submit" symptom.

</details>

#### [P2] Saving /settings/notifications silently clears a one-click unsubscribe, resuming all mail

`layers/base/pages/settings/notifications.vue:30`

The page PUTs `emailNotifications` as a five-key literal `{likes, comments, follows, mentions, digest}`. `updateUserProfile` assigns it straight onto the jsonb column — `if (input.emailNotifications !== undefined) updates.emailNotifications = input.emailNotifications` (packages/server/src/profile/profile.ts:183) — which is a whole-object replace, not a merge. `unsubscribedAll`, the only hard opt-out the platform has, is not read by the page (notifications.vue:16-23 destructures only the five) and not sent back, so it is destroyed on every save. The route validator permits the key (packages/schema/src/validators/auth.ts:90), so nothing rejects the truncated object.

**Failure:** A contest reminder lands in a registrant's inbox. They click the footer "Unsubscribe" and choose "Unsubscribe from all emails"; unsubscribe.post.ts:42 writes `{...current, digest: 'none', unsubscribedAll: true}` and the page confirms "You have been unsubscribed from all emails." Weeks later they open /settings/notifications to check — the page shows four ticked checkboxes and gives no indication they are unsubscribed — and press Save. The PUT replaces the jsonb with `{likes:true, comments:true, follows:true, mentions:true, digest:'none'}`. `unsubscribedAll` is gone. The next reminder sweep's predicate `(u.email_notifications ->> 'unsubscribedAll') IS DISTINCT FROM 'true'` (reminders.ts:204) now passes and the mail resumes, as does every instant notification email and admin broadcast. The user's recorded opt-out has been reversed by an action that never mentioned it.

**Reachable input:** Authenticated as user U, whose row is `users.email_notifications = {"likes":true,"comments":true,"follows":true,"mentions":true,"digest":"none","unsubscribedAll":true}` (written by `POST /api/unsubscribe {"token":"<valid HMAC>","scope":"all"}`, unsubscribe.post.ts:42). U then sends, with their own session cookie: `PUT /api/profile` body `{"emailNotifications":{"likes":true,"comments":true,"follows":true,"mentions":true,"digest":"none"}}` — byte-for-byte what the "Save Preferences" button on /settings/notifications emits (notifications.vue:31-39). Post-state: the jsonb column holds exactly those five keys; `unsubscribedAll` is gone. `(u.email_notifications ->> 'unsubscribedAll') IS DISTINCT FROM 'true'` (reminders.ts:204, broadcast.ts:35) now evaluates true, and `emailPrefs.ts:52/:90` and `notification-email.ts:158` stop short-circuiting, so instant notification mail, digest, contest reminders and admin broadcasts all become sendable again for U.

**Suggested fix:** Merge server-side instead of replacing: read the existing `emailNotifications` in `updateUserProfile` and spread the incoming partial over it, so absent keys are preserved. Independently, surface the state — show a banner on /settings/notifications when `unsubscribedAll` is set, with an explicit "resubscribe" control, so clearing it is a deliberate act.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Mechanism verified end to end, and I found no guard anywhere on the path. 1. `layers/base/pages/settings/notifications.vue:16-24` types the `/api/profile` response with a five-key `emailNotifications` and copies only `likes/comments/follows/mentions/digest` into refs. `unsubscribedAll` is never read, never held, never displayed. `handleSave` (lines 29-40) PUTs a fresh five-key literal. 2. `layers/base/server/api/profile.put.ts` is the only handler: `requireAuth` + `parseBody(event, updateProfileSchema)` + `updateUserProfile`. No merge, no feature gate. 3. `packages/schema/src/validators/auth.ts:83-92` — every key inside `emailNotifications` is `.optional()`, including `unsubscribedAll`. A five-key object validates cleanly; nothing rejects the truncation. 4. `packages/server/src/profile/profile.ts:183` `if (input.emailNotifications !== undefined) updates.emailNotifications = input.emailNotifications;` then `db.update(users).set(updates)`. `users.emailNotifications` is a nullable jsonb (`packages/schema/src/auth.ts:41-50`, where the declared type DOES include `unsubscribedAll?: boolean`), so this is a whole-value replace. There is no `sql\`... || ...\`` jsonb merge and no read-modify-write. 5. The contrast is decisive: `layers/base/server/api/unsubscribe.post.ts:39-44` does a read-modify-write with the explicit comment "Merge so other notification toggles aren't clobbered." The intended contract is preservation; the profile PUT violates it. 6. Consumers all key on the absent flag: `packages/server/src/notification/emailPrefs.ts:52` and `:90` (the self-described "single mailability gate"), `packages/server/src/comms/broadcast.ts:35` raw-SQL `->> 'unsubscribedAll' IS DISTINCT FROM 'true'`, `packages/server/src/contest/reminders.ts:204` same predicate, `layers/base/server/plugins/notification-email.ts:158`. Every one resumes sending the moment the key vanishes. Guards I looked for and did not find: no zod `.strict()`/required key; no DB default or constraint (plain nullable jsonb, no CHECK); no feature flag on the PUT itself (`profile.put.ts` calls no `requireFeature`); no auth restriction beyond the owner's own session (the owner is exactly the actor here); no test pinning it — `layers/base/server/api/__tests__/unsubscribe-route.test.ts` only asserts the unsubscribe writer sets the key, and the sole `layers/base/pages/settings/__tests__/` file is `personaSurfaceStyleLint.test.ts`. Git history (`2879b78f fix(settings): wire notification preferences to emailNotifications column`) shows the five-key literal was written before `unsubscribedAll` existed (added in session 227), so this is an unnoticed regression, not a deliberate design. One correction to the report's evidence: `layers/base/pages/settings/profile.vue` no longer touches `emailNotifications` at all (grep returns nothing), so the plan doc's claim of two clobbering editors is stale — notifications.vue is the only one. Doesn't change the outcome. The defect is also already logged as known-unfixed at `docs/plans/persona-customization-and-audience-analytics.md:50` and `:1463` ("Fix the `emailNotifications` whole-object clobber ... to a merge, so `unsubscribedAll` survives a profile save") — documented, never fixed. Severity corrected P1 -> P2. Three reasons it is not P1: (a) it is not attacker-triggerable and not remote — it needs the victim's own authenticated Save on a settings page they must choose to visit; (b) the harmful consequence (mail resuming) requires `features.emailNotifications`, which defaults false in `packages/config/src/schema.ts:99` and `layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:129` and is ON only on deveco per `docs/STATUS.md:71-72`; (c) delivery additionally requires a deliverable adapter — `layers/base/server/utils/email.ts` falls back to `ConsoleEmailAdapter` unless `NUXT_EMAIL_ADAPTER` is smtp/resend with full credentials, and the 2026-08-21 platform audit records deveco resolving to the console sink. It is not P3 either: the destruction of the consent record happens on every instance regardless of flags (the PUT is ungated), it silently reverses a legally-meaningful RFC 8058 opt-out with zero UI indication, and nothing pins the behaviour.

</details>

#### [P2] Auto-entry on publish swallows every failure except 403, so the author is told nothing

`layers/base/pages/u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/edit.vue:307`

After `doPublish`, the auto-enter POST's catch inspects the status and toasts only for 403. Every other outcome is discarded: the 400s from entries.post.ts:32 (contest not active), :57 (not published), :60 (ineligible content type) and :67 (already entered / over `maxEntriesPerUser`), plus a 404 for an unknown or unviewable contest slug. `doPublish` has already navigated the author to the published view (useContentSave.ts:232 `await navigateTo(viewPath(...))`), so what they see is their live project and silence.

**Failure:** An entrant clicks "Create a new project" in the Submit Entry dialog — captioned "Enters automatically when you publish it" (index.vue:533) — and writes their project over the final evening. They hit Publish twenty minutes after the stage deadline, by which time the organizer has advanced the contest to `judging`. `POST /api/contests/{slug}/entries` returns 400 "This contest isn't accepting entries right now. Submissions are closed, the contest is being judged." — a message that names exactly what went wrong and that the author never sees. They land on their published project page with no toast and no error, having been told publishing would enter them. The same silence covers the over-cap case (`maxEntriesPerUser` reached, 400 at entries.post.ts:67) and the wrong-type case, where `newProjectType` picked `eligibleTypes[0]` but the author switched type mid-edit.

**Reachable input:** Deterministic, no race. Contest `summer-build` is status=active with `maxEntriesPerUser = 1`; Alice is registered at `full` tier and has already entered project A. She clicks "Submit Entry" (still rendered — index.vue:648 has no cap guard), then "Create a new project" in the dialog, landing on GET /u/alice/project/new/edit?contest=summer-build. She writes project B and clicks Publish. doPublish succeeds and navigates her to /u/alice/project/b, then edit.vue:304 issues, as authenticated Alice: POST /api/contests/summer-build/entries {"contentId":"<uuid of B>"}. submitContestEntry returns null at the cap (packages/server/src/contest/entries.ts:263-269), so entries.post.ts:67 throws 400 "Couldn't submit, you may have already entered this project, or reached the contest's entry limit." The catch computes status=400, skips the 403 branch, and returns. Alice sees her published project and no toast, having been told publishing would enter her. The same silence covers status=judging/upcoming (entries.post.ts:32) when the organizer advances the contest during a long editing session, and ineligible type (entries.post.ts:60).

**Suggested fix:** Surface the server's message for any status other than 400-from-a-publish-race: `submitToast.error(`Published, but not entered: ${extractError(err)}`)`. Distinguish the deliberate quiet case (content not yet published because validation blocked the publish) by checking `isNew`/publish success before firing the POST at all, rather than by swallowing every 400.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified by reading the code. edit.vue:303-316 catches the auto-enter failure and branches only on `status === 403`; there is no else, so all five 400s and both 404s from entries.post.ts are discarded, each of which carries a specific, author-facing statusMessage. Guard hunt found nothing that prevents it: `contests` is enabled on a live instance; there is no server-side auto-enter fallback (publish.post.ts -> onContentPublished at packages/server/src/content/content.ts:1352 does hooks, AP object id, and federation only, so index.vue:313's "server pending-entry hook" comment is stale and the swallowed client call is the only mechanism); `useToast` is useState-backed and `<AppToast />` is mounted in layouts/default.vue:241, so a toast fired after doPublish's `navigateTo` does render — the 403 branch proves the mechanism works, the other statuses just aren't wired to it. No test pins the silence. The inline comment is deliberate but scoped to one case only ("Publish-validation races stay quiet (400)" — the not-yet-published race at entries.post.ts:57); it lumps the cap, status and eligibility 400s in with that race by accident, so this is an over-broad guard, not intended behaviour. Two corrections to the claim: the 404 paths are effectively unreachable via this flow because the link is generated from a contest the user just viewed, and the strongest input is not the deadline race but the entry-cap case, which needs no race at all — index.vue:648 shows the "Submit Entry" CTA to any registered user on an active contest with no maxEntriesPerUser check, and the dialog's "Create a new project / Enters automatically when you publish it" link at :529-533 is likewise ungated.

</details>

#### [P3] Confirmation email promises deadline reminders a contestReminders-off instance never sends

`packages/infra/src/email/templates.ts:137`

`deadlineLine` emits "We will send you reminders as it approaches" (line 136) or "We will send you reminders as the submission deadline approaches" (line 137) with no reference to `config.features.contestReminders`. The template takes no flag argument and `sendParticipantConfirmation` (registrations.ts:93-142) gates only on `config.features.emailNotifications`, so the promise ships whenever email is on at all. The sweep that would fulfil it returns `{contests: 0, enqueued: 0}` immediately unless `contestReminders` is also on (reminders.ts:98-100), and that flag defaults to `false` in both config sources.

**Failure:** An operator turns on `emailNotifications` (the natural first step: they want verification and password-reset mail working) and leaves `contestReminders` at its default `false`, then runs a contest. Every registrant receives "You are now registered for X. The submission deadline is <date>. We will send you reminders as it approaches." No reminder is ever enqueued, because `sweepContestReminders` returns at line 99 on every 10-minute tick. Registrants who relied on the promised nudge — the entire reason the tier exists — miss the deadline and the instance never surfaces that reminders were off. The same promise is baked into the organizer-facing seed copy at emailDefaults.ts:30, so an organizer editing the mail is shown it as the recommended default.

**Reachable input:** On an instance configured with features.contests=true, features.emailNotifications=true and features.contestReminders left at its default false (plus a mail transport wired): an authenticated user with a verified address sends `POST /api/contests/summer-build/register` with body `{"tier":"full"}` (or a bare POST, which the schema preprocesses to tier full) to a contest in status upcoming/active whose next stage deadline is in the future and whose emailCopy has no organizer bodyHtml. layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/register.post.ts:22 gates only requireFeature('contests') + requireAuth, then calls registerForContest, whose sendParticipantConfirmation (registrations.ts:93-142) passes the guard at :94 and enqueues an outbox row containing "The submission deadline is <date>. We will send you reminders as it approaches." The 10-minute plugin tick then calls sweepContestReminders, which returns {contests:0, enqueued:0} at reminders.ts:99 on every tick, so no reminder is ever sent.

**Suggested fix:** Pass a `remindersEnabled` boolean through `sendParticipantConfirmation` into the template and drop the sentence when it is false (keep the bare deadline line). Fix `defaultContestEmailBlocks('confirmation')` the same way, or have the email editor warn the organizer when the seeded reminders sentence is present and the flag is off.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Code facts verified by reading, not restated. templates.ts:113-120 takes no config/flag argument, and lines 133-137 emit "We will send you reminders as it approaches" in both non-empty branches; the only suppression is an organizer-authored copy.bodyHtml. registrations.ts:94 gates the send on `config.features.emailNotifications && email` and never on contestReminders (it checks contestEmailEditor at :101, proving flag-awareness was on the author's mind for other flags). reminders.ts:98-100 and layers/base/server/plugins/contest-reminders.ts:44 both return early unless contestReminders is also on, and the flag defaults false in packages/config/src/schema.ts:60, layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:116 and packages/test-utils/src/mockConfig.ts:26. I searched for a guard and found none: no validator, no auth check, no DB constraint, no test pinning the behaviour (contest-email-copy.integration.test.ts runs with contestReminders:true), and no other code path emits deadline reminders when the flag is off. Git shows faa5bb3f added the confirmation template and the contestReminders flag in the SAME commit, so this is an oversight, not deliberate design. Two corrections that lower severity from the claimed P2 to P3: (1) no live instance is in the claimed configuration — docs/STATUS.md:71-72 and the session-231 /api/features audit record deveco with emailNotifications AND contestReminders both ON (promise kept), while commonpub.io and heatsynclabs.io have emailNotifications OFF entirely (no confirmation mail at all), so this is latent for downstream self-hosters only, and the admin toggle at layers/base/pages/admin/features.vue:31 names the dependency explicitly; (2) the email is not the unique offender — no component reads contestReminders, so ContestSignup.vue:130/220/247/303 make the same ungated reminder promise on screen, meaning the mail is consistent with the shipped product rather than uniquely mismatched. Impact is copy-only: the same email still states the real deadline, and nothing is lost, leaked or corrupted.

</details>

### Is it clear how to submit a proposal?

#### [P3] Rejected proposal shows only a transient toast: no inline error, aria-invalid, focus move or summary

`layers/base/components/contest/ContestProposalForm.vue:40`

`submit()` blocks on `missingRequired` and calls `toast.error(`Please complete: ${labels}`)`, then returns. The form renders `ContestSubmissionField` (lines 70-76) passing only `:field`, `v-model` and `id-prefix` — it never passes `:invalid`, so `aria-invalid`, the `aria-describedby` error link and the red border in `ContestSubmissionField.vue:36-41` stay inert, and no error paragraph exists to point at. This is the exact gap `ContestRegistrationForm` was fixed for in this same session: it passes `:invalid="showsError(f.key)"` (ContestRegistrationForm.vue:182), renders a per-field error (line 195), renders a role=alert summary of what is missing with buttons that focus each field (lines 201-210), and moves focus to the first offender (lines 152-158). `ContestStageSubmission.vue:110-116` has the same omission.

**Failure:** An entrant fills a proposal template whose fields include an unticked required agreement partway up the form, presses "Submit proposal", and gets a toast that disappears after 5 seconds (useToast.ts:28) naming a label. Nothing on the page is marked, focus has not moved, and on a template with a `terms` block (ContestSubmissionField.vue:107, `max-height: 160px` scroller) the offending control can be several screens above the button. A screen-reader user gets no `aria-invalid` at all. Repeating the press repeats the toast.

**Reachable input:** Reference app (contestProposals: true in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:22), or any instance after an admin does PUT /api/admin/features {"overrides":{"contestProposals":true}}: sign in as a member, open GET /contests/<slug> for an active contest whose current stage is a proposal-mode submission stage with submissionTemplate [{"key":"project_name","label":"Project name","type":"text","required":true}, ...], leave project_name blank, click "Submit proposal". blockingFields() returns ["Project name"], submit() fires toast.error("Please complete: Project name") and returns; the rendered DOM contains no aria-invalid, no #cpub-proposal-project_name-error, no role=alert summary, and document.activeElement stays on the submit button. The toast is removed 5s later (useToast.ts:28). The same holds with NO flag change on the default-on twin: an entrant with an eligible entry on a contest stage carrying a required template field clicks "Submit" in ContestStageSubmission.vue (id-prefix "cpub-stagesub"), gets toast.error("Please fill: ...") and identically unmarked fields.

**Suggested fix:** Lift the `touched`/`attempted`/`showsError` + missing-answers-summary block out of ContestRegistrationForm into a shared composable and use it in ContestProposalForm and ContestStageSubmission — the three forms already share `blockingFieldKeys`, `visibleTemplateFields` and `buildSubmissionPayload` from utils/contestSubmission.ts, so only the presentation is forked.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The code is exactly as described and I read every cited line in the working tree. ContestProposalForm.vue:38-43 fails validation with a bare toast.error() + return, and its ContestSubmissionField instances (lines 70-76) omit `:invalid`, so aria-invalid, the aria-describedby error link and any error text never appear; there is no error paragraph, no role=alert summary and no focus move. The `invalid` prop was added to ContestSubmissionField in THIS session's uncommitted diff and wired up in ContestRegistrationForm (+140 lines: `:invalid="showsError(f.key)"`, per-field `<p :id="`${idPrefix}-${f.key}-error`">`, role=alert summary with focus buttons, focusField on failed save) while ContestProposalForm received only the visibleTemplateFields change (+8 lines) — so this is a genuine unapplied-to-siblings gap, not a design choice restated. ContestStageSubmission.vue:62-65,110-116 has the same omission and its flag (contestStageSubmissions) defaults to TRUE, so the class is live. No guard exists: the existing test pins the toast but does not forbid inline errors; there is no upstream validator, auth check or type that prevents the state. Severity corrected down: the toast is role=alert inside aria-live=polite (AppToast.vue:7-13) and names the missing field labels, so the message IS announced and WCAG 3.3.1 is arguably satisfied; nothing is lost and retry works. The cited surface is also behind contestProposals, which defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:38, layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:110) and is documented OFF on deveco/heatsync/commonpub.io (docs/STATUS.md:493,1249) — only apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:22 enables it. That makes it a P3 UX/a11y polish inconsistency, not P2.

</details>

### Upload, storage and media pipeline

#### [P2] create-commonpub scaffolds no /uploads serving route, so every local-storage upload 404s in production

`tools/create-commonpub/src/template.rs:191`

The scaffolded nuxt.config serves local uploads with `nitro.publicAssets: [{ dir: '../uploads', baseURL: '/uploads' }]`. Nitro's publicAssets are snapshotted into `.output/public` at BUILD time, so files the LocalStorageAdapter writes at RUNTIME are never served by a production build. apps/reference already knows this — its nuxt.config (lines 39-44) deliberately has an EMPTY `nitro: {}` with the comment "Local uploads (LocalStorageAdapter) are written at RUNTIME, which Nitro's build-time `publicAssets` can't serve" and ships `apps/reference/server/routes/uploads/[...path].get.ts` to stream them instead. `scaffold.rs:38-85` writes package.json, nuxt.config.ts, commonpub.config.ts, server/utils/config.ts, components/, assets/, public/, scripts/, Dockerfile — and no `server/routes/` at all. `layers/base/server/routes/` (actor, inbox, feed.xml, sitemap.xml, r/, content/, hubs/, users/, .well-known/) contains no uploads route either, so the layer does not supply the missing piece. The `dir` is wrong on a second axis too: scaffold.rs:44 creates `<app>/uploads`, `createStorageFromEnv` (packages/infra/src/storage.ts:373) defaults UPLOAD_DIR to `./uploads` = `<app>/uploads`, but publicAssets points at `'../uploads'` — the app's PARENT. So the path is wrong even in dev, where publicAssets would otherwise serve live from disk.

**Failure:** Operator runs `create-commonpub`, leaves the S3_* block commented out (the generated .env comments them out by default — template.rs:491-500), builds and deploys. A user uploads an avatar: POST /api/files/upload succeeds, the files row is written, and the response returns `url: http://<site>/uploads/avatar/<uuid>.webp`. Every request to that URL returns 404 — no handler is registered for /uploads/**, and .output/public contains only what existed at build time. Every avatar, banner, cover image and content image on the instance is a broken image, permanently, with no error anywhere in the logs.

**Reachable input:** Operator: `cargo install create-commonpub && create-commonpub my-hub`, accept defaults (leave the S3_* block in the generated .env commented out as shipped), `docker build -t my-hub . && docker run -e DATABASE_URL=... -p 3000:3000 my-hub` (the generated Dockerfile runs `pnpm build` then `node .output/server/index.mjs`). Then, as any authenticated registered user: POST /api/files/upload (multipart/form-data; part 0 = a JPEG/PNG; field `purpose=avatar`) → 201 { id, url: "http://my-hub.example/uploads/avatar/<uuid>.webp", ... } and a `files` row with that publicUrl. Then any request, authenticated or not: GET http://my-hub.example/uploads/avatar/<uuid>.webp → 404. No handler is registered for /uploads/** (layer has none, scaffolder writes none) and `.output/public` was snapshotted from `<app>/../uploads` at build time — a directory that both does not exist in the build context and would not contain a file written seconds ago at runtime. Same 404 for every cover/banner/content image thereafter. Dev is also broken by the same config: `pnpm dev` serves publicAssets live, but from `<app>/../uploads` while LocalStorageAdapter writes to `<app>/uploads`.

**Suggested fix:** Emit the streaming route (a copy of apps/reference/server/routes/uploads/[...path].get.ts, including its traversal guard, nosniff and SVG-sandbox CSP) from the scaffolder, or better move it into layers/base/server/routes/ so every consumer app gets it; and drop the publicAssets block.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED, and git history makes it stronger than the report. `render_nuxt_config` (tools/create-commonpub/src/template.rs:186-196) emits `nitro: { preset: 'node-server', publicAssets: [{ dir: '../uploads', baseURL: '/uploads', maxAge: ... }] }`. That block is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the one deleted from apps/reference/nuxt.config.ts in commit d071a1c0 ("fix(uploads): image upload 500 (EACCES) + local-storage serving"), whose message — written from live commonpub.io prod logs — says: "Nothing served runtime uploads. Added server/routes/uploads/[...path].get.ts ... Removed the dead publicAssets entry that 404'd before it." The fix was applied to the reference app, the root Dockerfile, .gitignore and packages/infra, and never propagated to the scaffolder template. So this is not a theoretical Nitro-semantics argument: the identical configuration was empirically observed 404ing in production and was deliberately deleted as dead. Guard hunt, all negative: - `write_instance` (scaffold.rs:38-88) creates assets/, components/, server/utils/, public/, uploads/, scripts/, .github/, .do/ — no `server/routes/` and no uploads handler. - `find layers/base/server/routes -type f` returns 23 files (actor, inbox, feed.xml, sitemap.xml, robots.txt, nodeinfo, r/[code], content/[slug], users/*, hubs/*, .well-known/*) — none under uploads. The published `@commonpub/layer` therefore does not supply it. layers/base/nuxt.config.ts has `routeRules: {}`, no `publicAssets`, no `nitro.handlers`; nothing in server/middleware/ touches /uploads. - Storage default is local, not S3: `createStorageFromEnv` (packages/infra/src/storage.ts:373-381) falls through to `LocalStorageAdapter` unless S3_BUCKET is set, and the generated .env (template.rs:491-500) ships every S3_* var COMMENTED OUT. LocalStorageAdapter line 126 returns `${baseUrl}/uploads/${key}`, so the DB row and the API response carry a URL nothing serves. - No test pins the correct behaviour: tools/create-commonpub/tests/cli.rs only asserts `uploads/.gitkeep` exists (line 41); it never asserts a serving route. - The bundled sample tools/create-commonpub/reference-app/nuxt.config.ts carries the same stale block, confirming it is un-updated copy, not a deliberate choice. - Second axis confirmed: scaffold.rs:44 creates `<app>/uploads`, UPLOAD_DIR defaults to `./uploads` = `<app>/uploads`, but `dir: '../uploads'` resolves against rootDir to the app's PARENT — so even the dev-mode live serving that publicAssets would give you points at the wrong directory. - The scaffolded Dockerfile runs as root with no USER directive and no pre-created dir, so unlike the reference app's original EACCES, the WRITE succeeds here. Upload returns 201; only the read 404s. Silent by construction — no error is logged on either side. Severity corrected P1 → P2. It is a total, permanent media outage (every avatar, banner, cover and content image) on any instance scaffolded by the CLI without S3, but: it is a scaffolder defect in tools/, not in the runtime that any live instance runs; no current production instance (commonpub.io, deveco, heatsync) is affected, since they run the reference app / forks that already carry the fix; there is no security or data-loss dimension (bytes land on disk correctly, and dropping in the route recovers every file retroactively); and the generated .env documents the S3 path that sidesteps it entirely. Broken-images-on-fresh-install for a crates.io-published scaffolder (v0.5.29) is a serious P2, not a P1.

</details>

#### [P3] processImage stores the original before generating variants, so a decode failure orphans the bytes with no files row

`packages/infra/src/image.ts:74`

`processImage` calls `sharp(data).metadata()` (header parse only), then immediately `await storage.upload(originalKey, data, ...)` at line 74, and only THEN enters the variant loop (lines 80-96) which fully DECODES the image. If any variant throws, the exception propagates out of `processImage`, out of `layers/base/server/api/files/upload.post.ts:75`, and the request 500s — after the original bytes are already committed to S3/local storage and BEFORE the `db.insert(files)` at upload.post.ts:94. Nothing catches the throw and nothing deletes the already-written object. The result is object-storage bytes with no `files` row: not in /api/files/mine, not deletable via /api/files/[id], not in the DSAR export (packages/server/src/profile/export.ts:554-564 selects from `files`), not reachable by `collectUserPrivateFileKeys` on account deletion, and not reachable by the contest sweep (which is scoped to purpose='contest' AND visibility='private').

**Failure:** I verified the trigger empirically against the repo's sharp 0.34.5: build a valid 2000x2000 PNG (60,167 bytes), truncate it to 40%, then `sharp(trunc, {limitInputPixels: 100000000}).metadata()` returns `png 2000 2000` (OK) while `.resize(150,...).webp().toBuffer()` throws `pngload_buffer: libspng read error`. So an authenticated user POSTs a truncated PNG to /api/files/upload with purpose='attachment' (allowed at upload.post.ts:42, capped at 100MB by MAX_UPLOAD_SIZES). Content-Length guard passes, validateUpload passes (image/png is allowed), metadata() passes, the 100MB original is uploaded, the thumb variant throws, the route 500s, no files row exists. The upload rate limit is 10/min, so one account can permanently strand ~1GB/min of unreclaimable paid object storage, and no cleanup path in the codebase can ever find those keys.

**Reachable input:** As ANY authenticated user (only `requireAuth` at upload.post.ts:16 — no admin, no flag, no ownership check), with a valid session cookie and a same-origin `Origin` header to satisfy layers/base/server/middleware/csrf.ts: POST /api/files/upload Content-Type: multipart/form-data part 1: name=file, filename="x.png", Content-Type: image/png, body = the first 40% of a valid 2000x2000 PNG (I generated one at 60,175 bytes and truncated to 24,070) part 2: name=purpose, body = "attachment" (in validPurposes at line 42; 100MB cap via MAX_UPLOAD_SIZES) Trace: Content-Length guard passes (<128MB) → readMultipartFormData OK → purpose valid, isPrivate=false so no requireFeature → validateUpload passes (image/png ∈ ALLOWED_MIME_TYPES, size < 100MB) → isProcessableImage('image/png')=true → processImage → sharp().metadata() SUCCEEDS (verified: `png 2000 2000`) → storage.upload writes `attachment/<uuid>.png` to S3/UPLOAD_DIR → thumb variant `.resize(150,...).webp().toBuffer()` THROWS `pngload_buffer: libspng read error` (verified) → exception propagates out of processImage, out of the handler, request returns 500 → line 94 `db.insert(files)` never runs. Observable end state: the object `attachment/<uuid>.png` exists in the bucket / upload dir, and `SELECT * FROM files WHERE storage_key = 'attachment/<uuid>.png'` returns zero rows. Repeatable up to 10 times per minute per the upload rate-limit tier; swap `purpose=attachment` for a same-shaped 100MB truncated PNG to make each orphan 100MB.

**Suggested fix:** Generate the variants first (or wrap the whole of processImage so a failure deletes every key it has already written), and insert the files row in the same failure-aware unit as the storage writes.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED as a mechanism, but the severity and the "permanently unreclaimable" framing are overstated. What I read myself: - `packages/infra/src/image.ts` in full. `processImage` has exactly one try/catch, and it wraps only `sharp(data, SHARP_OPTIONS).metadata()` (lines 63-68). Line 73-74 generates `originalKey` and `await storage.upload(originalKey, data, ...)` unconditionally. The variant loop (80-96) then calls `.resize().webp().toBuffer()` — the first full pixel decode — with no try/catch and no compensating `storage.delete(originalKey)` on any path. There is no rollback anywhere in the function. - `layers/base/server/api/files/upload.post.ts` in full. It is the ONLY non-dist call site of `processImage` (grep across packages/*/src + layers). Line 75 calls it un-wrapped; the `db.insert(files)` is at line 94. Nothing between them catches, and there is no route-level try/catch, so a throw from the variant loop propagates to Nitro as a 500 with the original bytes already committed and zero `files` rows written. - Guard hunt came up empty. `validateUpload` (packages/infra/src/storage.ts:413-428) checks only MIME membership in `ALLOWED_MIME_TYPES` and a per-purpose size cap — it never parses the image. `isProcessableImage` is a Set lookup on the client-supplied `Content-Type`. No feature flag gates the public path (only `isPrivate` → `requireFeature('contestPrivateFiles')`, and that branch skips `processImage` entirely). The CSRF middleware is an Origin/Referer host check, satisfied by any same-origin scripted client. `packages/infra/src/__tests__/image.test.ts` contains no throw/failure assertions, so nothing pins the current behaviour as deliberate. `git log -- packages/infra/src/image.ts` shows the last touch (4fd07efb, sharp DoS hardening) added `limitInputPixels` and the metadata try/catch without addressing write ordering. - The only orphan sweeper in the codebase is `sweepOrphanedContestFiles` (packages/server/src/contest/submissions.ts:118), driven by `layers/base/server/plugins/contest-file-sweep.ts`. Its DELETE is hard-scoped `WHERE f.purpose = 'contest' AND f.visibility = 'private'` and, critically, it works from `files` ROWS outward — it can only ever reach bytes that have a row. Bytes with no row are invisible to it by construction. I reproduced the trigger against the repo's own sharp 0.34.5 (node_modules/.pnpm/sharp@0.34.5), not from memory: a real 2000x2000 PNG (60,175 bytes) truncated to 40% gives `metadata OK png 2000 2000`, then `.resize(150,undefined,{fit:'inside',withoutEnlargement:true}).webp({quality:80}).toBuffer()` throws `pngload_buffer: libspng read error`. That is exactly the metadata-passes / decode-fails window the finding describes, and `originalWidth = 2000 > 150` so the thumb variant is not skipped by the line-82 guard. Severity corrected P2 → P3. Two claims in the finding do not hold up: 1. "No cleanup path in the codebase can ever find those keys" is true of the codebase but not of the system. `files.storage_key` is a plain column; an operator reclaims orphans with a bucket/`UPLOAD_DIR` listing diffed against `SELECT storage_key FROM files`. It is manual toil, not permanent loss. 2. "One account can permanently strand ~1GB/min of unreclaimable paid object storage" ignores that the same account, at the same 10/min upload tier (packages/infra/src/security.ts:250), can consume storage at the identical rate with SUCCESSFUL 100MB attachment uploads. The marginal harm of this bug is that the bytes lack a row (so they miss /api/files/mine, /api/files/[id] delete, the DSAR export, and account-deletion key collection), not that they consume space faster. That is a consistency/ops-hygiene defect plus a small GDPR-completeness wrinkle, not a storage-exhaustion vector. The realistic non-malicious case — a user uploading a genuinely corrupt image, getting a 500, and leaving <=10MB behind (cover/content cap) — is small and rare. Real, worth fixing (upload the original AFTER the variants succeed, or wrap in try/catch and delete written keys on failure), but P3.

</details>

#### [P3] upload-from-url never inserts a files row: no uploader record, no size cap, no delete path, no DSAR

`layers/base/server/api/files/upload-from-url.post.ts:48`

The handler authenticates (`requireAuth(event)` at line 10, whose return value is discarded), SSRF-fetches the remote image, uploads it via `storage.upload(key, buffer, contentType)` and returns `{ url: resultUrl }`. It never touches the `files` table. Compare upload.post.ts:94-109, which records uploaderId/mimeType/sizeBytes/storageKey/purpose/visibility. Consequences, each verified against the corresponding reader: the object never appears in GET /api/files/mine (mine.get.ts:17 filters on files.uploaderId); it can never be deleted, because DELETE /api/files/[id] takes a `files.id` that does not exist; it is absent from the DSAR export (packages/server/src/profile/export.ts:554-564 selects from `files`); and account deletion cannot purge it (layers/base/server/utils/purgeUserFiles.ts:26-31 selects from `files`). It also skips `validateUpload` entirely, so the per-purpose caps in MAX_UPLOAD_SIZES are not applied — only safeFetchBinary's flat 10MB streaming cap is.

**Failure:** A user imports a markdown document via useMarkdownImport (layers/base/composables/useMarkdownImport.ts:47), which POSTs each remote image URL to this route. Twenty images are rehosted into the public bucket with zero DB records. The user later deletes their account: deleteUser cascades the `files` rows they DO have and purgeUserFiles deletes their private bytes, but these twenty objects have no row to cascade and no key to collect, so they remain in the bucket, publicly readable at their stable URLs, forever. The same user's Art. 15 export lists none of them. Separately: POST {url: <a 9MB remote jpeg>, purpose: 'avatar'} stores a 9MB avatar, bypassing the 2MB avatar cap that upload.post.ts enforces.

**Reachable input:** Authenticated user (any role) issues: POST /api/files/upload-from-url Content-Type: application/json {"url":"https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/x.jpg","purpose":"content"} This exact request is produced automatically by the product: layers/base/composables/useMarkdownImport.ts:47 fires it once per remote image block when a user imports a markdown file in the editor. Result: bytes land at `content/<uuid>.jpg` in the PUBLIC bucket via `storage.upload` (upload-from-url.post.ts:48), the handler returns `{url}`, and `SELECT * FROM files` gains zero rows. The user then sees the image absent from GET /api/files/mine (the "my files" picker at layers/base/components/editors/ArticleEditor.vue:114), has no id to pass to DELETE /api/files/[id], and their Art. 15 export's file-metadata section (packages/server/src/profile/export.ts:554-564, `.from(files).where(eq(files.uploaderId, userId))`) omits it. For the size-cap half of the claim the reachable input is a hand-crafted request only — no caller sends it: POST /api/files/upload-from-url {"url":"<8MB remote jpeg>","purpose":"avatar"} stores an 8MB object under avatars/ where validateUpload would have enforced MAX_UPLOAD_SIZES.avatar = 2MB.

**Suggested fix:** Run validateUpload against contentType/buffer.length/purpose, then insert a `files` row exactly as upload.post.ts does, and return the id alongside the URL.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The core code fact is real and I confirmed it by reading the whole handler: layers/base/server/api/files/upload-from-url.post.ts is 51 lines, imports only `generateStorageKey, ALLOWED_IMAGE_TYPES, safeFetchBinary`, never calls `useDB()`, never imports `files`, and its only write is `storage.upload(key, buffer, contentType)` at :48. `requireAuth(event)` at :10 discards its return, so the uploader identity is never captured. Contrast upload.post.ts:94-109, which inserts uploaderId/mimeType/sizeBytes/storageKey/purpose/visibility. No feature flag gates the route, no `requireFeature` call, no admin check, no test in layers/base/server/api/files/__tests__/ (only private-files-route.test.ts) pins either behaviour, and git log on the file shows only SSRF and storage-key-argument fixes — nothing documenting the row-less write as a decision. So the defect exists, is reachable by any logged-in user, and is exercised by shipped UI. Three of the four claimed consequences hold as stated: no uploader record, no delete path (DELETE /api/files/[id] matches on files.id AND files.uploaderId, so a non-existent row 404s), and omission from the DSAR file list. But two load-bearing pieces of the finding are wrong, and they are the scary ones: 1. The Art. 17 erasure scenario is refuted by the code it cites. layers/base/server/utils/purgeUserFiles.ts:11-19 is an explicit, documented design decision to purge PRIVATE files only: "public files are intentionally left (their bytes were already orphaning on delete pre-existing, harmlessly). Full public-file erasure would need reference-counting and is out of scope here." `collectUserPrivateFileKeys` filters `and(eq(uploaderId), eq(files.visibility,'private'))`. A public content image uploaded through upload.post.ts — row and all — is equally never purged from the bucket on account deletion. The claimed "twenty objects remain publicly readable forever, unlike their other files" is a distinction that does not exist. This is intended behaviour restated, not a defect of this route. 2. The size-cap bypass is inert on the only real path. safeFetchBinary caps at MAX_RESPONSE_SIZE = 10MB (packages/protocol/src/ssrf.ts:149), and MAX_UPLOAD_SIZES.content is also 10MB (pinned at packages/infra/src/__tests__/storage.test.ts:139). For purpose 'content' — the only value any caller sends — the caps are identical, so nothing is bypassed. Only a hand-crafted purpose:'avatar' (2MB) or 'banner' (5MB) request differs, and even there the cap is not a security boundary: layers/base/server/api/profile.put.ts:12 accepts an arbitrary `avatarUrl` string, so a user can already point their avatar at any URL of any size. The residual impact is up to 8MB of extra authenticated bucket writes, with no per-user storage quota in the codebase to bypass (grep for "quota" hits only federation mirroring). What survives is a data-hygiene/completeness gap: URL-rehosted public images have no ownership row, so they are invisible to the file picker, individually undeletable, and missing from the Art. 15 export's file section (their URLs do still appear in the exported contentVersions content). No auth bypass, no privacy leak beyond content the user deliberately published, no erasure regression relative to every other public upload, no effective cap bypass on the shipped path. That is P3, not P2.

</details>

#### [P3] WebP variant keys are never persisted, so deleting a file leaves its derivative images served forever

`layers/base/server/api/files/upload.post.ts:94`

processImage writes up to four derivative objects per image (thumb/small/medium/large, keys `${purpose}/variants/<uuid>.webp`, image.ts:92-93) and returns them. upload.post.ts:81-86 folds them into a `variants` map that is returned to the client and then discarded — the `db.insert(files)` at line 94 does not persist it, and `packages/schema/src/files.ts:8-35` has no column for it (id, uploaderId, filename, originalName, mimeType, sizeBytes, storageKey, publicUrl, purpose, visibility, contentId, hubId, width, height, createdAt). So the platform has no record of the derivative keys anywhere. `DELETE /api/files/[id]` ([id].delete.ts:33) therefore deletes only `result[0].storageKey` — the original — and the four derivatives survive. Account deletion is worse: purgeUserFiles.ts:26-31 is scoped to `visibility = 'private'`, and variants only ever exist for public images, so they are excluded by design.

**Failure:** A user uploads a personal photo as a blog cover. processImage stores the original plus four WebP derivatives; the medium/large derivatives are full-resolution enough to be the same picture. The user changes their mind and deletes the file from the media library: DELETE /api/files/<id> returns `{deleted: true}` and the original disappears. `https://<bucket>/content/variants/<uuid>.webp` (and three siblings) still return the photo, and nothing in the system knows those keys exist to ever remove them. Same outcome on full account deletion.

**Reachable input:** Any authenticated member, no special role: 1. `POST /api/files/upload` — multipart body, part 1 = a JPEG wider than 1200px (e.g. a 3000x2000 photo), plus a part `purpose=cover`. `isProcessableImage('image/jpeg')` is true (ALLOWED_IMAGE_TYPES, storage.ts:385-390), `isPrivate` is false, so `processImage` runs and writes 5 public objects: `cover/<uuidA>.jpg` plus `cover/variants/<uuidT>.webp`, `<uuidS>`, `<uuidM>`, `<uuidL>` (150/300/600/1200). The `files` row records only `storageKey = cover/<uuidA>.jpg`. The four variant keys exist nowhere in the DB. 2. `DELETE /api/files/<id>` as the same uploader. `contestIdsForPrivateFile` returns empty (public file), the row deletes, and `[id].delete.ts:33` calls `adapter.delete(storageKey)` for the single original key. Response `{"deleted": true}`. Post-condition, verifiable against the bucket or the local uploads dir: `cover/<uuidA>.jpg` is gone; the four `cover/variants/*.webp` objects remain, including the 1200px `large` derivative, and no row, index, or job anywhere in the system holds their keys, so nothing can ever remove them. Same post-condition on full account deletion via `/api/auth/delete-user` (collectUserPrivateFileKeys filters `visibility = 'private'`).

**Suggested fix:** Add a `variants jsonb` column to `files`, write it on upload, and iterate it in [id].delete.ts and in the account-erasure purge.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED as a real, reachable storage-hygiene/erasure-completeness defect, but downgraded P2 -> P3 because the claimed privacy exposure does not hold. Verified by reading every link in the chain: image.ts:80-96 uploads each variant to a public key `${purpose}/variants/<randomUUID>.webp` and returns key+url; upload.post.ts:81-86 folds them into a local `variants` map that escapes only as a JSON response field, while the insert at :94-109 persists no variant data; packages/schema/src/files.ts has no column capable of holding it (no jsonb, no metadata blob); [id].delete.ts:20-38 returns exactly `{id, storageKey, visibility}` and deletes one key. Repo-wide, the literal `/variants` occurs in exactly ONE place -- image.ts:92, the write site. There is no reader, no orphan sweep, no lifecycle job. Guards checked and absent: no feature flag covers this path (the route's only `requireFeature('contestPrivateFiles')` gates the PRIVATE branch, which deliberately skips processImage altogether, so it cannot suppress variant creation); no DB constraint; no test pins cleanup. Git history (560f1152, ad31c8af, 0cd12b14) shows the delete path was hardened for the private/public key-space split with a detailed comment that never mentions variants -- an omission, not a decision. Two corrections to the finding: (1) The "photo stays publicly served" framing is overstated. No UI code consumes the upload response's `variants` map -- grepped layers/base/components, pages, composables, packages/ui/src, packages/editor/src: zero consumers. Variant URLs are never rendered, never placed in a srcset, never embedded in content. Keys are randomUUID (storage.ts:43), so they are not enumerable. The only party that ever receives a variant URL is the uploader's own browser in its own upload response. So the impact is orphaned bytes and incomplete erasure, not content reachable by a third party. (2) The account-deletion half is documented intended behaviour, not a defect. purgeUserFiles.ts carries an explicit SCOPE docstring: public-file bytes are deliberately left because their direct bucket URLs may be embedded in other users' content, and reference-counted public erasure is out of scope. Only the single-file DELETE path is a true gap, since that path does intend to remove the bytes and carefully handles the private/public adapter split. Net: unbounded orphaned-object growth (4x per image, forever) and derived copies of user content surviving deletion with no pointer to remove them. Real and worth fixing -- the natural fix is a `variants` jsonb column on `files` plus a loop in [id].delete.ts -- but with no third-party read path it is P3.

</details>

#### [P3] Rate-limit key collapses all of /api/files/* into one bucket while the tier differs

`packages/infra/src/security.ts:312`

`checkRateLimit` picks the TIER from the full pathname (`getTierForPath`, :256-277: `/api/files/upload*` → upload tier, limit 10/min; everything else under `/api/` → api tier, limit 60/min) but derives the COUNTER KEY from only the first two path segments: `pathname.split('/').slice(0, 3).join('/')` → `/api/files` for all of them. So `POST /api/files/upload`, `GET /api/files/mine`, `GET /api/files/<id>/raw` and `DELETE /api/files/<id>` all increment ONE shared counter, which is then compared against whichever limit the current request happens to map to (MemoryRateLimitStore.check, :194-210, does `existing.count++` then `if (existing.count > tier.limit)`). `/api/files` is the only prefix where two tiers collide — /api/auth/* is uniformly the auth tier, /api/social/* uniformly social, everything else uniformly api.

**Failure:** A user edits an article in ArticleEditor. Opening the media library issues `GET /api/files/mine?limit=30` (ArticleEditor.vue:114) — count 1. They upload 6 images — count 7. They delete 2 unwanted ones — count 9. They reopen the library — count 10. The next upload is request 11 within the window and is rejected with 429 Too Many Requests, even though only 6 uploads occurred against a nominal 10/min upload budget. The same collision means an organizer opening a handful of private contest files via /api/files/<id>/raw silently consumes their own upload allowance.

**Reachable input:** As an authenticated user on a production instance (NODE_ENV=production, so layers/base/server/middleware/security.ts:57 does not skip): load `/u/<username>/blog/<slug>/edit` (the only page mounting ArticleEditor). Its onMounted fires `GET /api/files/mine?limit=30`, which increments key `user:<id>:/api/files` to 1 under the api tier (limit 60, allowed). Then within the same 60s window issue ten `POST /api/files/upload` requests (multipart FormData, field `purpose=content`, a <10MB image each) - the flow the editor's file input drives via composables/useFileUpload.ts:53. Those take the counter to 2..11. The tenth POST is count 11, compared against the upload tier limit of 10, so MemoryRateLimitStore.check returns allowed:false and the middleware throws 429 Too Many Requests - after only 9 uploads have actually succeeded against a nominal 10/min upload budget.

**Suggested fix:** Include the tier name in the key (e.g. `user:<id>:upload` vs `user:<id>:/api/files`), so each tier gets its own window.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Mechanism confirmed by reading the code. security.ts:310 selects the tier from the FULL pathname (getTierForPath, :261 `/api/files/upload` -> upload tier limit 10; :274 everything else under /api/ -> api tier limit 60) while :311 derives the counter key from only the first two segments (`pathname.split('/').slice(0,3).join('/')` = `/api/files` for all of them). Both stores increment the shared key and compare against the caller's own tier: MemoryRateLimitStore.check :205-206 and RedisRateLimitStore.check (packages/infra/src/redis/rateLimitStore.ts:82-85). Both tiers use windowMs 60_000, so even the Redis windowStart bucket is identical - nothing separates them. The middleware is live in production (layers/base/server/middleware/security.ts:69, guarded only by `if (!isDev)`), and auth.ts sorts before security.ts so the key is per-user. I enumerated every other prefix (/api/auth, /api/social, /api/federation, /inbox, /users/*, and /auth/<x> which keys three segments deep): /api/files is indeed the only 2-segment prefix spanning two tiers. No guard exists: no flag gates /api/files/mine or /api/files/upload, and the tests (packages/infra/src/__tests__/security.test.ts:110, :239-257) only assert tier selection and use /auth/login, which keys per-path and so never exercises the collision. git log -L on the function shows slice(0,3) present in the original commit as deliberate cardinality-bounding, written before getTierForPath reached deeper for uploads - drift, not intent. However the claim overstates the magnitude in three ways I verified: (1) DELETE /api/files/[id] has ZERO UI call sites anywhere in the repo, so the 'delete 2 unwanted ones' step is fabricated; (2) the media library is not fetched per open - ArticleEditor.vue:111 fetches /api/files/mine in onMounted, once per editor mount; (3) /api/files/[id]/raw is behind requireFeature('contestPrivateFiles') and only reached by clicking an <a href> (ContestRegistrantsPanel.vue:97, ContestSubmissionField.vue:194), never an <img src>, because public uploads return the storage adapter's public URL (upload.post.ts:113) rather than the raw route. So the realistic non-upload contribution to the shared counter is 1, not 4. The error is also fail-closed in one direction only: an upload is always compared against 10, so the bug can only shrink the upload budget, never enlarge it. Real defect, but a quota-accounting inaccuracy against the user's own budget, not a bypass or an outage.

</details>

#### [P3] Markdown import silently stops rehosting images after 10, leaving the rest as third-party hotlinks

`layers/base/composables/useMarkdownImport.ts:47`

`importMarkdown` loops over every remote image block and POSTs each to /api/files/upload-from-url with no batching or backoff. That path matches the `upload` rate-limit tier (packages/infra/src/security.ts:261, limit 10 per 60s), so the 11th image in a single import is rejected with 429. The catch block at line 51-54 swallows it into a `console.warn` and leaves `block.content.src` pointing at the original remote URL; `progress.value.uploaded` is still incremented, so the progress indicator reports full completion. Nothing surfaces to the user.

**Failure:** An author imports a markdown post carrying 25 images from an external CMS. The first ten are rehosted; images 11 through 25 hit 429 and stay as external URLs. The published article silently hotlinks fifteen third-party images: they break when the source rotates or expires, they leak every reader's IP and User-Agent to that third party (with no cookie/consent disclosure covering it), and the author is told the import succeeded.

**Reachable input:** On a production instance (NODE_ENV=production, e.g. deveco), an authenticated author opens `/u/<their-username>/blog/<slug>/edit`, clicks the "Markdown" topbar button, and pastes markdown containing 25 remote images: ``` ![a](https://cdn.example.com/1.png) ... ![y](https://cdn.example.com/25.png) ``` then clicks Import (mode `append`). The browser issues 25 sequential `POST /api/files/upload-from-url {"url":"https://cdn.example.com/N.png","purpose":"content"}` requests with the author's session cookie. Requests 1-10 return `{url: "https://...digitaloceanspaces.com/content/<uuid>.png"}`. Request 11 onward hits the shared `user:<id>:/api/files` bucket at limit 10/60s and returns `429 Too Many Requests` from `layers/base/server/middleware/security.ts:74`; `useMarkdownImport.ts:52` swallows each into a `console.warn`, so blocks 11-25 retain `src: "https://cdn.example.com/N.png"`. Clicking Publish stores and serves those 15 third-party URLs, with no error shown anywhere in the UI. Second input, no dialog required: a docs editor opens `/docs/<siteSlug>/edit` and selects a legacy page whose `content` is still a markdown string with >10 remote images — `docs/[siteSlug]/edit.vue:175` fires the same loop on page selection.

**Suggested fix:** Surface the failures to the caller (a returned count of un-rehosted images) and pace the loop under the tier, rather than reporting a clean completion.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Survives, with one factual correction to the write-up. Chain verified by reading each file: 1. `layers/base/composables/useMarkdownImport.ts:45-57` — a strictly sequential `for` loop, one `$fetch` POST to `/api/files/upload-from-url` per remote image, no concurrency cap, no delay, no retry, no backoff, no aggregate error surface. `catch {}` at :52-55 only does `console.warn`, and the comment ("Non-fatal — remote URL stays in place") confirms the block keeps its original third-party `src`. 2. `packages/infra/src/security.ts:249-251` — `getTierForPath` matches `pathname.startsWith('/api/files/upload')`, and `/api/files/upload-from-url` matches that prefix → `DEFAULT_TIERS.upload` = `{ limit: 10, windowMs: 60_000 }` (:261). 3. `packages/infra/src/security.ts:304-313` — the bucket key is `pathname.split('/').slice(0,3).join('/')` = `/api/files`, so all these POSTs share ONE 10-per-60s bucket keyed `user:<id>:/api/files`. 4. `packages/infra/src/security.ts:194-211` — `MemoryRateLimitStore.check` is a fixed window: the 11th call inside the window returns `allowed:false`. 5. `layers/base/server/middleware/security.ts:69-79` — throws 429 on `!result.allowed`. Runs on every request in production (`isDev` skip only, no feature flag). 6. `node_modules/.../ofetch@2.0.0-alpha.3/dist/index.mjs:178` — `retries = isPayloadMethod(method) ? 0 : 1`, so a POST does not retry the 429 even though 429 is in `retryStatusCodes`. No global `$fetch` interceptor exists in the layer or apps. Hunted for the guard and found none: no feature flag on the Markdown button (`.../[slug]/edit.vue:510` renders it unconditionally in the topbar), no image-count cap in `MarkdownImportDialog.vue`, no cap in `markdownToBlockTuples`, no test anywhere referencing `useMarkdownImport` or `upload-from-url`, and git history on the file is only the two structural commits (no deliberate rate-limit-aware design). CORRECTION to the claimed evidence: "the progress indicator reports full completion" is wrong — `progress` is destructured by NO consumer. Both call sites take only `{ importing, importMarkdown }` (`.../[slug]/edit.vue:371`, `docs/[siteSlug]/edit.vue:174,325`) and the string "progress" appears in neither page nor in the dialog. There is no progress UI at all. That makes the "nothing surfaces to the user" half strictly true (console.warn is the only signal) while the specific progress-lie claim must be dropped. Second reachable path the write-up missed: `layers/base/pages/docs/[siteSlug]/edit.vue:174-175` calls `importMarkdown(page.content, 'replace')` automatically when an editor opens a legacy-markdown docs page — the same 10-image ceiling applies with no user action beyond selecting the page. Caveat on the timing, which is why I did not raise the severity: the 429 only bites if the first ten uploads complete inside the 60s window. Each upload is a remote fetch (10s timeout, 10MB cap) plus an S3 put — typically 0.5–2s, so ten finish in ~5–20s and image 11 lands well inside the window; the remaining images then fail fast (the middleware 429s before any network work) and the loop finishes long before the window resets. With unusually slow/large sources the window can roll over mid-import and only a middle subset fails. Also production-only: dev skips rate limiting entirely (`middleware/security.ts:58`), which is exactly why this would never be caught locally. Severity P3 is correct and I am leaving it there. The "leaks reader IP and User-Agent to a third party with no consent disclosure" framing is inflated: the author supplied markdown that already pointed at those third-party URLs, so remote-hotlinking is the baseline the rehost feature opportunistically improves on, not a new exposure the code introduces. The real harm is a silent partial failure with a misleading success — broken images later, and the author never told.

</details>

### Authorization

#### [P2] Any event creator can pin their own event to the public Featured tab

`packages/server/src/events/events.ts:252`

`updateEvent` authorizes with `if (!isAdmin && existing[0].createdById !== userId) return null` (line 237) and then applies every field in the body identically, including `updates.isFeatured = data.isFeatured` at line 252. The route computes `const isAdmin = hasPermission(event, 'event.manage')` and passes it in, but that flag only decides *whether the caller may edit at all* — it never gates the one field that is instance curation rather than event content. The equivalent field on content is admin-only: `PATCH /api/admin/content/[id]` sets `isFeatured` behind `requirePermission(event,'content.editorial')`.

**Failure:** Any logged-in user posts an event (POST /api/events is `requireAuth` only, deliberately per the RBAC-7 comment), then sends `PUT /api/events/<slug>` with `{"isFeatured": true}` — the edit page even ships the checkbox at pages/events/[slug]/edit.vue:138. `listEvents` with `filters.featured` (events.ts:108-110) then returns it, so the event carries the Featured badge (EventCard.vue:52) and occupies the 'Featured' tab on /events (pages/events/index.vue:25). Repeat with N events to fill the instance's curated surface; no moderator permission is required and nothing in the audit log records it.

**Reachable input:** As any ordinary authenticated (non-admin, non-staff) member on deveco.io or commonpub.io, where /api/features reports "events":true: 1) POST /api/events with session cookie, body {"title":"Squat","startDate":"2026-09-01T10:00:00.000Z","endDate":"2026-09-01T12:00:00.000Z"} — succeeds (requireFeature + requireAuth only), returns slug "squat" and status "published". 2) PUT /api/events/squat with the same cookie, body {"isFeatured": true} — passes the owner branch of `!isAdmin && createdById !== userId` and line 252 writes is_featured = true. (The same write is reachable from the UI: the owner-accessible /events/squat/edit page ships the "Featured Event" checkbox.) 3) GET /api/events?featured=true (unauthenticated) and GET /api/public/v1/events?featured=true now return the event, and the public /events?filter=featured tab shows it with the Featured badge. Repeat step 1-2 per event to fill the curated tab; no moderator permission and no audit-log entry involved.

**Suggested fix:** In `updateEvent`, apply `isFeatured` only when `isAdmin` is true, and drop the checkbox from the non-privileged edit form (or render it only when the viewer holds `event.manage`).

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code, not the claim. `updateEvent` (packages/server/src/events/events.ts:237) authorizes owner-OR-admin, and line 252 then applies `isFeatured` unconditionally with no separate curation gate; `isAdmin` from the route only decides whether the caller may edit at all. The PUT route's zod schema explicitly accepts `isFeatured` ([slug].put.ts:18), so nothing upstream strips it, and event creation is intentionally open to every authenticated user (index.post.ts RBAC-7 comment). I hunted for a guard and found none: no test in the repo references `updateEvent` at all, no DB constraint on the column, and the feature is not flag-gated off — live `GET /api/features` returns `"events":true` on both deveco.io and commonpub.io. The result is publicly consumable: `listEvents` filters `eq(events.isFeatured, true)` (events.ts:109-110) for the auth:false "Featured" tab (pages/events/index.vue:85) and for `/api/public/v1/events?featured=true`. Git history supports that this is a defect rather than intent — the feature landed as "events system with RSVP + admin curation", and the sibling concept for content is gated by `requirePermission(event,'content.editorial')` in admin/content/[id].patch.ts. Severity trimmed reasoning: it is member-triggerable with no privilege gain, no data exposure, fully attributable and admin-reversible, so P2 is the ceiling, not P1.

</details>

#### [P2] A `users.manage` holder can self-promote to `staff`, bypassing the RBAC-5 privilege ceiling

`layers/base/server/api/admin/users/[id]/role.put.ts:15`

The route gates on `users.manage` and then adds an extra `admin.access` check for only ONE target value: `if (input.role === 'admin')`. Every other value of `adminUpdateRoleSchema` (`member|pro|verified|staff`) is unguarded, and there is no self-target guard and no privilege ceiling — unlike its sibling `roles.put.ts`, which has both (`if (id === actor.id && actor.role !== 'admin') 403` plus `enforceGrantCeiling`). `updateUserRole` (packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts:363-374) then inserts the matching `user_roles` row, so the resolver unions that system role's grants on the next request.

**Failure:** With `features.rbac` on, an admin creates a custom role holding only `users.manage` (legal: `ADMIN_BYPASS_GRANTS` in packages/server/src/rbac/admin.ts strips only `*`/`admin.access`/`admin.*`) and assigns it to Mallory for user-support work. Mallory sends `PUT /api/admin/users/<her own id>/role` with `{"role":"staff"}`. The route passes (`users.manage` held, target role is not `admin`), `updateUserRole` writes `users.role='staff'` and inserts the `staff` `user_roles` row. Within 30s (cache TTL) Mallory holds the entire `STAFF_PERMISSION_SET` (packages/server/src/rbac/seed.ts:20-32): `contest.pii`, `contest.manage`, `content.moderate`, `content.editorial`, `reports.review`, `audit.read`. On deveco that is read access to every live contest entrant's PII via `GET /api/contests/:slug/entries/:id/private` and the PII columns of `GET /api/contests/:slug/export`. The same call with `{"role":"member"}` also lets her demote any admin down to the last one (the only guard is `LAST_ADMIN`).

**Reachable input:** Preconditions verified live: `features.admin` = true and `features.rbac` = true on deveco.io and commonpub.io (`curl https://deveco.io/api/features` -> {"rbac":true,"admin":true}). Operator precondition: an admin has created a custom role and assigned it to Mallory (a `member`) — `POST /api/admin/roles {"key":"support","name":"Support","permissions":["users.manage"]}` then `PUT /api/admin/users/<mallory-id>/roles {"roleIds":["<support-role-id>"]}`. Both succeed for an admin (admin floor clears the ceiling; `users.manage` is not in ADMIN_BYPASS_GRANTS so it survives sanitizeGrants). Attack request, issued by Mallory herself, authenticated with her own Better Auth session cookie from the instance's own origin (satisfies middleware/csrf.ts): PUT https://deveco.io/api/admin/users/<mallory-own-id>/role Content-Type: application/json {"role":"staff"}. requireFeature('admin') passes; requirePermission('users.manage') passes via her custom role; `input.role !== 'admin'` so the admin.access branch is skipped; updateUserRole sets users.role='staff', deletes her `member` user_roles row, inserts the `staff` row (her `support` row is untouched); invalidatePermissions fires. Her very next request resolves permissions = support grants UNION STAFF_PERMISSION_SET. She then reads any live entrant's PII with GET https://deveco.io/api/contests/<slug>/entries/<entryId>/private (passes because staff holds contest.pii and contest.manage, satisfying both conjuncts at private.get.ts:40) and the PII columns of GET /api/contests/<slug>/export. The same route with {"role":"member"} against any admin's id demotes them, bounded only by the LAST_ADMIN check.

**Suggested fix:** Mirror roles.put.ts: refuse `id === actor.id` for non-admins, and ceiling-check the target system role's seeded grant set with `enforceGrantCeiling` before calling `updateUserRole`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Survives. I read the whole route: role.put.ts gates on `users.manage` and adds `admin.access` for exactly one target value (`admin`); `staff` is unguarded, there is no self-target guard, and `enforceGrantCeiling` is never called — in direct contrast to the sibling roles.put.ts, which has both. `updateUserRole` (packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts:330-386) writes `users.role` and inserts the matching `user_roles` row, leaving the actor's custom role row intact, so the resolver unions both; the route then calls `invalidatePermissions(id)` on the target, so a SELF-promotion takes effect on the very next request rather than after the 30s TTL. STAFF_PERMISSION_SET (rbac/seed.ts:20-32) contains both `contest.pii` and `contest.manage`, and the PII route (contests/[slug]/entries/[entryId]/private.get.ts:40) requires `contest.pii AND (canManage || isJudge)` where canManage includes `contest.manage` — so staff satisfies BOTH halves and the entrant-PII consequence is real, not assumed. Refutation hunts all failed: there is no global /api/admin middleware gate (only features.ts, which maps the /admin PAGE prefix); a custom non-admin role holding `users.manage` is explicitly legal (ADMIN_BYPASS_GRANTS strips only */admin.access/admin.*, and admin.integration.test.ts:199-203 proves an admin can mint exactly that role); the RBAC-5 remediation (docs/plans/session-231-remediation-roadmap.md:110-122) scoped the ceiling to roles.manage/createRole/updateRole/setUserCustomRoles and the roles.put route, never the system-role path; no test pins the behaviour (admin-route-keys.test.ts:135 only asserts the key string). The flag-off refutation used against a sibling finding in docs/reviews/platform-audit-2026-08-21.md:498 is STALE — I curled /api/features and got rbac:true, admin:true on BOTH deveco.io and commonpub.io. The one honest counterweight: the inline comment shows the author deliberately drew the line at `admin` ("must not be a backdoor to creating admins"), so this is a considered design gap, not an oversight — but it still contradicts the codebase's own RBAC-5 invariant that a non-admin may not confer a grant they do not hold, which is enforced on every sibling path. Severity held at P2 rather than raised: the exploit requires an operator to have already delegated a `users.manage` custom role to a non-admin, which is supported config but not attacker-controlled, and I found no evidence such a role exists on a live instance today.

</details>

#### [P2] POST /api/docs/{siteSlug}/versions passes the slug where a uuid site id is expected — every call 500s

`layers/base/server/api/docs/[siteSlug]/versions.post.ts:10`

The handler calls `createDocsVersion(db, siteSlug, user.id, input)`, but the function's second parameter is `siteId` (packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:169-181) and is bound directly into `eq(docsSites.id, siteId)`. `docsSites.id` is `uuid` (packages/schema/src/docs.ts:8) while `siteSlug` is a `varchar(128)` slug, so the bind throws `invalid input syntax for type uuid`. Both parameters are typed `string`, so nothing catches it at compile time. Every sibling docs route resolves the site first (`getDocsSiteBySlug`) and passes `result.site.id`; this one does not.
