**Failure:** An owner opens the docs editor, types a version name in Settings and clicks Create Version. `useDocsSiteSettings.ts:80` posts to `/api/docs/${siteSlug}/versions`. Postgres rejects the uuid cast, Nitro returns 500, and the composable's catch shows 'Failed to create version'. No docs site on any instance can ever gain a second version through the UI.

**Reachable input:** As any authenticated user who owns a docs site with slug `handbook` (created via POST /api/docs {"name":"Handbook"}): POST /api/docs/handbook/versions with body {"version":"v2","isDefault":false} and the session cookie. The body passes createDocsVersionSchema; the handler calls createDocsVersion(db, 'handbook', userId, input); Postgres rejects the uuid bind with 22P02 and the route returns 500. Via the UI: open /docs/handbook/edit, open Settings, type "v2" in the new-version field, click Create Version, get the toast "Failed to create version".

**Suggested fix:** Resolve the site first (`const result = await getDocsSiteBySlug(db, siteSlug)`; 404 if null) and pass `result.site.id`. Separately, `createDocsVersion` copies pages from `input.sourceVersionId` (docs.ts:193-198) without checking that version belongs to the same site — fix that at the same time, or the working route becomes a cross-site page-copy IDOR.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the full handler, the callee, the schema, and by empirically reproducing the failure. layers/base/server/api/docs/[siteSlug]/versions.post.ts:10 passes the route slug into createDocsVersion's `siteId` parameter (packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:169-181), which binds it directly into `eq(docsSites.id, siteId)` as the function's FIRST query, before any authorization branch. docsSites.id is `uuid` (packages/schema/src/docs.ts:8); the slug is varchar(128) generated by generateSlug(name) in createDocsSite (docs.ts:100), so it is never a uuid. I ran the exact driver the app uses (pg 8.20.0 via drizzle-orm/node-postgres, per layers/base/server/utils/db.ts) against the project's Docker Postgres docs_sites table: `select 1 from docs_sites where id = $1 and owner_id = $2` with ['my-docs', <uuid>] returns `22P02 invalid input syntax for type uuid: "my-docs"`. The handler has no try/catch, so Nitro returns 500. Guard hunt found nothing: the API route has no requireFeature call (layers/base/server/middleware/features.ts gates PAGE routes only and says so in a comment, and features.docs defaults to true in packages/config/src/schema.ts:29); no middleware resolves the slug; both parameters are typed `string` so TypeScript is blind; the only test (layers/base/composables/__tests__/useDocsSiteSettings.test.ts) stubs $fetch and never reaches the handler; git history shows the file unchanged since the original layer commit 00790b98, so it is a copy-paste omission, not deliberate. The two siblings in the same directory (index.put.ts, index.delete.ts) both correctly do getDocsSiteBySlug then pass result.site.id. Severity stays P2: it breaks an owner-facing action (createDocsSite seeds a 'v1' version, so sites work but can never gain a second version through the UI) with no security or data impact; a non-owner also gets 500 instead of 403, which is noise, not privilege escalation. Adjacent, not part of this finding: createDocsVersionSchema declares `copyFromVersionId` while the function reads `input.sourceVersionId`, so version page-copying is silently dead too.

</details>

#### [P2] User-submitted videos can never be removed: no update or delete route exists anywhere

`layers/base/server/api/videos/index.post.ts:10`

`POST /api/videos` is `requireAuth` only and inserts a row into the public `videos` listing with a caller-supplied title, description, url, embedUrl and thumbnailUrl. The entire videos API is `[id].get.ts`, `index.get.ts`, `index.post.ts` plus the three category routes; there is no `[id].put.ts`, no `[id].delete.ts`, and no admin video route. `packages/server/src/video/video.ts` exports `createVideo`, `updateVideoCategory` and `deleteVideoCategory` — there is no `deleteVideo` or `updateVideo` function anywhere in the server package, so the `content.moderate` permission has no reachable sink for this content type.

**Failure:** A member posts a video whose title/description is spam or abuse, or whose `embedUrl` points at an arbitrary third-party https page that /videos renders. The author cannot delete it, a `content.moderate` holder cannot delete it, and a platform admin cannot delete it — no HTTP route reaches the row. The only remediation is direct SQL against production. Account deletion does remove it (FK cascade), which means the only in-product takedown is deleting the author's whole account.

**Reachable input:** Any authenticated member on commonpub.io (features.video === true, verified via GET /api/features): POST /api/videos with session cookie and body {"title":"spam or abuse text","description":"...","url":"https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x","embedUrl":"https://attacker.example/page","categoryId":null}. The row is inserted and immediately public at GET /api/videos, GET /api/public/v1/videos, /videos and /videos/{id} (embedUrl rendered into an iframe src). No subsequent HTTP request by anyone removes or edits it: there is no videos/[id].put.ts or [id].delete.ts, no deleteVideo/updateVideo in @commonpub/server, admin/content/[id].delete.ts targets content_items not videos, the videos table has no status column to soft-hide, and report_target_type has no 'video' member so it cannot even be reported. Demonstrated live: GET https://commonpub.io/api/videos currently returns an unremovable member-submitted row id f60fce27-580f-4c9d-9fbc-bc339ce62d00 titled "ssdads".

**Suggested fix:** Add `DELETE /api/videos/[id]` gated by `ownerOrPermission(event, video.authorId, 'content.moderate')` backed by a `deleteVideo(db, id, userId?)` that is owner-scoped when no moderator permission is present — the same shape products/[id].delete.ts already uses.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading every claimed file plus the live instance. `layers/base/server/api/videos/index.post.ts` is requireAuth-only and inserts into the `videos` table. The ONLY writes to that table in the entire repo are `.insert(videos)` (video.ts:179) and `.update(videos)` for view counts (video.ts:282) — there is no `db.delete(videos)` and no content-field update anywhere outside tests. The admin sink does not reach it: `admin/content/[id].delete.ts` calls `removeContent`, which at packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts:709 selects from and archives `contentItems`, a different table; `videos` is its own pgTable in packages/schema/src/video.ts and has NO status column, so there is not even a soft-hide. Worse than claimed: `reportTargetTypeEnum` (packages/schema/src/enums.ts:45) is ['project','article','blog','post','comment','user','explainer'] with no 'video', so a video cannot even enter the report queue, despite [id].vue rendering `<EngagementBar target-type="video">`. `layers/base/pages/videos/[id].vue` (248 lines) has zero owner/delete controls. Hunted for guards and found none: the POST has no requireFeature (server/middleware/features.ts explicitly skips /api), no DB constraint, no pinning test, no TODO or deliberateness marker; the adjacent commit 9330e87c added owner-or-moderator delete for products and left videos alone, which reads as oversight not intent. Also verified `createVideoSchema` (packages/schema/src/validators/video.ts:6) takes caller-supplied embedUrl with shape-only URL validation, and [id].vue:49 pipes it into `<iframe :src>`. Live: commonpub.io reports "video":true, /videos returns 200, and /api/videos already serves a real member-submitted junk row titled "ssdads" with 84 views that no route can remove. Two corrections to the finding: (1) the dimension label is wrong — this is a missing-capability/moderation gap, not an authz defect; nothing is authz-broken, there is no privilege escalation and no unauthorized read; (2) deveco has "video":false so its page routes 404, meaning commonpub.io is the exposed instance. P2 stands: a live public UGC surface whose only remediation is production SQL or deleting the author's whole account.

</details>

#### [P2] Contest `editor` stakeholders are missing from the entries-list privileged set

`layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/entries.get.ts:31`

`privileged` is computed as owner OR `contest.manage` OR accepted judge. `isContestEditor` is absent — even though every other organizer route folds it into `canManage`: index.put.ts, advance.post.ts, transition.post.ts, export.get.ts, registrants.get.ts, registrants-export.get.ts, entries/[entryId]/private.get.ts, and files/[id]/raw.get.ts all include it. Because `privileged` also drives `onlyPublishedContent: !privileged` (line 53), an editor is treated as a member of the public: no judge scores, no stage artifacts, and no draft/proposal entries at all.

**Failure:** A contest owner grants a co-organizer the `editor` stakeholder role (packages/server/src/contest/stakeholders.ts:157-168 — 'full edit rights to THIS contest only'). That editor opens the contest's entries view. `GET /api/contests/:slug/entries` returns `privileged=false`, so `includeJudgeScores` is forced false, `includeStageSubmissions` is forced false, and `onlyPublishedContent:true` filters out every proposal-mode placeholder entry (whose content is `status:'draft'`). On a proposal-mode contest the editor sees an empty or near-empty entry list — yet `advance.post.ts` lets that same editor perform the stage cut that this list is the evidence for. The editor can also read every entrant's PII via private.get.ts but cannot see the entry the PII belongs to.

**Reachable input:** Contest "summer-build", default judgingVisibility 'judges-only', status 'judging', with a review stage. (1) Owner Olivia: POST /api/contests/summer-build/stakeholders {"userId":"<alice-uuid>","role":"editor"} (or picks "Editor" in the Collaborators tab) -> 200. (2) Entrant Bob submits his published project (POST /api/contests/summer-build/entries {"contentId":"<bob-project-uuid>"}) and afterwards edits that project to visibility "members" (or back to draft); nothing re-checks the entry. (3) Alice (authenticated, NOT owner, NOT admin, NOT a judge, holds no contest.manage): GET /api/contests/summer-build/entries -> privileged=false, so the response omits Bob's entry entirely and returns score:null on every remaining entry (and null scores inside stageState). (4) Alice opens /contests/summer-build/edit -> Stages tab -> Advancement panel (rendered because index.get returned viewerCanManage=true), selects the entries she can see, and POSTs /api/contests/summer-build/advance {"reviewStageId":"<review-stage-id>","mode":"manual","advancedEntryIds":["<visible-entry-ids>"]} -> 200 (authorized via isContestEditor). advanceContestStage marks Bob's never-displayed entry 'eliminated' and sends him a "Contest update" notification.

**Suggested fix:** Add `|| (await isContestEditor(db, contest.id, user.id))` to the `privileged` expression, matching the `canManage` shape used by the other seven contest routes.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified in source. entries.get.ts:31-34 computes `privileged` from owner OR hasPermission('contest.manage') OR isContestJudge only; `isContestEditor` is absent, while index.get/index.put/advance/transition/export/registrants/registrants-export/email-*/private.get/files raw.get all include it (grep confirmed). Consequences are real and gated on that single boolean: (a) `onlyPublishedContent: !privileged` -> listContestEntries (packages/server/src/contest/entries.ts:53-66) filters to status='published' AND visibility='public', and entries.post.ts:57 only checks status at submit time and nothing revalidates later, so an entry whose project is members-visibility or later unpublished vanishes from the editor's list; (b) revealScores=shouldRevealScores(judgingVisibility,status,privileged) with judgingVisibility defaulting to 'judges-only' (packages/schema/src/contest.ts:467) nulls `score` and every stageState score for the editor until the contest is 'completed'. The editor reaches this surface by design: index.get.ts:20-24 returns viewerCanManage=true for an editor, ContestEditor.vue:56 renders the full editor on that flag, and ContestAdvancementPanel.vue:24 self-fetches /api/contests/:slug/entries to build the Top-N / manual cut list. Worse than claimed: advanceContestStage (packages/server/src/contest/judging.ts:250-278) in manual mode marks every eligible entry NOT in advancedEntryIds as 'eliminated' and notifies the entrant, so the editor silently eliminates entries her list never showed — on a call advance.post.ts:19 explicitly authorizes her for via isContestEditor. Hunted for guards and found none that closes it: the only pinning test (layers/base/server/api/contests/__tests__/entries-score-gating.test.ts) is a leak guard asserting owner/contest.manage/judge are present and does not forbid adding the editor; the role is a real, UI-exposed value (STAKEHOLDER_ROLES=['reviewer','editor'], ContestStakeholderManager.vue:96,116 role <option>); no flag gates the editor role beyond features.contests itself. git show 3edc7689 ("per-contest editors": "routes compute owner || editor || contest.manage") shows entries.get was simply missed in that sweep, not deliberately excluded. Note the report's proposal-mode framing is the flag-dependent variant (contestProposals defaults false, packages/config/src/schema.ts:38); the members-visibility/unpublished variant and the score blackout need no non-default flag.

</details>

#### [P3] createEvent accepts an arbitrary hubId with no membership check

`packages/server/src/events/events.ts:215`

`POST /api/events` accepts `hubId: z.string().uuid().optional()` and `createEvent` writes it straight to the row (`hubId: input.hubId ?? null`) with no check that the hub exists or that the caller is a member of it. Every sibling hub-scoped create does enforce membership in the data layer: `createProduct` (packages/server/src/product/product.ts:82-90), `createPost`, and `shareContent`. The read side is gated (`events/index.get.ts:36-41` calls `canReadHubById` for `?hubId=`), so this is a write-side-only gap.

**Failure:** Any authenticated user posts `{title, startDate, endDate, hubId: "<any hub uuid>"}`. Two outcomes: (a) with a private hub's id, `listEvents`'s private-hub exclusion at events.ts:134-138 only runs when `!filters.hubId`, so the event both disappears from the public feed and surfaces to that private hub's members on `GET /api/events?hubId=<hub>` — a stranger writing into a private hub's namespace; (b) with a public hub's id, the event is permanently attributed to a hub that never agreed to host it, and neither the hub owner nor a hub admin has any route to detach it (only the event's creator or an `event.manage` holder can edit or delete it).

**Reachable input:** As any authenticated non-member on commonpub.io or deveco.io (both have `"events":true`), first read a hub id from the public unauthenticated `GET /api/hubs?limit=2` — I ran this and it returned `"id":"972b8e42-0f0f-4378-b2eb-fd22b5df0cb8"` ("Simms Intelligent Solutions"). Then, with a normal session cookie and no membership in that hub: `POST /api/events` with body `{"title":"Injected","startDate":"2026-09-01T00:00:00.000Z","endDate":"2026-09-02T00:00:00.000Z","hubId":"972b8e42-0f0f-4378-b2eb-fd22b5df0cb8"}`. Zod accepts it (line 18), the FK is satisfied because the hub exists, and the row lands with `hub_id` set to a hub the caller has no relationship with. The event is then returned under `GET /api/events?hubId=972b8e42-...`, and no hub owner or hub admin route can detach or delete it (`UpdateEventInput` has no hubId; `[slug].put.ts`/`[slug].delete.ts` allow only the creator or a platform `event.manage` holder).

**Suggested fix:** In `createEvent`, when `input.hubId` is set, require an active `hubMembers` row for `input.createdBy` in that hub (and reject an unknown hub id), matching `createProduct`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The gap is real and unguarded. `createEvent` (packages/server/src/events/events.ts:190-221) performs no membership lookup and writes `hubId: input.hubId ?? null` directly; `layers/base/server/api/events/index.post.ts` gates only on `requireFeature('events')` + `requireAuth`, declares `hubId: z.string().uuid().optional()`, and spreads `...body` into `createEvent`. The sibling `createProduct` (product.ts:82-90) does enforce active hub membership, so this is an inconsistency, not a stated policy — the route's long RBAC-7 comment concerns `event.create` permission gating only and never mentions hub scoping. I hunted for guards and found only a partial one: the `hubId` FK to `hubs.id` (packages/schema/src/events.ts:24) rejects a nonexistent hub, so the 'hub exists' half of the claim is guarded; membership is not. The `events` flag is ON in production (verified by curl: `"events":true` on both commonpub.io and deveco.io), `GET /api/hubs` publicly returns raw hub uuids, and no test pins the behaviour. The unauthorised attribution is also stickier than claimed: `UpdateEventInput` has no `hubId` field, so not even the event's creator can detach it — only deletion by the creator or a platform `event.manage` holder. I did trim the finding: outcome (a) (private hub) requires a private hub uuid that no public endpoint discloses, so I do not count it as reachable; outcome (b) (public hub) is fully reachable and is what I verified.

</details>

#### [P3] Turning `features.rbac` off does not clear the permission cache — the kill switch has no caller

`layers/base/server/api/admin/features/index.put.ts:26`

`invalidateAllPermissions()` is documented in layers/base/server/utils/permissions.ts as 'the RBAC kill-switch companion to flipping the flag off'. The only callers are the three /admin/roles write routes; the flag-writing route never calls it. `resolvePermissions` reads `useConfig().features.rbac` only on a cache MISS, so an entry resolved while the flag was on keeps its full grant set until its own 30s TTL expires.

**Failure:** An operator discovers a mis-granted custom role and hits the documented kill switch: PUT /api/admin/features with `{overrides:{rbac:false}}`. The write commits, but every process-local cache entry resolved in the preceding 30s still carries its grants, so the mis-granted user keeps `content.moderate` / `contest.pii` for up to 30s per pod after the operator believes the whole subsystem is off. The same route also fails to invalidate when an operator toggles any other flag that a cached decision depends on.

**Reachable input:** On deveco.io or commonpub.io (rbac override ON per docs/STATUS.md:1207). User U is a non-admin holding a role granting `content.moderate`. T=0s: U makes any authenticated request, so middleware/auth.ts:128 stores U's full grant set in the process-local cache. T=5s: an admin with `settings.manage` clicks the kill switch on /admin/roles, which issues `PUT /api/admin/features` with body `{"overrides":{"rbac":false}}` and their session cookie; it returns 200 and the override commits. T=6s: U calls `DELETE /api/admin/content/{id}` (guarded by `requirePermission(event, 'content.moderate')` at layers/base/server/api/admin/content/[id].delete.ts:5) against the same Nitro process. It still succeeds, and keeps succeeding until T=30s, because resolvePermissions returns the pre-flip cached Set without consulting the now-false flag.

**Suggested fix:** Call `invalidateAllPermissions()` after the settings commit in admin/features/index.put.ts whenever the `rbac` key appears in `body.overrides` (or unconditionally — the cache is cheap to rebuild).

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the full chain. layers/base/server/api/admin/features/index.put.ts persists `features.overrides` and calls only `invalidateConfigCache()`; `invalidateAllPermissions()` appears nowhere in it (grep across the repo shows callers only in admin/roles/index.post.ts:36, admin/roles/[id]/index.put.ts:32, admin/roles/[id]/index.delete.ts:23). permissions.ts:78-86 returns `readFresh()` before ever reading `useConfig().features.rbac`, so a live entry keeps its rbac-on grant set for its own 30s TTL. No gate-time guard rescues it: requirePermission.ts:44-46 reads `event.context.cpubPermissions` and deliberately does not re-check the flag ("NOT wrapped in requireFeature('rbac') — the flag lives only in the resolver"), and middleware/auth.ts:128 attaches the cached set every request. The flag is not dead code: docs/STATUS.md:1207-1215 records rbac ON on commonpub.io and deveco.io. The documented kill switch routes through exactly this endpoint: docs/plans/rbac.md:110 specifies "features.rbac=false + invalidateAllPermissions()", and layers/base/pages/admin/roles.vue:35 implements the operator toggle as PUT /api/admin/features {overrides:{rbac:false}}. Not deliberate: git log shows the route predates the RBAC work (beeedbfc "feat(admin): runtime feature flags"), and no test pins invalidation. Notably invalidateConfigCache() nulls `mergedConfig` so the very next useConfig() falls back to base config (rbac default false) — proving the flip is instant for cache MISSES and isolating the defect to live cache hits only. Severity stays P3: admin-only trigger, hard 30s bound, no new privilege granted (only delayed revocation of grants already in force), and the same file already documents 30s staleness as accepted for v1 in the sibling multi-pod case.

</details>

### Security review of packages/protocol/

#### [P1] One signed inbox POST fans out into thousands of full-size message rows — unbounded `to` list, no length cap, replayable

`packages/server/src/federation/inboxHandlers.ts:521`

`onCreate`'s private-Note branch iterates `object.to` verbatim (`for (const recipientUri of toField)`) and calls `sendMessage(db, conv.id, localUser.id, ...)` once per entry with the sanitized `object.content` as the body. There is no cap on the number of `to` entries, no de-duplication of repeated entries, and no length cap on the body — `messages.body` is `text` (packages/schema/src/social.ts:182) and the local route's `sendMessageSchema` cap of 10,000 chars (packages/schema/src/validators/messaging.ts:11) is bypassed entirely because this path calls `sendMessage` directly. The only bound on the request is `MAX_BODY_SIZE = 1 MB` in verifyInboxRequest, which bounds the INPUT, not the amplified output.

**Failure:** Anyone who can stand up an ActivityPub actor on a domain they control (a static actor doc + a signing key — no relationship with the target instance is required) POSTs one ≤1 MB body to https://deveco.io/inbox: {"type":"Create","actor":"https://evil.example/actor","object":{"type":"Note","to":["https://deveco.io/users/alice", ...16000 identical copies...],"content":"<500 KB of high-entropy text>"}} Verification passes (the actor and the object.id host both belong to the attacker). `isPrivateNote` is true (Note, non-empty `to`, no #Public). The loop then runs 16,000 times, each iteration resolving alice, hitting the `findOrCreateConversation` fast path, and INSERTing a ~500 KB `messages` row — roughly 8 GB of writes from a single 1 MB request. Two further multipliers: (a) the activity carries no `id`, so the route-level `recordActivitySeen` dedup at layers/base/server/routes/inbox.ts:27 is skipped entirely and the byte-identical signed request can be replayed for the whole 5-minute Date-skew window; (b) the federation rate-limit tier allows 60 such requests per minute per IP (packages/infra/src/security.ts:251). Even at a conservative 1,000 recipients × 100 KB the instance takes ~6 GB/min of unrecoverable disk growth, which fills a normal droplet volume in minutes and takes Postgres — and therefore the live contest on deveco.io — down. No admin surface prunes these rows.

**Reachable input:** Attacker hosts an ordinary ActivityPub actor at https://evil.example/actor (a JSON-LD Person doc with `publicKey.publicKeyPem`, on a public IP so `createSafeActorFetchFn`'s SSRF pin allows it). No follow, no relationship, no account on the target. They then send ONE signed request: POST https://deveco.io/inbox Date: <now, within 5 min> Digest: SHA-256=<digest of body> Signature: keyId="https://evil.example/actor#main-key",algorithm="rsa-sha256",headers="(request-target) host date digest",signature="<valid>" Content-Type: application/activity+json Body (~1,000,000 bytes, deliberately WITHOUT a top-level "id"): {"type":"Create","actor":"https://evil.example/actor","object":{"type":"Note","to":["https://deveco.io/u/<any-existing-username>", ... ~15,000 identical copies ...],"content":"<~500 KB of text>"}} Path taken, all verified by reading: verifyInboxRequest passes (signature valid, keyId host == actor.id host == evil.example, Date fresh, body <= MAX_BODY_SIZE); assertActorMatchesSigner passes (activity.actor host == signer host == evil.example); the id-less body skips recordActivitySeen at layers/base/server/routes/inbox.ts:26; processInboxActivity's Create case requires only an object; onCreate computes isPrivateNote = true (type Note, to non-empty, no #Public) and enters the loop at inboxHandlers.ts:521 BEFORE the object.id/actor host attribution guard. Each of the ~15,000 iterations parses the URL, takes the last path segment as the username (so /u/<name>, /users/<name>, or even /<name> all work), finds the local user, hits the findOrCreateConversation fast path, and INSERTs a ~500 KB row into `messages` (text column, no cap; sendMessage at messaging.ts:186 does no length validation). One 1 MB request therefore produces roughly 7.5 GB of writes plus ~90,000 DB round trips against a single victim account. Because no activity id was claimed, the byte-identical request replays for the whole 5-minute Date window, at up to 60/min/IP (federation rate-limit tier, packages/infra/src/security.ts:251). deveco.io is live with features.federation true (verified via GET /api/features) and POST /inbox answers 401, not 404.

**Suggested fix:** Cap the recipient fan-out hard (dedupe `toField` and reject beyond ~20 local recipients), enforce the same 10,000-char body limit the local route uses before calling `sendMessage` (best: move the cap into `sendMessage` itself so no caller can bypass it), and require a top-level activity `id` before applying any write side-effect on the inbox path so an id-less activity cannot be replayed.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Survives. I read the whole onCreate unit, both inbox routes, processInboxActivity, verifyInboxRequest, sendMessage/findOrCreateConversation/createConversation, sanitizeHtml and the messages table, and hunted for every plausible guard: there is no cap or de-duplication on `object.to`, no length validation anywhere on this write path (the 10,000-char sendMessageSchema cap is used only by the local REST route), no allowlist/blocklist/follow requirement on the inbox, and the attribution-binding guard that would reject a foreign object.id sits after the private-Note branch's early return so it never runs. The single-participant conversation is created successfully (createConversation only checks the ids exist) and sendMessage's participancy check passes because the local user is both sender and sole participant, so the try/catch never swallows anything. The dedup skip is real: processInboxActivity does not require an activity id for Create, and both inbox routes claim an id only when one is present. The gate is federation, which is empirically ON on deveco.io, and /inbox answers 401 (route live) rather than 404. Git history shows the loop has never had a bound. Two corrections to the finding: (1) its example payload is arithmetically impossible (16,000 recipient URIs plus 500 KB of content exceeds the 1 MB MAX_BODY_SIZE), though the achievable optimum inside 1 MB is still ~15,000 recipients x ~500 KB ~= 7.5 GB of amplification, so the defect class is unaffected; (2) severity is P1, not P0 - it is unauthenticated resource exhaustion / storage amplification requiring the attacker to stand up and sign from their own AP actor, it leaks nothing, escalates nothing, and each request is bounded by the 1 MB body and the 60 req/min/IP federation tier, with recovery available by deleting the rows and blocking the actor.

</details>

#### [P2] Remote `cpub:blocks` are stored with zero sanitization and rendered through the permissive admin-grade sanitizer

`packages/server/src/federation/inboxHandlers.ts:770`

Every other remote HTML field on the ingest path goes through `sanitizeHtml` (object.content at :763, summary at :817, hub summary at :1032). `cpub:blocks` does not: `const cpubBlocks = Array.isArray(object['cpub:blocks']) ? object['cpub:blocks'] : null` stores the remote JSON verbatim into `federated_content.cpub_blocks`. `useMirrorContent` then prefers those blocks over the sanitized `content` string, and `BlockContentRenderer` maps the block type `html` to `BlockHtmlView`, whose only barrier is `sanitizeRichHtml` — the sanitizer whose own header comment states "Authoring is gated to trusted (staff/admin) contest creators and is opt-in per contest." Remote unauthenticated input therefore receives the trusted-author allowlist: `style` attributes, `id`, `div/section/button/svg`, `<a target>`.

**Failure:** A hostile instance delivers a signed Create with `"cpub:type":"blog"` and `"cpub:blocks":[["html",{"html":"<a href='https://evil.example/deveco-login' style='position:fixed;top:0;left:0;width:100vw;height:100vh;z-index:2147483647;background:#fff;display:block'><div style='margin-top:30vh;text-align:center;font-size:28px'>Session expired — sign in to devEco</div></a>"}]]`. Nothing sanitizes it on ingest. Any member who opens the mirror page for that item (`/mirror/{id}`, linked from the federated timeline, unauthenticated-readable via /api/federation/content/{id}) gets a full-viewport white overlay served from deveco.io's own origin, in the address bar of the instance they trust, whose entire surface is one link to the attacker's credential-harvesting page. `sanitizeStyleAttr` strips only `url()/expression()/javascript:/@import/behavior`, so every layout and positioning declaration survives. The same blocks are copied verbatim into a LOCAL `content_items` row by the fork path (`content: fc.cpubBlocks ?? fc.content`, packages/server/src/content/content.ts:1286), so the overlay can be made to persist on a normal /u/{user}/{type}/{slug} page.

**Reachable input:** From any host the attacker controls that serves a resolvable AP actor document with a public key (no local account, no follow relationship, no allowlisting): `POST https://deveco.io/inbox` with a valid HTTP Signature whose keyId is `https://evil.example/users/bob#main-key`, body `{"@context":"https://www.w3.org/ns/activitystreams","id":"https://evil.example/act/1","type":"Create","actor":"https://evil.example/users/bob","object":{"id":"https://evil.example/u/bob/blog/x","type":"Article","attributedTo":"https://evil.example/users/bob","to":["https://www.w3.org/ns/activitystreams#Public"],"name":"Hello","content":"<p>hi</p>","cpub:type":"blog","cpub:blocks":[["html",{"html":"<a href='https://evil.example/deveco-login' style='position:fixed;top:0;left:0;width:100vw;height:100vh;z-index:2147483647;background:var(--cpub-bg);display:block'><div style='margin-top:30vh;text-align:center;font-size:28px'>Session expired &mdash; sign in to devEco</div></a>"}]]}}`. object.id host == actor host, so the attribution-binding check at inboxHandlers.ts:604-630 passes; the row is inserted with cpubBlocks verbatim. Then any visitor (unauthenticated) does `GET https://deveco.io/api/federation/timeline` (or opens the federated timeline page) to obtain the row's UUID and `GET https://deveco.io/mirror/{uuid}` — the fixed-position full-viewport anchor renders on deveco.io's own origin. Escalation to a permanent local URL: an authenticated member `POST https://deveco.io/api/federation/content/{uuid}/fork`, which copies `fc.cpubBlocks` straight into content_items.content (packages/server/src/content/content.ts:1286) and renders at /u/{user}/blog/{slug}.

**Suggested fix:** Validate `cpub:blocks` against a schema on ingest (known block types only, `data` must be an object) and run every `html`/`text` field inside it through the same `sanitizeHtml` used for `object.content`. Independently, make `BlockHtmlView` use `sanitizeBlockHtml` (not `sanitizeRichHtml`) whenever the block did not come from a trusted local author — the rich sanitizer should never see federated input.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The chain is real and I reproduced the decisive step. (1) inboxHandlers.ts:770 stores `object['cpub:blocks']` after only `Array.isArray`, while the adjacent `content`/`summary` on the same code path go through `sanitizeHtml`; grep across packages/ and layers/ finds no other validator, Zod schema, or block-type allowlist for cpubBlocks (ingest, `getFederatedContent` at timeline.ts:147, and useMirrorContent are all pass-through). (2) The ingest route (layers/base/server/routes/inbox.ts) gates only on `config.features.federation` plus an HTTP signature; `verifyInboxRequest` has no peer allowlist and there is no domain blocklist anywhere (grep for blockedDomain/domainBlock: no hits). `onCreate` stores federatedContent for any signed actor with no follow relationship and no mirror match; `isHidden` defaults false, so `listFederatedTimeline` (no follow filter) surfaces it and useContentUrl.ts:46 links /mirror/{id}. (3) The flag is ON in production: `curl https://deveco.io/api/features` returns "federation":true. The read API (`/api/federation/content/[id].get.ts`) only calls `requireFeature('federation')` — no auth. (4) Render: /mirror/[id].vue:101 -> ViewsArticleView -> ArticleView.vue:175 BlocksBlockContentRenderer -> BlockContentRenderer.vue:81 `html: BlockHtmlView` -> `sanitizeRichHtml(html, {neutralizeColors:true})`. (5) I ran the sanitizer on the payload in vitest; it emitted verbatim: `<a href="https://evil.example/login" target="_blank" style="position:fixed;top:0;left:0;width:100vw;height:100vh;z-index:2147483647;background:var(--cpub-bg);display:block">`. STYLE_DECL_BLOCKLIST covers only url()/expression()/javascript:/vbscript:/behavior/-moz-binding/@import, and ADAPTIVE_COLOR explicitly preserves `var()` so neutralizeColors does not remove the backdrop. No ancestor in ArticleView.vue sets transform/filter/contain, so position:fixed resolves against the viewport, and CSP ships `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'` (packages/infra/src/security.ts:99), so inline style attributes are permitted. No guard exists at any layer. Severity corrected from P1 to P2: script execution IS blocked (script/style/iframe bodies removed, on* dropped, javascript: URLs rejected), so there is no session theft or code execution — the impact is persistent HTML/CSS injection giving clickjacking, defacement, and a phishing overlay that still requires the victim to click through to a visibly different domain, and moderators can set isHidden.

</details>

#### [P2] Inbound federated DMs are stored as messages authored BY the recipient, in a conversation only they are in

`packages/server/src/federation/inboxHandlers.ts:548`

The private-Note branch calls `findOrCreateConversation(db, [localUser.id])` — a single-participant conversation — and then `sendMessage(db, conv.id, localUser.id, ...)`, i.e. the message's `senderId` is the RECIPIENT. The remote sender's identity survives only as a plain-text `[displayName]: ` prefix built from `remote_actors.display_name`, a field the remote instance controls.

**Failure:** A remote actor sends a signed Create{Note, to:[https://deveco.io/users/alice], content:"…"}. Alice never learns it arrived: `getUnreadMessageCount` filters `messages.senderId != userId` (messaging.ts:274) so it is never unread, and `sendMessage`'s SSE fanout at :242 explicitly skips the sender, so no live event fires. When Alice does open Messages she finds a conversation with no other participant containing text attributed to herself. Because the prefix is the attacker's chosen `displayName`, a remote actor can set it to `devEco Support` and write `[devEco Support]: Your contest entry was disqualified — re-submit at https://…` into a thread that renders as Alice's own message history on her own instance. Federated DMs are, in short, both undeliverable as designed and a spoofing surface.

**Reachable input:** From any Fediverse server the attacker controls (actor doc at https://evil.example/users/support with `"name": "devEco Support"` and a published public key), send a properly signed inbox POST — the same request a Mastodon DM produces: POST https://deveco.io/users/alice/inbox Content-Type: application/activity+json Date: <now, within 5 min> Digest: SHA-256=<sha256 of body> Signature: keyId="https://evil.example/users/support#main-key",headers="(request-target) host date digest",signature="<valid>" {"@context":"https://www.w3.org/ns/activitystreams","id":"https://evil.example/activities/1","type":"Create","actor":"https://evil.example/users/support","to":["https://deveco.io/users/alice"],"object":{"id":"https://evil.example/notes/1","type":"Note","to":["https://deveco.io/users/alice"],"content":"Your contest entry was disqualified, re-submit at https://evil.example/x"}} No local account, follow relationship, or approval is required — only a valid signature from the attacker's own key, which assertActorMatchesSigner accepts because actor host == signer host. Result: a conversations row with participants ["<alice-uuid>"], and a messages row with senderId = alice's uuid and body "[devEco Support]: Your contest entry was disqualified, re-submit at https://evil.example/x". Alice gets no unread badge (senderId != userId filter fails) and no SSE event (fanout skips the sender); when she opens /messages the thread is listed under her own name (index.vue:27-28 falls back to conv.participants when the others list is empty) and the text renders right-aligned as her own message with a read receipt.

**Suggested fix:** Model the remote actor as a first-class conversation participant (or add a `remote_actor_uri` column to `messages` the way `hub_posts` already does with `remoteActorUri`/`remoteActorName`) so the sender is never the recipient, render the remote name as attributed metadata rather than as body text, and gate inbound federated DMs behind an explicit follow/allow relationship.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified line by line; no guard exists. inboxHandlers.ts:539/548 does call findOrCreateConversation(db,[localUser.id]) then sendMessage(db, conv.id, localUser.id, `[${senderLabel}]: ${content}`), so senderId is the RECIPIENT. findOrCreateConversation matches on jsonb_array_length = sorted.length, so it is truly a 1-participant conversation and cannot be an existing thread; federateDirectMessage (outbound) stores no local copy at all, so no counterpart thread ever exists. messages.senderId is an FK to users, so the model cannot represent a remote sender. senderLabel = remoteActors.displayName, written by resolveRemoteActor as `displayName: actor.name` straight from the remote actor doc, no sanitization or trust check. Consequences confirmed: getUnreadMessageCount filters `messages.senderId != userId` (never unread), sendMessage's SSE fanout does `if (participantId === senderId) continue` (no live event), and no notification is inserted on the path. Guard hunt came up empty: protocol/src/inbox.ts:78-84 dispatches Create unconditionally; onCreate has no blocklist, allowlist, or follow requirement (federationConfig is only consulted for mirroring/backfill); the only gates are features.federation and HTTP-signature verification. features.federation defaults false in config/schema.ts:87 BUT live curl of /api/features shows "federation":true on both deveco.io and commonpub.io, and POST https://deveco.io/users/test/inbox returns 401 "Missing HTTP Signature" — mounted and live. No test pins this behaviour. Two framing corrections that do not refute it: MessageThread.vue:55 renders `{{ msg.body }}` as escaped text, not v-html, so there is no XSS and injected HTML shows as literal markup (a plain-text payload still renders cleanly); and the message is styled cpub-msg-own, so it impersonates the victim to themselves rather than impersonating a third party, a weaker phish than claimed. What survives unambiguously: any Fediverse actor can, unsolicited and with no block or rate limit, insert attacker-chosen text into any local user's private message store attributed to that user, in a conversation with no other participant, and inbound federated DMs are functionally undeliverable (silent, never unread, no notification).

</details>

#### [P2] A malformed remote `cpub:blocks` entry throws inside the render function, 500-ing the mirror page

`layers/base/components/blocks/BlockContentRenderer.vue:141`

`visibleBlocks` destructures every element as `block[0]` / `block[1]` with no shape check, and the fallback branch evaluates `'html' in block.data`. When a stored block is not a two-element array — anything the remote sends that survives the single `Array.isArray` check at inboxHandlers.ts:770 — `block.data` is `undefined` (or a primitive) and the `in` operator throws a TypeError during render.

**Failure:** A remote instance delivers `"cpub:blocks": [["paragraph"]]` (or `[{}]`, or `["x"]`). It is stored verbatim. A visitor opens `/mirror/{id}`: `resolveComponent('paragraph')` returns BlockTextView, which computes `sanitizeBlockHtml(props.content.html)` on an undefined `content` and throws; with an unknown type the `v-else-if="'html' in block.data"` at line 141 throws `Cannot use 'in' operator to search for 'html' in undefined`. The throw happens during SSR, so Nuxt serves a 500 error page instead of the article — for that mirrored item, permanently, with no admin control that can repair or hide it short of a DB edit. Forking such an item (packages/server/src/content/content.ts:1286 copies `fc.cpubBlocks` into `content_items.content`) reproduces the same crash on a first-party /u/{user}/{type}/{slug} page owned by a local member.

**Reachable input:** Unauthenticated remote, against a live instance (deveco.io / commonpub.io, both `"federation":true` as of today). Step 1 — attacker stands up any AP actor, e.g. `https://evil.example/users/x`, serving a public key at that actor document. No account or approval on the victim instance is needed. Step 2 — signed POST (HTTP Signature by that key) to `https://deveco.io/inbox`: POST /inbox HTTP/1.1 Host: deveco.io Content-Type: application/activity+json Signature: keyId="https://evil.example/users/x#main-key",headers="(request-target) host date digest",signature="..." {"@context":"https://www.w3.org/ns/activitystreams", "id":"https://evil.example/acts/1","type":"Create", "actor":"https://evil.example/users/x", "object":{"id":"https://evil.example/u/x/blog/p1","type":"Article", "name":"Hello","content":"<p>hi</p>","published":"2026-08-20T00:00:00Z", "cpub:type":"blog", "cpub:blocks":[["paragraph"]]}} `assertActorMatchesSigner` passes (actor == signer). The attribution check at inboxHandlers.ts:604 passes (object.id host == actor host == evil.example). Line 770's `Array.isArray` passes. Row inserted at line 809 with `isHidden=false`, `deletedAt=null`, `cpubType='blog'`. Step 3 — `GET https://deveco.io/api/content?limit=50` (public, no auth) now returns that row as `{"source":"federated","federatedContentId":"<uuid>",...}` and the homepage feed card links to `/mirror/<uuid>` (verified live: two real federated items are returned this way right now). Step 4 — any visitor, logged in or not: `GET https://deveco.io/mirror/<uuid>` → useMirrorContent sets `content = [["paragraph"]]` → ArticleView's Array/length guard passes → BlockContentRenderer maps `data: block[1]` = undefined → BlockTextView.vue:5 evaluates `props.content.html` on undefined → "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'html')" thrown during SSR render. Substitute `"cpub:blocks":[{}]` to hit the cited line 141 instead: "Cannot use 'in' operator to search for 'html' in undefined". Both reproduced by mounting the real component. Step 5 (amplification, authenticated local member) — `POST /api/federation/content/<uuid>/fork` copies the same malformed array into `content_items.content` (content.ts:1286), so `/u/<member>/blog/<slug>` throws identically once published.

**Suggested fix:** Filter in `visibleBlocks`: keep only entries where `Array.isArray(block) && block.length >= 2 && block[1] && typeof block[1] === 'object'`. Additionally validate the block-tuple shape at ingest (inboxHandlers.ts:770) so malformed blocks are never persisted.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

I tried to refute this and could not find a guard anywhere on the path. Every link was read, and the render throw was reproduced empirically. WHAT I VERIFIED 1. No element-level validation on ingest. `packages/server/src/federation/inboxHandlers.ts:770` is exactly `const cpubBlocks = Array.isArray(object['cpub:blocks']) ? object['cpub:blocks'] : null;` and line 829 inserts it verbatim into `federated_content.cpubBlocks` (line 845 on conflict-update; line 1077 does the same on Update). I grepped `packages/` and `layers/` for `normalizeBlock|sanitizeBlocks|isBlockTuple` — zero hits. The only correct guard in the repo is the one the finding cites, `packages/protocol/src/contentMapper.ts:58` (`if (!Array.isArray(block) || block.length < 2) continue`), and that is on the OUTBOUND html path, never on ingest. 2. No allowlist gates the inbox. `layers/base/server/routes/inbox.ts` requires only `config.features.federation`, a valid HTTP signature, and `assertActorMatchesSigner`. `layers/base/server/utils/inbox.ts` has zero hits for blocked/trusted/allowlist. The Create handler's only additional check (inboxHandlers.ts:604-630) is that `object.id`'s host equals the actor's host — satisfied trivially by the attacker's own domain. No follow/mirror relationship is required for the `federated_content` upsert (that gate exists only for the hub-post branch above it). 3. Nothing sanitizes on read. `getFederatedContent` → `/api/federation/content/[id].get.ts` (public, only `requireFeature('federation')`) returns cpubBlocks raw. `layers/base/composables/useMirrorContent.ts:24` sets `content = fc.cpubBlocks` when `Array.isArray(...) && length > 0` — a length check on the OUTER array only. 4. The consumer guard does not help. `ArticleView.vue:174` guards `content.content && Array.isArray(...) && length > 0` — `[["paragraph"]]` passes all three. Same in ProjectView.vue:343 and ExplainerView.vue:301. 5. The throw is real — I mounted the actual component with @testing-library/vue and printed the errors: - `[["paragraph"]]` → "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'html')" (BlockTextView.vue:5 `props.content.html`, prop arrives undefined — Vue only warns) - `[{}]`, `["x"]`, `[["unknownType"]]` → "Cannot use 'in' operator to search for 'html' in undefined" (BlockContentRenderer.vue:141) - well-formed tuple → no throw. There is no test file for BlockContentRenderer at all, so nothing pins tolerant behaviour. 6. The route is live and the UUIDs are published. `curl https://deveco.io/api/features` and `https://commonpub.io/api/features` both return `"federation":true`. `curl https://deveco.io/api/content?limit=50` returns two items with `source:"federated"` and their `federatedContentId` UUIDs — `queryFederatedAsListItems` (content.ts:165-171) filters only on `deletedAt IS NULL` and `isHidden = false`, no mirrorId requirement, and `useContentUrl.ts:46` turns every such card into `/mirror/{federatedContentId}`. So the crash URL is handed to every visitor by the homepage feed, not guessed. 7. The fork amplification is real: `packages/server/src/content/content.ts:1286` is `content: fc.cpubBlocks ?? fc.content` with no validation, writing the malformed array into `content_items.content`; that row renders through the same BlockContentRenderer at `layers/base/pages/u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/index.vue:139`. 8. No remediation surface. `ls layers/base/server/api/admin/federation/` has mirrors, hub-mirrors, mirror-requests, trusted-instances, refederate, repair-types, retry — no endpoint that sets `federated_content.isHidden` or deletes a single row. `isHidden` is read in queries but never written by any API. So "DB edit only" is accurate. SEVERITY: P2 is correct, keep it. It is remotely triggerable by an unauthenticated third party (no account on the victim instance; they only need to run a signed AP actor), it is persistent, and there is no admin control to clear it. But it is not P1: the blast radius is one mirror page per planted item, not a site-wide outage; no data is exposed; nothing escalates privilege. Repeating it N times costs N pages, still not the site. HONEST LIMIT: I did not inject a live activity, so I did not observe the HTTP 500 itself — I verified the TypeError in the real component and read every step that carries a malformed array to it. Whether Nuxt renders its 500 error page or a degraded shell, the mirrored item is unrenderable either way.

</details>

#### [P3] Every inbox POST triggers an uncached outbound fetch to a caller-named URL before any cheap validation

`layers/base/server/utils/inbox.ts:106`

`verifyInboxRequest` resolves the signer's actor document live on every request — `resolveActor(actorUri, createSafeActorFetchFn())` — with no key cache and no negative cache, and it does so before reading the body or checking the Date header. The instance already maintains a 24-hour remote-actor cache (`resolveRemoteActor`, federation.ts:138-160) but the verification path does not consult it.

**Failure:** An attacker sends POST /inbox with `Signature: keyId="https://target-of-choice.example/some/path",headers="(request-target) host date digest",signature="AAAA"`. Before anything else, the instance performs a full HTTPS GET to that URL with a 30-second deadline and a 10 MB body cap; verification then fails and returns 401. At the federation tier's 60 requests/min/IP the instance can be pointed at a third party as a request reflector, and each in-flight request holds a Nitro handler plus an undici socket for up to 30 s against a slow-responding host. Because the fetch is unconditional and uncached, repeatedly re-sending the same keyId re-fetches every single time — no back-off, no negative cache for hosts that 404 or hang.

**Reachable input:** Unauthenticated, no session, against any instance with features.federation on (apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:31): POST https://<instance>/inbox Host: <instance> Date: <current UTC in RFC 1123, freshness irrelevant since the check runs later> Content-Type: application/activity+json Digest: SHA-256=AAAA Signature: keyId="https://victim.example/slow/endpoint#main-key",algorithm="rsa-sha256",headers="(request-target) host date digest",signature="AAAA" {"id":"https://x.example/1","type":"Create","actor":"https://x.example/actor"} Before reading the body or checking Date, the handler issues GET https://victim.example/slow/endpoint with Accept: application/activity+json and up to 30 s per hop (3 redirect hops possible), then returns 401. Re-sending the identical request re-fetches every time — no positive or negative cache. Higher fan-out variant: the same request to POST https://<instance>/hubs/<any-slug>/inbox, which getTierForPath does not classify as federation and therefore rate-limits at 120/min/IP instead of 60. Rate limiting is skipped entirely when NODE_ENV !== 'production' (layers/base/server/middleware/security.ts).

**Suggested fix:** Check the Date header and body size before resolving the key, cache verified public keys (keyed on keyId, with a short TTL and explicit invalidation on verification failure so rotation still works), and add a negative cache with back-off for keyIds that fail to resolve.

<details><summary>Verifier (medium confidence)</summary>

The code is exactly as described and I found no guard that closes it. `verifyInboxRequest` (layers/base/server/utils/inbox.ts:80-185) performs `resolveActor(actorUri, createSafeActorFetchFn())` at :106 on an unauthenticated request, against a URL taken verbatim from the caller's `Signature: keyId=...`, with no DB read before it and no cache write after it. Grep across the repo confirms this is the only `resolveActor(` call site that bypasses `resolveRemoteActor` (packages/server/src/federation/federation.ts:132-168), which holds the 24 h `remote_actors` cache for exactly this document — so the claim that the verification path ignores the existing cache is accurate. Ordering is also as claimed: the Date-freshness check (:124-137) and the raw body read (:146) both run after the fetch; only content-length, Signature-presence and keyId-presence precede it. Timeouts compound rather than cap at 30 s: safeFetchFn.ts:18 and actorResolver.ts:88 each set 30_000 ms, and actorResolver follows up to MAX_REDIRECTS=3 hops with a fresh 30 s deadline per hop. There is no cachedFunction, no in-flight coalescing and no negative cache anywhere on this path. Decisive corroboration that this is a recognised hazard rather than intended design: the sibling route layers/base/server/api/registry/ping.post.ts:30-33 adds a dedicated pre-verification per-IP cap (20/60 s) with the comment "verifyInboxRequest resolves the (attacker-controlled) keyId actor over the network, so an unauthenticated caller must not fan that out unbounded" — the same mitigation is absent on /inbox and /hubs/[slug]/inbox. federation:true in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:31, so the route is live. I did trim the impact story: this is not an amplifier (one inbound POST of up to 1 MB yields one small outbound GET, and the target sees the instance's own IP), and the suggested Date-first reorder does not stop an attacker who sends a fresh Date. The surviving, real defect is the uncached/negative-cache-free unconditional outbound fetch and the handler+socket occupancy it buys against the instance itself — a hardening gap, correctly filed at P3. Note also that /hubs/<slug>/inbox does not match getTierForPath's federation prefixes (packages/infra/src/security.ts:266-273) and falls to the `general` 120/min tier, i.e. twice the fan-out budget of /inbox.

</details>

#### [P3] Backfill follows a remote-supplied `next` URL to any host with an instance-actor-signed GET

`packages/server/src/federation/backfill.ts:249`

The outbox crawler takes `collection.next` straight from the remote JSON and assigns it to `nextPage`, which is then fetched by `signedGet` — a GET signed with the instance's own actor keypair and `keyId https://{domain}/actor#main-key`. Item ATTRIBUTION is correctly host-bound (the `ownerHost`/`itemActorHost` check at :219-225), but the crawl TARGET is not: `next` is never required to stay on the outbox owner's host.

**Failure:** An operator adds a pull mirror for evil.example (or a previously-legitimate peer is compromised). Its outbox page returns `"next": "https://third-party.social/users/victim/outbox?min_id=…"`. The crawler follows it and issues a request signed by the CommonPub instance's actor. On any authorized-fetch peer that trusts this instance, the instance is now a confused deputy fetching content on the attacker's behalf — up to `MAX_PAGES = 50` attacker-chosen URLs per backfill run, each carrying a valid signature from the victim instance. The attacker also learns the timing/existence of the instance's signing identity against arbitrary third parties. `safeFetchSigned` forces `followRedirects: false` and `isPrivateUrl` blocks internal targets, so this is a cross-instance confused-deputy rather than an SSRF, but nothing restricts the host.

**Reachable input:** Three concrete steps, all of which I traced to code: 1. An admin (permission `federation.manage`, `features.federation` on) creates a pull mirror for a host the attacker controls: `POST /api/admin/federation/mirrors` with `{"remoteDomain":"evil.example","remoteActorUri":"https://evil.example/actor","direction":"pull"}` (layers/base/server/api/admin/federation/mirrors/index.post.ts:14-16). 2. The same admin triggers the crawl: `POST /api/admin/federation/mirrors/{mirrorId}/backfill` with `{"maxItems":500}` (layers/base/server/api/admin/federation/mirrors/[id]/backfill.post.ts:53). Equivalently, approval-with-depth (`mirroring.ts:296`) or an `Accept` from `https://evil.example/actor` when `backfillOnMirrorAccept` is enabled (`inboxHandlers.ts:257-272`) reaches the same loop. 3. `https://evil.example/actor` responds with `{"type":"Person","@context":"https://www.w3.org/ns/activitystreams","id":"https://evil.example/actor","inbox":"https://evil.example/actor/inbox","outbox":"https://evil.example/actor/outbox"}`, and `GET https://evil.example/actor/outbox` responds: `{"type":"OrderedCollectionPage","orderedItems":[],"next":"https://third-party.social/users/victim/outbox?min_id=1"}` backfill.ts:195 reads that `next`, :249 assigns it to `nextPage`, and the next loop iteration at :171 issues `GET https://third-party.social/users/victim/outbox?min_id=1` carrying an HTTP Signature with `keyId=https://{local-domain}/actor#main-key`. Chaining a fresh `next` on each page gives the remote up to 49 further attacker-chosen targets in that run (MAX_PAGES=50, one per second). Caveat on this input, which is why it is P3 and not P2: step 1 requires an admin to have deliberately mirrored the attacker's host (or a legitimate mirrored peer to have been compromised) — the attacker cannot self-select as the crawl origin — and the response bodies from step 3 are discarded, never returned to the attacker.

**Suggested fix:** Require `new URL(next).hostname === ownerHost` (the host of `remoteActorUri`) before assigning `nextPage`, and treat a cross-host `next` as end-of-collection rather than as a link to follow.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The code claim is accurate and I verified every link. `packages/server/src/federation/backfill.ts:189/195` reads `next` straight off the remote JSON with a bare cast, `:249` assigns `nextPage = next`, and `:171` feeds it to `signedGet`, which (`:25-51`) signs with `getOrCreateInstanceKeypair` and `keyId = https://${domain}/actor#main-key`. Nothing between those points constrains the host. The item-attribution guard at `:220-228` pins `itemActorHost === ownerHost` (pinned by `packages/server/src/__tests__/backfill-attribution-binding.test.ts`), but that guard is about what gets stored, not where the crawler goes. I looked for the guard that would make this impossible and it does not exist: `safeFetchResponse` (`packages/protocol/src/ssrf.ts:391-465`) only enforces `isPrivateUrl` + pinned dispatcher + `followRedirects:false`; `validateActorResponse` (`actorResolver.ts:9-70`) accepts any `outbox: z.string().url()` with no same-host constraint; no test anywhere asserts a next-host rule; git history (bb8358f0 signed the fetches, c1c57308/e31c3ae6 were the 241/242 hardening passes that added the ITEM binding) shows the crawl target was simply never considered, not deliberately allowed. What I do NOT accept is the claimed impact, and this is why I drop the severity two notches rather than confirming P2: 1. Nothing the crawler fetches ever reaches the attacker. A page pulled from `third-party.social` yields items whose `actor` host is `third-party.social`, which the `:225` check rejects (`errors++; continue`). The only thing that returns to anyone is a `{processed, errors, pages}` count, and it returns to the local admin, not the remote. So "fetching content on the attacker's behalf" is a request the attacker caused, not content the attacker obtains — it is blind. 2. The signature produced is a GET signature over (request-target)/host/date with no digest. It cannot be replayed into any state-changing action, and it can only be replayed against the same host+path the attacker already chose. 3. `isPrivateUrl` per-hop plus `followRedirects:false` genuinely close the SSRF-into-internal-network direction, as the claim concedes. 4. The incremental capability over what the attacker already has is small: because `validateActorResponse` does not pin `outbox` to the actor's host either, a hostile actor document can already redirect the FIRST signed GET to any public host. `next` widens that from 1 request to at most `MAX_PAGES = 50` (`:12`), throttled by `DELAY_BETWEEN_PAGES_MS = 1_000` (`:14`) — roughly a 50-request, 50-second blind relay per backfill run. 5. The hostile host is not self-selected. Every entry point is operator-gated: `layers/base/server/api/admin/federation/mirrors/index.post.ts` and `.../mirrors/[id]/backfill.post.ts` both `requireFeature('federation')` + `requirePermission(event, 'federation.manage')`; `mirroring.ts:296-311` runs only inside admin approval of a mirror request; `inboxHandlers.ts:257-272` runs only when `federationConfig.backfillOnMirrorAccept` is set AND the Accept comes from an actor the operator already created a pull mirror for. So: a real, unguarded hardening gap in an area the file's own comment (`:149-155`) shows the authors were reasoning about, worth fixing with a two-line `new URL(next).hostname === ownerHost` check (and the identical omission is real at `hubMirroring.ts:1140` and `:1237` — I read both; the followers crawl at `:1237` is actually the looser one, since it hands attacker-supplied follower URIs to `resolveRemoteActor`). But its consequence is bounded, blind, rate-limited outbound request generation from an operator-vetted peer, not a confused-deputy content read. P3.

</details>

### Adversarial audit of session 256's uncommitted changes

#### [P1] Key locking was only wired to the registration builder; stage submission templates still rekey on a label edit

`layers/base/components/contest/ContestStageCard.vue:179`

The session's headline audit fix — snapshot server-supplied field keys and freeze them so a label edit cannot regenerate them — is plumbed only into `ContestEditor.vue:611` (`:locked-keys="persistedRegistrationKeys"`). `ContestStageCard.vue` renders the SAME `FormTemplateEditor` for each stage's `submissionTemplate` and passes no `locked-keys`, so the prop falls back to `() => []`, `locked` is always false, and `templateFieldLabelChanged`'s `tracksLabel` branch (`field.key === fieldKeyFromLabel(field.label)`) is live for every stage field the operator did not hand-key. `withTemplateFieldLabelChanged` even grew a `lockedKeys?` parameter that no production caller passes (only the test file references it).

**Failure:** deveco has 7 stages. An organizer fixes a typo in a stage field's label ("Repositry URL" → "Repository URL"). The key changes from `repositry_url` to `repository_url`. Every entrant's already-submitted answer stays in `contest_stage_submissions.fields` under the dead key; `contest_entry_private_fields.fields` and `contest_agreement_acceptances.fieldKey` rows recorded under the old key are orphaned the same way. The entrant's form shows the question as unanswered; the CSV export loses the column. Nothing warns.

**Reachable input:** Signed in as the contest owner (or a `contest.manage` holder / per-contest `editor` stakeholder) on an instance with `contests` on and `contestStageSubmissions` at its default true (deveco). Contest has a submission stage whose saved template contains an auto-keyed field `{ key: "repositry_url", label: "Repositry URL", type: "url" }`, and at least one entrant has already PUT `/api/contests/deveco-build/entries/<uuid>/submission` with `{"stageId":"prototype","fields":{"repositry_url":"https://github.com/x/y"}}`, persisted into `contest_entries.stage_submissions[].fields`. Trigger: open `/contests/deveco-build/edit`, in the Stages panel type the missing "o" into that field's Label input (ContestStageCard → FormTemplateEditor label input → `templateFieldLabelChanged` with an empty lockedKeys set), then Save. That issues: PUT /api/contests/deveco-build {"stages":[{"id":"prototype","kind":"submission","submissionTemplate":[{"key":"repository_url","label":"Repository URL","type":"url","required":true}], ...}], ...} which 200s. The entrant then loads the stage form: `ContestStageSubmission.vue:49` seeds `values["repository_url"] = existing.fields["repository_url"] ?? ''` → their answer shows blank/unanswered; their next save writes only `repository_url`, and the old `repositry_url` value plus any `contest_entry_private_fields` / `contest_agreement_acceptances.field_key` rows recorded under the old key are orphaned. Re-posting the old key is rejected by validateSubmissionFields' unknown-key guard, so there is no in-app recovery.

**Suggested fix:** Snapshot the stage templates' keys at hydrate the same way `persistedRegistrationKeys` is built and thread them through `ContestEditor` → `ContestStageCard` → `FormTemplateEditor`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified in the working tree (this is uncommitted session-256 work; `git status` shows ContestStageCard.vue, FormTemplateEditor.vue, useContestEditor.ts, contestStages.ts all modified). The claim holds on every link of the chain I read personally: 1. `layers/base/composables/useContestEditor.ts:232,320` — the new `persistedRegistrationKeys` snapshot is taken ONLY from `registrationTemplate`. There is no equivalent for `stages[].submissionTemplate`; `grep -rn "persistedStage\|stageKeys"` returns nothing. 2. `layers/base/components/contest/ContestEditor.vue:611` is the single `:locked-keys=` binding in the repo. 3. `layers/base/components/contest/ContestStageCard.vue:177-188` renders the same shared `FormTemplateEditor` for `stage.submissionTemplate` with no `locked-keys`; the whole diff of that file this session is the unrelated proposal-empty warning banner. So `FormTemplateEditor.vue:56` default `lockedKeys: () => []` applies, `lockedKeySet` (line 203) is empty, and `labelInput` (line 205) calls `templateFieldLabelChanged(..., new Set())`. 4. `layers/base/utils/contestStages.ts:171-172` — `const locked = lockedKeys?.has(field.key) === true; const tracksLabel = !locked && (!field.key || field.key === fieldKeyFromLabel(field.label));` — with an empty set, every auto-keyed saved stage field still re-derives its key from the new label. Hunted for the guard; none exists: - Flag: `contestStageSubmissions` defaults **true** (`packages/config/src/schema.ts:35`, `layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:109`), so this is on everywhere, not behind an off flag. - Server: `layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/index.put.ts` validates with `updateContestSchema`, which accepts `stages: z.array(contestStageSchema).max(20)` (`packages/schema/src/validators/contest.ts:379`) with no key-stability check against existing submissions. `packages/server/src/contest/validation.ts` (also modified this session) only validates a submission against the CURRENT template — and its unknown-key guard means old-key values can no longer be re-submitted, so the API can't restore them. - UI: `ContestStagesEditor.vue` has no `disabled`/`locked`/`hasSubmissions` gating; the template stays editable after entrants submit. - Tests: `layers/base/utils/__tests__/contestStages.test.ts:154-158` only exercises the hand-pinned-key path, not locking; the locked-key tests live in `contestConditions.test.ts` and only call the array-level function directly. Nothing pins the stage wiring. Damage path confirmed: `layers/base/components/contest/ContestStageSubmission.vue:49,54` seeds and diffs values as `existing.value?.fields[f.key]`, so after a rekey the entrant's stored answer reads as empty and their next save writes only the new key. `packages/schema/src/contest.ts:124-128` documents that `agreement`/PII fields are partitioned out of `stageSubmissions.fields` by the same key, and `packages/server/src/contest/submissions.ts:242,706` stores/reads `contestAgreementAcceptances.fieldKey`, so those rows orphan identically. This is a genuine gap in the session's own headline fix, not a restatement of intent: the doc comment the session added to `templateFieldLabelChanged` ("Without this, fixing a typo in 'Repositry URL' rewrites the key and orphans every registrant's answer") describes exactly the behaviour still live on all stage templates. Fix is a one-line prop plus a per-stage persisted-key snapshot. Severity: keeping P1 for parity — same mechanism, same silent data-loss outcome, larger surface (deveco has 7 stages vs one registration form), and the flag is on by default. It is an accidental-organizer-action bug, not a privilege issue, so a rubric that discounts owner-triggered paths would land it at P2; it is not lower than that.

</details>

#### [P2] Markdown import regenerates every field key from its label, bypassing the new key lock entirely

`layers/base/components/contest/FormTemplateEditor.vue:187`

`importMarkdown()` replaces the whole template with `registrationMarkdownToTemplate(...).fields`, whose keys are assigned by `uniqueKey(parsed.label)` / `uniqueKey(label)` (registrationMarkdown.ts:161 and :129) — i.e. recomputed from the label with a `_2`-style collision suffix. It never sees `props.lockedKeys`. `templateToRegistrationMarkdown` does not emit keys either, so an export→edit→import round-trip cannot preserve them. This is the same registration builder the lock was added to protect, one menu item away.

**Failure:** Operator on deveco opens the registration builder's markdown view to bulk-edit the 42-field template (the reason the DSL exists), pastes it back and confirms "Replace the current 42 field(s) with 42 field(s) from markdown?". Any field whose stored key is not exactly `fieldKeyFromLabel(label)` — hand-keyed fields, fields renamed before the lock existed, fields whose labels collided and got a `_2` suffix in a different order — gets a new key. Every registrant's answer, private-field entry and agreement acceptance under the old key is orphaned. The confirm text says nothing about answers.

**Reachable input:** As the contest owner/admin on deveco, with contest `jinger-2026` whose saved registrationTemplate contains {key: "repositry_url", label: "Repository URL", type: "url"} — a key that diverged because the operator fixed the label typo after the lock landed (contestStages.ts:171-183 keeps the old key) — and registrants whose contest_registrations.fields holds {"repositry_url": "https://github.com/..."}: open /contests/jinger-2026/edit → Registration tab → click "Markdown" (FormTemplateEditor.vue:300, enable-markdown set at ContestEditor.vue:613) → "Load current form" (emits `- Repository URL (url)`, no key) → "Import (replace form)" → accept the confirm "Replace the current N field(s) with N field(s) from markdown?" → Save, which issues PUT /api/contests/jinger-2026 with registrationTemplate[i].key = "repository_url". contest.ts:172 overwrites the template unconditionally. Every registrant's stored answer under "repositry_url" is now unreachable: export.ts:238 reads pub[f.key] = pub["repository_url"] and returns "" for that CSV column, and the same key mismatch strands contest_registration_private_fields / contest_agreement_acceptances rows for renamed PII and agreement fields.

**Suggested fix:** Match imported fields to the existing template by position/label and carry the existing key when one matches, or emit a `key=` modifier in the DSL. At minimum, name the orphaned keys in the confirm dialog.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified by reading the code, not the claim. importMarkdown() (FormTemplateEditor.vue:187-200) emits registrationMarkdownToTemplate().fields straight through; props.lockedKeys is consumed only by labelInput (:203-205). The DSL has no key syntax — uniqueKey() (registrationMarkdown.ts:74-85) derives every key from fieldKeyFromLabel(label) at :129/:161, and templateToRegistrationMarkdown emits label + modifiers only — so no export→import round-trip can preserve a key. I hunted for the guard and found none: no feature flag (enable-markdown is hardcoded on the registration builder at ContestEditor.vue:611), no server-side key preservation (packages/server/src/contest/contest.ts:172 is a bare wholesale overwrite of registrationTemplate), no schema constraint, and no test (FormTemplateEditor.test.ts has zero refs to markdown or lockedKeys). The consequence is real: export.ts:238 reads answers as pub[f.key]/priv[f.key], so a regenerated key blanks the registrants export and the panel. Crucially the new lock is what creates the divergent state it then fails to protect — contestStages.ts:171-183 deliberately keeps a saved key while the label changes, which is precisely the key the importer cannot reproduce. Where I depart from the finding: severity. It requires a contest owner/admin, a deliberate Markdown → Import (replace form) plus a confirm, and a template already carrying a diverged key; the answers are orphaned, not destroyed (they stay in the fields jsonb under the old key and return if the key is restored), and docs/reference/registration-markdown.md:91-99 already documents label-derived keys and replace-the-whole-form semantics. That is P2, not P1. The claim's 'hand-keyed fields' path is overstated — the builder exposes no key input, so divergence comes from the lock's own label-edit path, duplicate-label keys, or API-set keys.

</details>

#### [P3] The missing-answers summary cannot focus an agreement, radio, address or file field

`layers/base/components/contest/ContestRegistrationForm.vue:146`

`focusField` looks up `#${idPrefix}-${key}` first and only falls back to `[aria-labelledby="id"] input, … select, … textarea` when that returns null. But for `agreement`, `radio`, `address` and `file`, `ContestSubmissionField` puts `:id="fieldId"` on a `<span class="cpub-subfield-label">`, not on a control. `querySelector('#…')` therefore always finds the span, the `??` short-circuits, and `span.focus()` on a non-tabbable element is a no-op. The fallback branch — written for exactly these cases — can never execute.

**Failure:** deveco's registration form: 20 required fields including agreements and radio groups. A participant presses Save, the summary lists "I confirm my country of residence is the United States", they click it (or reach it by keyboard). The page scrolls but focus stays on the summary link — for a screen-reader or keyboard-only user the affordance does nothing, and the WCAG 3.3.1/2.4.3 benefit the session set out to deliver is absent for precisely the consent controls that matter. The vitest trace above confirms `target` was non-null for the radio field `country`, i.e. the span was matched.

**Reachable input:** No HTTP request is needed; this is a pure client interaction on an already-shipped-shaped page. Authenticated participant opens `/contests/<slug>/register` on a contest whose `registrationTemplate` contains a required `radio`, `agreement`, `address` or `file` field (an `agreement` qualifies with no `required` flag at all, since `isRequiredFormField` treats `mustAccept !== false` as required), leaves it unanswered, and presses "Save details". `save()` sets `attempted` and calls `focusField(first.key)`; focus stays on `document.body`/the Save button. Clicking (or activating by keyboard) the matching `.cpub-regform-summary-link` in the "N answers are still needed" alert has the same result. Reproduced verbatim with `render(ContestRegistrationForm, { props: { template: [{key:'name',type:'text',required:true},{key:'country',type:'radio',required:true,options:[{value:'us',label:'US'}]},{key:'tos',type:'agreement',required:true},{key:'addr',type:'address',required:true}] } })`, then `fireEvent.click(saveBtn)` and `fireEvent.click(link)` — activeElement was BODY for country, tos and addr.

**Suggested fix:** Query the control first: `root.querySelector('#id input, #id select, #id textarea, [aria-labelledby="id"] input, …') ?? root.querySelector('#id')`, or have `ContestSubmissionField` expose a dedicated focus target id for label-on-span types.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading and by running the component. `ContestSubmissionField` puts `:id="fieldId"` on a `<span class="cpub-subfield-label">` for agreement (:104), address (:139), radio (:158) and file (:177), while `focusField` (ContestRegistrationForm.vue:145-149) resolves `#${idPrefix}-${key}` first — it always matches that span, so `??` short-circuits and the `[aria-labelledby="id"] input|select|textarea` fallback is dead code. `span.focus()` on an element with no tabindex is a no-op. I rendered the real component with a text/radio/agreement/address template, clicked Save, then each `.cpub-regform-summary-link`, and logged `document.activeElement`: `Full name -> INPUT#cpub-reg-name`, but `Country -> BODY`, `I confirm my country of residence -> BODY`, `Mailing address -> BODY`. No guard exists: no feature flag gates the summary, `isRequiredFormField` (packages/schema/src/contest.ts:216) makes an agreement required by DEFAULT via `mustAccept`, `blockingFieldKeys` includes all four types, and no test clicks a summary link (grep over layers/ finds `.cpub-regform-summary-link` only in the component itself). One correction that enlarges the fix: for `agreement` and `file` the fallback could never have worked either, because neither branch emits `aria-labelledby` at all — only address (:142) and radio (:161) have that container — so reordering the lookup alone still leaves the consent controls in the failure scenario unfocusable. Severity reduced P2 -> P3: `scrollIntoView` still fires on the span so the page does scroll, the inline error, `aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby` and the `role="alert"` summary all still work, every field stays Tab-reachable, and the code is uncommitted session-256 work (git status shows ContestRegistrationForm.vue modified against e4694a97), so nothing is live. What is genuinely broken is the focus shortcut for 4 of 12 field types, including the `save()` handler's own `nextTick(() => focusField(first.key))` at :157.

</details>

#### [P3] balanceTags adds a spurious empty <p></p> for every unclosed <p> — the case it was written for

`layers/base/composables/useSanitize.ts:104`

`balanceTags` treats HTML as strictly nested, so `<p>a<p>b` becomes `<p>a<p>b</p></p>`. The HTML parser auto-closes the first `<p>` at the second `<p>`, so the trailing `</p>` has no `p` in button scope — per spec the parser then inserts an empty `<p>` element. Each unclosed `<p>` in the source therefore yields one extra empty paragraph, stacked at the end of the container.

**Failure:** Differential test against jsdom, run in this repo: input `<p>one<p>two` → parsed DOM `<p>one</p><p>two</p>` (2 children). After balanceTags → output `<p>one<p>two</p></p>` → parsed DOM `<p>one</p><p>two</p><p></p>` (3 children). deveco's rules body is the motivating case — 50,000 characters of pasted author HTML ending with an unclosed `<p>`; with `.cpub-md-html p { margin: 1em 0 }` each phantom paragraph is visible blank space at the foot of the rules. Both SSR and CSR parse the same string, so it is not a hydration mismatch, but the rendered output is now wrong where before it was right (an unclosed `<p>` alone caused no node relocation).

**Reachable input:** PUT /api/contests/<slug> as the authenticated contest owner (or a user passing canManage — layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/index.put.ts:7-23) with body {"rulesFormat":"html","rules":"<p>one<p>two"}. Omitting </p> is legal HTML5, so this arrives naturally in pasted author HTML.
