The legacy `/{type}/{slug}` → `/u/{author}/{type}/{slug}` middleware looks the slug up with `.where(and(eq(contentItems.slug, slug), isNull(contentItems.deletedAt)))` — no `status` filter and no `visibility` filter. Its own AP twin got this fixed and says so: layers/base/server/routes/content/[slug].ts:44-48 gates on `status='published' AND visibility='public'` with the note that without it 'a members-only/private item leaks its full body over AP **and its canonical URL over the browser redirect** (P-1 site 2)'. The browser-redirect half of that same defect still exists here, in the middleware, which runs before any auth and on every request.

**Failure:** A member is drafting `q3-layoff-announcement` (status='draft') or has a post at visibility='private'. An anonymous attacker requests `GET /blog/q3-layoff-announcement`. Because a matching row exists, the middleware answers `301 Location: /u/hr-anna/blog/q3-layoff-announcement`; if no such row existed it would fall through to the Nuxt catchall and render a page. The attacker learns the item exists, who authored it, and its real content type (the lookup does not even check that the requested type matches the row's type), all from the response status and Location header, on drafts and private items that are supposed to be invisible. Slug guessing is cheap because slugs are deterministic from titles (generateSlug at content.ts:737).

**Reachable input:** Unauthenticated, no cookies, no flags: GET https://deveco.io/blog/{slug} Accept: text/html where `{slug}` is the slug of any `content_items` row with `deleted_at IS NULL` — including one with `status='draft'` or `visibility IN ('members','private')`. Response is `301` with `Location: /u/{author_username}/{true_type}/{slug}`. The same request for a slug that has no row returns `404`. Verified live end to end for the author/type disclosure and the 301-vs-404 oracle using a published row: `curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code} %{redirect_url}" -H "Accept: text/html" https://deveco.io/blog/sentinal-net` → `301 https://deveco.io/u/zardaptor/project/sentinal-net` (note: requested `blog`, disclosed `project`), versus `https://deveco.io/blog/definitely-not-a-real-slug-xyz123` → `404`. The draft/private case follows from the WHERE clause at content-redirect.ts:43 containing only `slug` and `deletedAt`; I did not probe a real private item on the live instance. To reproduce the draft case locally: POST /api/content as user A with `{"type":"blog","title":"Q3 Layoff Announcement"}` (created as `status='draft'`, slug `q3-layoff-announcement`), then unauthenticated `GET /blog/q3-layoff-announcement` with `Accept: text/html` → 301 to `/u/{A}/blog/q3-layoff-announcement`, while `GET /api/content/q3-layoff-announcement` returns 404.

**Suggested fix:** Add `eq(contentItems.status,'published')` and `eq(contentItems.visibility,'public')` to the middleware's where clause (and `eq(contentItems.type, type)` so the redirect is type-correct), matching its documented twin.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED, with the severity corrected down. What I read and ran: 1. `layers/base/server/middleware/content-redirect.ts:37-55` — the whole unit. The lookup is `.where(and(eq(contentItems.slug, slug), isNull(contentItems.deletedAt)))`. There is no `status` filter, no `visibility` filter, and the requested `type` from the URL is not part of the WHERE at all (it is only used to decide whether to run, via `CONTENT_TYPES`). The row's own `type` and the joined `users.username` are then written into a `301 Location` header at line 55. 2. Both twins do gate: `layers/base/server/middleware/content-ap.ts:53-58` and `layers/base/server/routes/content/[slug].ts:41-49` add `eq(contentItems.status,'published')` and `eq(contentItems.visibility,'public')`, and the latter's comment explicitly names the browser-redirect half of the defect as part of what it is fixing. That comment is on the wrong file — the middleware, which is the surface a browser actually hits for `/{type}/{slug}`, was never fixed. 3. Guard hunt, all negative: - Middleware ordering is alphabetical: `auth.ts`, `blog-redirect.ts`, `content-ap.ts`, `content-redirect.ts`, `csrf.ts`, `features.ts`. `blog-redirect.ts` only handles `/article` and `/u/*/article/*`, so it does not intercept. `features.ts` runs *after* `content-redirect.ts` and gates only `/learn /docs /videos /admin /contests /events /explainer /settings/referral-links` anyway. - `layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:186` — `routeRules: {}`. Nothing rewrites these paths. - No feature flag guards this middleware; it is unconditional. - No RLS policy and no DB view: `grep -rn "ROW LEVEL SECURITY|CREATE VIEW" packages/schema/ layers/base/server/` returns nothing, so the query hits `content_items` raw. - Drafts do have slugs: `packages/server/src/content/content.ts:734-756` assigns `ensureUniqueSlugFor(generateSlug(input.slug || input.title))` and inserts `status: 'draft'` on create; `packages/schema/src/content.ts:46,49` default `status='draft'`, `visibility` enum is `['public','members','private']`. - The middleware ships to consumers: `layers/base/package.json` `files` includes `server`. - `layers/base/server/middleware/__tests__/` contains only `content-ap`, `csrf`, `require-terms`, `security-analytics-csp` — nothing pins content-redirect behaviour either way. Git history (`46e8f6d5`, `7b6c4a3f`, `2790a6a1`) shows the file was written for the URL restructure and never revisited during the session-204 visibility sweep, so the omission is an oversight, not a deliberate decision. 4. Empirically verified on the live instance that the middleware is active and that the Location header is the oracle: - `curl -H "Accept: text/html" https://deveco.io/project/sentinal-net` → `301 https://deveco.io/u/zardaptor/project/sentinal-net` - `curl -H "Accept: text/html" https://deveco.io/blog/definitely-not-a-real-slug-xyz123` → `404` (falls through to `pages/[...customPath].vue`, which throws 404) - `curl -H "Accept: text/html" https://deveco.io/blog/sentinal-net` → `301 .../u/zardaptor/project/sentinal-net` and `.../explainer/sentinal-net` → the same. This proves the requested type is ignored and the true type plus author are disclosed from the wrong-type URL. So 301-vs-404 is a real, observable existence oracle, and because the WHERE clause contains only slug+deletedAt, a `status='draft'` or `visibility='private'` row takes the identical 301 path. 5. The leak is incremental, not already-public: the canonical destination is properly gated. `getContentBySlug` (`packages/server/src/content/content.ts:599-608`) returns null when `status !== 'published'` or `visibility !== 'public'` for a non-author, so `/api/content/{slug}` and the `/u/.../[slug]/index.vue` page both 404 for the anonymous attacker. The middleware is therefore the only unauthenticated surface that confirms a draft/private item exists and names its author and true type. Severity correction: P2 → P3. What actually leaks is a confirmation oracle plus author username and content type, for a slug the attacker must already supply. No title, no body, no description crosses the boundary — unlike the AP twin, which leaked the full rendered article and was rightly P0/P1. Impact is bounded to "yes, hr-anna has an unpublished blog with that exact slug." Fix is a two-line addition of the same `status`/`visibility` predicates the twin already carries.

</details>

### The product subsystems round 1 never opened

#### [P1] docs_pages.parentId blocks only self-parenting — a two-request cycle hides both pages and loops buildPagePath

`packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:422`

`updateDocsPage` validates the parent with exactly one rule: `if (input.parentId !== undefined && input.parentId === pageId) throw new Error('A page cannot be its own parent')`. It never checks that the parent exists, that it belongs to the same `versionId`, that it belongs to the same site, or that the assignment does not create a cycle. `updateDocsPageSchema` is just `createDocsPageSchema.partial()` so `parentId` is only shape-checked as a uuid. The consumers walk the parent chain with no visited set: `buildPagePath` (packages/docs/src/navigation/tree.ts:57-61) is `let current = byId.get(pageId); while (current) { segments.unshift(current.slug); current = current.parentId ? byId.get(current.parentId) : undefined; }` and `buildBreadcrumbs` (tree.ts:40-45) does the same. `getDocsPage` (docs.ts:322-327) calls `buildPagePath` once for EVERY page in the version.

**Failure:** A docs owner with pages A and B sends `PUT /api/docs/handbook/pages/<A> {"parentId":"<B>"}` then `PUT /api/docs/handbook/pages/<B> {"parentId":"<A>"}`. Both return 200. The sidebar tree is built from the null root (`buildChildren(null)` in layers/base/pages/docs/[siteSlug]/[...pagePath].vue:78-83), and neither A nor B has a null parent, so both pages disappear from the nav on every docs page of that site while remaining live at their direct URLs — with no error and no way to notice in the editor. On the published `@commonpub/server` API, `getDocsPage` on that version enters `buildPagePath`'s `while (current)` with a cycle and spins synchronously forever, growing `segments` without bound: any fork calling it wedges its Node event loop. Setting `parentId` to a page uuid in a DIFFERENT site is also accepted, silently orphaning the child the same way.

**Reachable input:** As any registered (non-admin) user: 1) POST /api/docs {"name":"Handbook"}; 2) POST /api/docs/handbook/pages {"title":"A","content":[["paragraph",{"html":""}]],"status":"published"} -> id A; 3) POST /api/docs/handbook/pages {"title":"B","content":[["paragraph",{"html":""}]],"status":"published","parentId":"<A>"} -> id B; 4) PUT /api/docs/handbook/pages/<A> {"parentId":"<B>"} -> 200 (only the self-parent rule runs). Equivalent no-API path: open /docs/handbook/edit and drag page A onto its own child B with an "inside" drop — DocsPageTree.onDrop has no descendant check and issues that same PUT. Then 5) any visitor, unauthenticated, GET /docs/handbook/a: both pages are published so /api/docs/handbook/pages returns both to the public, renderedPage.parentId = B, and the breadcrumbs computed at [...pagePath].vue:139-152 walks B -> A -> B forever during SSR; the request never returns and the page's entry disappears from the sidebar of every page on that docs site.

**Suggested fix:** In `updateDocsPage`, load the proposed parent and reject it unless it exists AND `parent.versionId === page.versionId`, then walk the ancestor chain of the proposed parent and reject if `pageId` appears in it. Independently add a visited `Set` guard to `buildPagePath` and `buildBreadcrumbs` so malformed rows degrade instead of hanging.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The defect is real, unguarded and reachable by an ordinary authenticated user. `updateDocsPage` (packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:421-424) validates only self-parenting; the FK on docs_pages.parent_id enforces existence but not version, site, or acyclicity, and `updateDocsPageSchema` is shape-only. No feature flag blocks it (features.docs defaults true), no admin gate exists (docs sites are created by any authenticated user via requireAuth), and no test pins the behaviour. I corrected two parts of the claim: (a) a non-existent parent uuid is NOT silently accepted — the FK rejects it with a 500; (b) `getDocsPage`, the cited server-side spin, has zero callers in this repo (the layer route resolves by slug), so that specific loop only affects forks consuming published @commonpub/server. But the in-repo impact is worse than claimed: layers/base/pages/docs/[siteSlug]/[...pagePath].vue:139-152 contains its own unguarded ancestor walk for breadcrumbs, evaluated during SSR (ssr on, routeRules empty, useLazyFetch still fetches server-side), so a cycle turns a public docs page request into an unbounded loop with unbounded array growth in the Nitro process — hang then OOM — for unauthenticated visitors, plus the claimed sidebar disappearance via buildChildren(null). I simulated the walk over a two-node cycle: past 1,000,000 iterations with no termination. The cycle is also creatable through pure UI: DocsPageTree.vue onDrop (:196-205) guards only draggedId===targetId, so dropping a parent 'inside' its own child emits reparent and issues the PUT.

</details>

#### [P2] POST /api/docs/:siteSlug/versions passes the slug where a uuid site id is expected — always 500s

`layers/base/server/api/docs/[siteSlug]/versions.post.ts:10`

The route resolves `siteSlug` with `parseParams(event, { siteSlug: 'string' })` (no validation) and hands that raw string straight to `createDocsVersion(db, siteId, ownerId, input)` as the `siteId` argument. `createDocsVersion` immediately runs `.where(and(eq(docsSites.id, siteId), eq(docsSites.ownerId, ownerId)))` (packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:178) against `docsSites.id`, a `uuid` column (packages/schema/src/docs.ts:8). Every OTHER route under `[siteSlug]/` first calls `getDocsSiteBySlug` and passes `result.site.id`; this one does not. Secondly, even if the id were right, the copy-pages branch reads `input.sourceVersionId` (docs.ts:193) while the validator only whitelists `copyFromVersionId` (packages/schema/src/validators/docs.ts:36) — zod strips the unknown key, so `sourceVersionId` is always undefined and 'copy from version' can never copy anything.

**Failure:** A docs-site owner opens /docs/handbook/edit, expands Settings, types 'v2' in the new-version field and clicks create. `useDocsSiteSettings.createVersion` (layers/base/composables/useDocsSiteSettings.ts:80) POSTs to /api/docs/handbook/versions. Postgres receives `'handbook'` bound to a uuid comparison and raises `invalid input syntax for type uuid: "handbook"`; the error is uncaught, Nitro returns 500 and the UI shows 'Failed to create version'. Docs versioning is 100% non-functional on every instance and has never worked.

**Reachable input:** As a signed-in user who owns the docs site with slug `handbook`, open /docs/handbook/edit, expand Settings, type `v2` into the new-version field and click Create. That issues: POST /api/docs/handbook/versions Cookie: <valid session> Content-Type: application/json {"version":"v2","isDefault":false} requireAuth passes, createDocsVersionSchema validates {version,isDefault}, and parseParams returns the literal string "handbook", which reaches createDocsVersion as `siteId` and lands in `where docs_sites.id = 'handbook'` against a uuid column. Response is a 500 (invalid input syntax for type uuid, or the 'Not authorized' throw); the UI shows "Failed to create version". Any authenticated user reaches the same 500, since the failure occurs on the uuid bind before ownership is decided. Reproducible with curl by substituting any existing docs-site slug.

**Suggested fix:** Resolve the site first (`const result = await getDocsSiteBySlug(db, siteSlug)`; 404 if missing) and pass `result.site.id`. Rename `input.sourceVersionId` to `copyFromVersionId` in createDocsVersion, and scope the source-page query to the same site (`INNER JOIN docs_versions ON site_id = siteId`) so a caller cannot copy another owner's pages once the route works.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified in source, not taken on faith. versions.post.ts:7-10 resolves the route param with parseParams(event, { siteSlug: 'string' }) — and parseParams (layers/base/server/utils/validate.ts:112-135) only enforces a format when the declared type is 'uuid' or 'slug'; 'string' is an unchecked pass-through. That raw value is then passed as the `siteId` argument to createDocsVersion(db, siteSlug, user.id, input). createDocsVersion (packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:169-181) compares it against docsSites.id, declared uuid('id').defaultRandom().primaryKey() (packages/schema/src/docs.ts:8), and would also insert it into docsVersions.siteId, another uuid FK. The param really is a slug: getDocsSiteBySlug matches eq(docsSites.slug, slug), and edit.vue:10 takes route.params.siteSlug and feeds it to useDocsSiteSettings({ siteSlug: () => siteSlug.value }), whose createVersion POSTs to /api/docs/${slug}/versions with only { version, isDefault }. I hunted for the guard and there is none. features.docs defaults to true (packages/config/src/schema.ts:29) and the route performs no flag check. requireAuth only stops anonymous callers. No DB constraint or type prevents the value from arriving — the failure IS the type mismatch. The only test touching this path, layers/base/composables/__tests__/useDocsSiteSettings.test.ts, stubs $fetch, so it pins the client call shape rather than server behaviour; there is no integration or E2E coverage of the endpoint. Git history shows the file unchanged since the original layer-extraction commit (00790b98), so this is an oversight carried forward, not a deliberate design. Every sibling route under [siteSlug]/ (index.put.ts:10-15, index.delete.ts:8-13, index.get.ts) resolves getDocsSiteBySlug -> result.site.id first; this one alone does not. The outcome holds under either reading of the driver behaviour, which is why I am not marking it refuted on an unexecuted assumption: if Postgres raises 22P02 on binding 'handbook' to a uuid comparison, the error is uncaught and Nitro returns 500; if instead the predicate simply matched nothing, createDocsVersion throws new Error('Not authorized'), which is likewise an uncaught 500. Version creation cannot succeed on either path. Two corrections to the claim. First, severity: the report says P1, but this needs an authenticated docs-site owner acting inside the editor's Settings panel, fails loudly with a 'Failed to create version' toast, writes nothing, corrupts nothing and leaks nothing, and breaks one sub-feature of an optional module while docs page CRUD (which passes correct uuids) continues to work. That is P2, not a P1 outage. Second, the secondary allegation is factually accurate but overstated in impact: docs.ts:193 reads input.sourceVersionId while packages/schema/src/validators/docs.ts:33-37 whitelists copyFromVersionId, and the plain z.object strips unknown keys, so the copy-pages branch is unreachable. Since the UI sends neither key, that half is inert dead code rather than a user-visible failure. (A third divergent copy of the schema, requiring siteId, sits unused at packages/docs/src/validators.ts:17.)

</details>

#### [P2] Lesson slugs are unique per module but resolved per path — duplicate titles hide a lesson and block certificates

`packages/server/src/learning/learning.ts:461`

`createLesson` derives `const slug = generateSlug(input.title) || 'lesson-' + Date.now()` with no uniqueness check at all. The DB constraint is `unique('learning_lessons_module_slug').on(moduleId, slug)` (packages/schema/src/learning.ts:73) — scoped to the module. But `getLessonBySlug` resolves `.where(and(eq(learningLessons.slug, lessonSlug), eq(learningModules.pathId, path.id))).limit(1)` (learning.ts:923) — scoped to the whole PATH, with no ORDER BY. The entire learner UI addresses lessons by that path-scoped slug (`/learn/${slug}/${lesson.slug}` in pages/learn/[slug]/index.vue:196). `updateLesson` re-derives the slug from the new title on every rename with the same no-uniqueness path (learning.ts:508).

**Failure:** An author builds a path 'Intro to KiCad' with Module 1 → lesson 'Overview' and Module 2 → lesson 'Overview'. Both inserts succeed (different moduleId). Both get slug `overview`. Every learner hitting /learn/intro-to-kicad/overview gets whichever row Postgres returns first; the second lesson's content is unreachable, and POST /learn/intro-to-kicad/overview/complete always marks the FIRST lesson complete. `markLessonComplete` counts distinct completed lessons against the path total, so `completed` can never equal `total` — progress caps below 100%, `isPathComplete` never fires, and no certificate is ever issued for anyone on that path. Separately, adding two lessons with the same title inside ONE module hits the unique constraint; `createLesson` has no catch and layers/base/server/api/learn/[slug]/lessons.post.ts has none either, so the author gets a raw 500.

**Reachable input:** As any authenticated user (no special role), the ordinary authoring sequence: 1) POST /api/learn {"title":"Intro to KiCad","difficulty":"beginner"} -> path slug "intro-to-kicad". 2) POST /api/learn/intro-to-kicad/modules {"title":"Module 1"} -> moduleId M1. 3) POST /api/learn/intro-to-kicad/modules {"title":"Module 2"} -> moduleId M2. 4) POST /api/learn/intro-to-kicad/lessons {"moduleId":"<M1>","title":"Overview","type":"article"} -> 200, slug "overview". 5) POST /api/learn/intro-to-kicad/lessons {"moduleId":"<M2>","title":"Overview","type":"article"} -> 200 (the unique index is on (module_id, slug), different module), slug "overview" again. 6) POST /api/learn/intro-to-kicad/publish. Then as a second authenticated user: POST /api/learn/intro-to-kicad/enroll, then GET /api/learn/intro-to-kicad/overview returns the M1 lesson only; the M2 lesson is listed in the path detail and linked as /learn/intro-to-kicad/overview (pages/learn/[slug]/index.vue:196) but its content is unreachable. POST /api/learn/intro-to-kicad/overview/complete always writes lessonProgress for the M1 lesson id, so completed=1 against total=2: enrollment progress locks at 50, isPathComplete never fires, and no certificate is ever issued to any learner on that path. Separately, repeating step 4 with the SAME moduleId M1 and title "Overview" hits the (module_id, slug) unique index and returns an unhandled 500 to the author (layers/base/server/api/learn/[slug]/lessons.post.ts has no try/catch and there is no Nitro error plugin).

**Suggested fix:** Make the lesson slug unique across the PATH (query existing slugs for all modules of the path and suffix `-2`, `-3`… via a path-scoped `ensureUniqueSlugFor`), and change the DB constraint to match the resolution scope. Wrap the insert so a residual 23505 returns 409 rather than 500.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified line by line, and no guard exists at any layer. createLesson (packages/server/src/learning/learning.ts:461) assigns `generateSlug(input.title) || 'lesson-'+Date.now()` with no uniqueness pass, while every sibling creator (createPath :228/:273, content, product, video, docs) calls ensureUniqueSlugFor — the omission is confirmed by grep, not inferred. generateSlug (packages/server/src/utils.ts:5) is deterministic apart from RESERVED_SLUGS, so identical titles always yield identical slugs. The DB unique constraint is (module_id, slug) (packages/schema/src/learning.ts:73) but getLessonBySlug (:918-924) resolves `and(eq(lessons.slug, lessonSlug), eq(modules.pathId, path.id)).limit(1)` with no ORDER BY — path-scoped. learningModules has no slug column at all (schema:42-53), so the route space /learn/[slug]/[lessonSlug] structurally cannot disambiguate; this is a scope mismatch, not a missing test. complete.post.ts:20 is the ONLY caller of markLessonComplete outside tests and it feeds it result.lesson.id from that same ambiguous lookup, so the shadowed lesson can never receive a lessonProgress row; markLessonComplete's total (:734) counts every lesson under pathId while completed (:740) counts only rows that exist, so calculatePathProgress(2,1)=50, isPathComplete is never true, and the certificate insert at :770 never runs for anyone on that path. The learner UI compounds it: pages/learn/[slug]/[lessonSlug]/index.vue:47 uses `lessons.find(l => l.slug === lessonSlug)` so the completion tick also binds to the first match. Not flag-gated off: features.learning is true in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:28 and apps/shell/commonpub.config.ts:18 and defaults true in packages/config/src/schema.ts:85. Not pinned by tests: learning.integration.test.ts calls markLessonComplete with raw lesson ids, bypassing slug resolution. The secondary 500 claim also holds — lessons.post.ts has no try/catch, createLessonSchema only bounds title length, and layers/base/server/plugins/ contains no Nitro error hook, so a same-module duplicate title surfaces as a raw 23505 500. Downgraded to P2: it needs the path's own author to name two lessons identically, it is confined to that author's instance-local (non-federated) path, it is not a security or privilege issue, and it is recoverable without a migration because updateLesson re-derives the slug on rename (:508), so retitling the shadowed lesson restores both reachability and certificate issuance.

</details>

#### [P2] GET /api/content/:id/products is unauthenticated and ungated — a private project's BOM is public

`layers/base/server/api/content/[id]/products.get.ts:8`

The handler is `const { id } = parseParams(...); return listContentProducts(db, id);` — no `requireAuth`, no `getOptionalUser`, and no `visibleContentWhere` predicate. `listContentProducts` (packages/server/src/product/product.ts:432-479) joins content_products → products by contentId only and returns product name, slug, image, quantity, `role`, `notes` and `required` for every linked product. Every sibling route on the same content id is gated: products.post.ts:14-22, products-sync.post.ts:17-25 and products/[productId].delete.ts:11-19 all check `contentItems.authorId === user.id`; the read has nothing. The neighbouring `listProductContent` (product.ts:565-575) explicitly applies `visibleContentWhere()` with a comment about the P-1 leak, so the pattern exists and this route missed it.

**Failure:** A maker drafts a project (status draft, or visibility 'members'/'private') listing supplier parts with `notes` like 'sourced from X at $Y unit, do not publish'. The project id appears in the author's own editor URLs, in a members-only listing, or in an older public revision that was later made private. Any anonymous caller with that uuid does `curl /api/content/<uuid>/products` and receives the complete BOM including every `notes` string, while `curl /api/content/<uuid>` correctly 404s.

**Reachable input:** Three anonymous HTTP requests plus one ordinary author action, no crafted input and no guessing: 1. Anonymous: `GET /api/content?type=project&limit=100` — public route, returns ContentListItem[] each with `id`. Record the uuid U of any public project that has a BOM. 2. The author (or an admin) restricts it, e.g. `PUT /api/content/U` with `{"visibility":"private"}` (or `{"status":"draft"}`, or `DELETE /api/content/U`, which soft-deletes: sets status='archived' and deletedAt). 3. Anonymous, no cookie: `GET /api/content/U` → 404 (getContentBySlug returns null on the visibility/status/deletedAt gate). 4. Anonymous, no cookie: `GET /api/content/U/products` → 200 with the full BOM: every linked product's name, slug, imageUrl plus the author's `quantity`, `role`, `required` and free-text `notes` (the supplier/pricing field). A second, narrower path needs no prior publication: any authenticated non-author who knows U (for example a hub member who saw the project while its visibility was 'members', before the author switched it to 'private') gets the same 200 from step 4.

**Suggested fix:** Join `contentItems` and apply `visibleContentWhere(getOptionalUser(event)?.id)` inside `listContentProducts` (or gate in the route by loading the content item through the shared visibility helper first and 404-ing when it is not readable), matching what `listProductContent` already does in the same file.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code, not the summary. layers/base/server/api/content/[id]/products.get.ts contains no auth call of any kind and passes the raw uuid straight to listContentProducts, which (packages/server/src/product/product.ts:449-479) filters only on `eq(contentProducts.contentId, contentId)` — no join to contentItems, no status/visibility/deletedAt predicate. Meanwhile getContentBySlug (content.ts:598-608) returns null for non-published or non-public content to a non-author, so GET /api/content/<uuid> 404s on exactly the rows whose BOM this route still serves. I searched for every candidate guard and found none: features.ts middleware explicitly returns early for /api so no flag gates it; auth.ts only resolves a session and never denies; no other middleware covers /api/content/*; no DB constraint or type prevents draft/members/private/soft-deleted content from holding content_products rows (products.post.ts checks only authorId, never status); and the only test touching listContentProducts (packages/server/src/__tests__/product.integration.test.ts) calls the server function directly and asserts round-tripping, never authorization. openapi.ts:192-193 declares this GET with no `security` block while the sibling POST carries `security: [{ bearerAuth: [] }]`, which documents the unauthenticated behaviour rather than guarding it. Git history shows the file has never been touched since the original layer commit (00790b98), so it predates the P-1 visibility work that added visibleContentWhere and was applied to the neighbouring listProductContent — this is an omission, not a deliberate public surface. Crucially, the uuid is not a secret: ContentListItem carries `id` (packages/server/src/types.ts:159-160) and the unauthenticated GET /api/content returns it for every visible item, so any project's uuid is published while it is public and remains valid after updateContent (content.ts:806 accepts visibility, updateContentSchema accepts status) or the soft-deleting deleteContent (content.ts:844-851) removes it from view. Severity corrected to P2 as claimed: read-only, bounded field set, requires a previously-disclosed uuid, no write or privilege escalation.

</details>

#### [P2] Product edit/delete authorize on createdById alone — a removed hub member keeps write access

`packages/server/src/product/product.ts:141`

`createProduct` requires an ACTIVE hub membership before insert (product.ts:77-86, `eq(hubMembers.status, 'active')`, with a comment that pending requests do not count). `updateProduct` then authorizes with only `if (existing[0]!.createdById !== userId) return null;` (product.ts:141) and `deleteProduct` with `if (existing.length === 0 || existing[0]!.createdById !== userId) return false;` (product.ts:182). Neither re-reads `hub_members`. The write permission granted at create time is never revocable. The mirror problem is that a hub owner or admin has no path to `updateProduct` at all — `PUT /api/products/[id]` (layers/base/server/api/products/[id].put.ts:11) passes only `user.id` and has no `ownerOrPermission` escape hatch, unlike the DELETE route which does allow `content.moderate` (products/[id].delete.ts:21).

**Failure:** User U joins public hub H, adds product 'Widget v2' with a legitimate purchaseUrl. H's owner removes U from the hub for spamming. U's session is unaffected: `PUT /api/products/<widget-id>` with `{ name: 'FREE CRYPTO', purchaseUrl: 'https://phish.example' }` returns 200 and the edited row renders on H's public products page and in the global /api/products directory. The hub owner opening the same product gets 404 'Product not found' from products/[id].put.ts:14, because updateProduct returns null for a non-creator, so the only remediation available is a full delete by someone holding content.moderate.

**Reachable input:** Four concrete requests against a hub H with joinPolicy 'open' and privacy != 'private', as authenticated user U (session cookie only, no elevated role): 1. U: `POST /api/hubs/H/join` with `{}` → joinHub inserts hub_members(hubId=H, userId=U, role='member', status='active' by column default). 2. U: `POST /api/hubs/H/products` with `{"name":"Widget v2","purchaseUrl":"https://vendor.example/widget"}` → createProduct's active-membership check passes, row inserted with createdById=U, hubId=H. Returns 200 with the product id P. 3. Hub owner O: `DELETE /api/hubs/H/members/<U-uuid>` → kickMember deletes the hub_members row. (Optionally also `POST /api/hubs/H/bans` — makes no difference to step 4.) 4. U, same unexpired session: `PUT /api/products/<P-uuid>` with `{"name":"FREE CRYPTO","purchaseUrl":"https://phish.example"}` → updateProduct reads createdById === U, applies the update, returns 200 with the new ProductDetail. The mutated row is then served by `GET /api/hubs/H/products` (listHubProducts, no creator filter) and `GET /api/products` (searchProducts, only filters PRIVATE hubs). Remediation gap, same state: O issues `PUT /api/products/<P-uuid>` → updateProduct returns null (O is not the creator) → 404 'Product not found' from [id].put.ts:14. O issues `DELETE /api/products/<P-uuid>` → 403 'Missing permission: content.moderate' unless O separately holds the platform-level content.moderate permission (hub 'owner' is a hub_members role, not an RBAC grant).

**Suggested fix:** In `updateProduct`/`deleteProduct`, re-verify an active `hub_members` row for `(product.hubId, userId)` in addition to (or instead of) `createdById`, and accept a hub owner/admin role or a platform `content.moderate` holder as an authorized editor, mirroring the delete route's `ownerOrPermission`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The core claim survives. `updateProduct` (packages/server/src/product/product.ts:134-141) authorizes on `createdById` alone: it selects `{id, createdById}` and returns null only when the caller is not the creator. There is no `hubMembers` lookup and no `checkBan` call anywhere in the function (I read the whole 795-line file). `PUT /api/products/[id]` (layers/base/server/api/products/[id].put.ts:5-17) is `requireAuth` + uuid param + zod body, then a direct call to `updateProduct(db, id, user.id, input)` — no `ownerOrPermission`, no hub lookup, no flag. So the write permission granted by the create-time active-membership gate (product.ts:82-90) is never revocable. I hunted for the guard that would make this unreachable and found none: no feature flag gates /api/products or hubs/[slug]/products.post.ts; `joinHub` inserts a `hub_members` row whose `status` defaults to `'active'` (packages/schema/src/hub.ts:75) for an `open` joinPolicy; `kickMember` (packages/server/src/hub/members.ts:549-553) hard-deletes the membership row and does nothing to that user's `products` rows, which keep `hubId = H` and keep rendering through `listHubProducts` and `searchProducts` (neither filters on creator membership). `privileged-route-keys.test.ts` pins only the DELETE route's `ownerOrPermission` gate — nothing pins the PUT. Two things make this stronger than the submitted finding claims. First, banning does not close it either: `product.ts` never imports `checkBan`, unlike `editPost` (packages/server/src/hub/posts.ts:284-286) which does block banned authors. So a kicked AND banned user still edits the product. Second, git history shows this was an oversight, not a design: commit 9330e87c ("fix(security): product hub-membership + owner-or-moderator delete", session 204) added the create-time membership gate and the delete escape hatch and left `updateProduct` untouched. The `layers/base/pages/products/[slug].vue:11` comment ("PUT is owner-only on the server") documents the behaviour, it does not justify it. One correction to the finding: `deleteProduct`'s `createdById` check is NOT a defect. `[id].delete.ts:27-28` deliberately passes `undefined` as userId for non-owner moderators, so the data-layer check is an owner-scoped narrowing behind an already-correct route gate. The defect is `updateProduct` only, plus the mirror gap that a hub owner without platform `content.moderate` has neither an edit path (404 from the PUT) nor a delete path for a product sitting in their own hub.

</details>

#### [P2] Layout publish snapshots are never served — by-route returns live draft rows, so auto-saves go public

`layers/base/server/api/layouts/by-route.get.ts:90`

The public renderer calls `getLayoutByScope(db, scope)`, which assembles from the LIVE `layout_rows` / `layout_sections` tables (packages/server/src/layout/layout.ts:255-282). `layout_versions` and `layouts.published_version_id` are never read on any public path — grep shows `publishedVersionId` is only ever written. The only gate is `layout.state === 'published'` (by-route.get.ts:112). `saveLayout` rewrites those live rows on every PUT (layout.ts:390-450), and the editor auto-saves the whole draft on a debounce (layers/base/composables/useLayoutEditor.ts:445 `$fetch(PUT /api/admin/layouts/${id})` with `state: draft.value.state`). After a publish, `refresh()` sets `draft.state = 'published'`, so every subsequent auto-save keeps state published while replacing the live rows.

**Failure:** Admin publishes the homepage layout, then reopens the editor and deletes the hero section to try a variant. ~1s later the auto-save PUT fires with `state: 'published'` and the transaction deletes and re-inserts layout_rows. `invalidateLayoutsByRouteCache()` clears the cache. The very next anonymous request to / renders the homepage without the hero — no publish step, no confirmation. The layout_versions snapshot taken at publish time still holds the old content but nothing serves it; the only recovery is POST .../versions/:id/revert. The schema comment at packages/schema/src/layout.ts:41 states published_version_id is 'the version snapshot currently serving traffic', which is false.

**Reachable input:** On commonpub.io (verified `"layoutEngine":true` via GET /api/features, and GET /api/layouts/by-route?path=/ currently returns a published homepage layout to anonymous callers): 1. An admin holding `layout.manage` opens /admin/layouts/<homepage-layout-id>, whose stored `state` is already 'published'. 2. They delete the hero section in the canvas (or any config edit). No save click. 3. 1500ms later the editor auto-fires, verbatim from useLayoutEditor.ts:452-457: PUT /api/admin/layouts/<homepage-layout-id> If-Match: <original.updatedAt> {"scope":{"type":"route","path":"/"},"name":"Homepage","pageMeta":{...},"zones":[<hero removed>],"state":"published"} The handler runs `saveLayout(..., { id, userId })`, which in one transaction deletes every `layout_rows` row for that layout and re-inserts from the payload, leaving `layouts.state = 'published'` and `published_version_id` pointing at the now-stale snapshot. It then calls `invalidateLayoutsByRouteCache()`. 4. The next unauthenticated request — `GET https://commonpub.io/` (SSR issues `GET /api/layouts/by-route?path=/`) — misses the cache, `getLayoutByScope` reads the freshly rewritten live rows, `accessOk` is true (`pageMeta.access` defaults to 'public') and `layout.state === 'published'` is still true, so the anonymous visitor is served the homepage without the hero. No publish call, no confirmation, and the version snapshot that was supposed to be "currently serving traffic" is never consulted. Equivalent raw reproduction without the UI, as any authenticated admin: POST /api/admin/layouts/<id>/publish, then PUT /api/admin/layouts/<id> with `{"scope":{"type":"route","path":"/"},"name":"Homepage","zones":[],"state":"published"}` and no If-Match header, then GET /api/layouts/by-route?path=/ with no cookie — it returns `{"zones":[],...,"state":"published"}`.

**Suggested fix:** On the public path, when `state === 'published'` and `published_version_id` is set, serve `layout_versions.snapshot` rather than the live rows (falling back to the newest version row for a stale pointer, as the schema comment already promises). Keep the live-row read for the admin/preview tier only.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the whole path. `by-route.get.ts:90` calls `getLayoutByScope`, which assembles from the live `layout_rows`/`layout_sections` (packages/server/src/layout/layout.ts:255-282); no serving path ever reads `layout_versions` or `publishedVersionId` (repo-wide grep shows only the write at layout.ts:514, the projection at :316, and tests). The only publication gate is `isAdmin || layout.state === 'published'` (by-route.get.ts:112). `saveLayout` rewrites those live rows wholesale on every PUT and honours the client's `state` (`state: input.state ?? 'draft'`, layout.ts:365); `[id].put.ts` passes `body.state` straight through and then calls `invalidateLayoutsByRouteCache()`. The editor's auto-save sends `state: draft.value!.state` (useLayoutEditor.ts:452-457) on a 1500ms debounce (useLayoutAutoSave.ts, wired at pages/admin/layouts/[id].vue:353), and `publish()` ends with `refresh()` which clones the server record — now `state: 'published'` — into `draft` (useLayoutEditor.ts:291-296). So every post-publish auto-save carries `state: "published"` and replaces the rows that anonymous traffic reads. No guard exists: no snapshot read, no DB constraint, no auth difference, and no test pinning the correct behaviour. The nearest test asserts the opposite (layout-server.integration.test.ts:303-329: after editing a published layout, the current record holds the new content). This is not intended behaviour. `AdminLayoutsToolbar.vue:81-91` implements an explicit three-state model where 'modified' means "live, has un-published draft edits" — a state that can only exist inside the 1.5s debounce window, because the auto-save makes the edit live and clears `dirty`. `packages/schema/src/layout.ts:40` documents `published_version_id` as "the version snapshot currently serving traffic", which is false. `docs/plans/layout-and-pages.md:1027` specifies "Resolution: getLayoutByRoute('/') returns published version, NOT draft". The claim's severity rationale is factually wrong and I corrected it upward-then-held: `curl https://commonpub.io/api/features` returns `"layoutEngine":true`, and `curl "https://commonpub.io/api/layouts/by-route?path=/"` returns a populated published homepage layout (the anonymous HTML carries 60 `cpub-layout-section` markers). The feature is live in production on commonpub.io, not off everywhere. I keep P2 rather than raising to P1 because triggering it requires the `layout.manage` permission acting on the instance's own content, no trust boundary is crossed, and `layout_versions` plus POST /api/admin/layouts/:id/versions/:versionId/revert make it recoverable. One nuance that narrows (but does not remove) the blast radius: a client that omits `state` on the PUT would flip the layout back to 'draft' and take it offline instead; only the editor's explicit `state` pass-through produces the described silent-go-live, and that is the only production client.

</details>

#### [P3] The video detail page reads video.category, a field the API never returns — the category chip never renders

`layers/base/pages/videos/[id].vue:33`

The page computes `const categoryName = computed(() => { const cat = video.value?.category; if (!cat) return null; return typeof cat === 'string' ? cat : cat.name; })`. The API response has no `category` key: `getVideoById` returns flat `categoryId`, `categoryName` and `categorySlug` fields (packages/server/src/video/video.ts:141-161) and layers/base/server/api/videos/[id].get.ts:10-17 spreads that object and adds only an `author` sub-object. `categoryName` is therefore always null and the `<span v-if="categoryName">` tag block at line 68 is dead markup.

**Failure:** An admin creates the 'Soldering' video category, a member submits a video into it, and the videos index filters correctly by categoryId. Opening the video's own page at /videos/<id> shows title, view count and duration but never the category tag, so a viewer has no way to see or navigate to the category from the detail page — silently, with no console error.

**Reachable input:** On any instance with the default `video: true` flag: (1) as an admin, `POST /api/videos/categories {"name":"Soldering"}` -> returns category uuid C; (2) as any authenticated member, `POST /api/videos {"title":"Reflow basics","url":"https://youtu.be/abc123","categoryId":"C"}` (createVideo accepts categoryId, packages/server/src/video/video.ts) -> returns video uuid V; (3) as any visitor, `GET /videos/V` in a browser. The underlying `GET /api/videos/V` response contains `"categoryName":"Soldering","categorySlug":"soldering","categoryId":"C"` and no `category` key, so the page's `categoryName` computed evaluates to null and the tag chip is never rendered — title, view count, duration and date all show, the category silently does not, with no console error.

**Suggested fix:** Either read `video.categoryName` (and link via `video.categorySlug`) in the page, or have the route assemble `category: video.categoryId ? { id, name: video.categoryName, slug: video.categorySlug } : null` the way it already assembles `author`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the full call graph. The page's computed at layers/base/pages/videos/[id].vue:33-37 reads `video.value?.category`, but the only producer of that payload — layers/base/server/api/videos/[id].get.ts:10-17 — returns `{ ...getVideoById(...), author: {...} }`, and `getVideoById` in packages/server/src/video/video.ts builds an explicitly enumerated flat object whose category fields are `categoryId`, `categoryName`, `categorySlug`. No nested `category` object is constructed anywhere in the video subsystem; `grep -rn "category" layers/base/server/api/videos/` returns zero hits, so no sibling route or Nitro hook reshapes it. The fetch is typed `useLazyFetch<any>` (line 6, with an explicit comment excusing the loose type), so vue-tsc cannot catch the wrong key. `categoryName` is therefore permanently null and the `<span v-if="categoryName">` at line 68 is dead markup. No guard exists: the route is gated only by the `video` flag, which defaults true (packages/config/src/schema.ts:30) and is explicitly true in both apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:16 and apps/shell/commonpub.config.ts:16. No test pins the chip. Scope is one file wider than claimed: test-site/pages/videos/[id].vue:29-30,64 carries the identical dead computed and span.

</details>

#### [P3] POST /api/docs/migrate-content writes JSON strings into a jsonb column, re-creating double-encoding

`layers/base/server/api/docs/migrate-content.post.ts:71`

Both write paths set `content` to a JSON *string*: line 50 `.set({ content: JSON.stringify([['paragraph', { html: '' }]]) })` and line 71 `.set({ content: jsonContent })` where `jsonContent = JSON.stringify(blocks)`. `docsPages.content` is `jsonb('content')` (packages/schema/src/docs.ts:48) and drizzle stringifies again, so the row lands as a jsonb STRING rather than a jsonb ARRAY — precisely the failure the sibling code warns about in createDocsPage's comment ('Manually JSON.stringify-ing here would double-encode … storing the content as a jsonb STRING rather than a jsonb ARRAY, which breaks any SQL that uses jsonb_typeof / jsonb_array_elements', docs.ts:379-385) and which `searchDocsPages` carries a dedicated 'Historical: content got double-stringified before the session-129 fix' branch for (docs.ts:606-621). Separately, now that the ALTER to jsonb in CLAUDE.md rule #4 has run, drizzle returns already-migrated pages as parsed ARRAYS: the `typeof content === 'string'` skip at line 34 and the empty-check at line 45 both miss, and `markdownToBlockTuples(content as string)` throws `md.trim is not a function` (packages/editor/src/markdown/parser.ts:71) for every one. Finally the gate is `requirePermission(event, 'content.editorial')` (line 19) despite the file docstring saying 'Requires admin auth' — an editor, not an admin, can trigger a rewrite of every docs page on the instance across every owner's site.

**Failure:** An editor on commonpub.io clicks the migrate button. Pages already in BlockTuple form come back as arrays, throw inside the try, and are counted as `errored` — the response reports errors for the whole site and tells the operator to 'Fix errors and re-run'. Any page still holding legacy markdown (a jsonb string) is converted and written back double-encoded, so `jsonb_typeof(content)` is `'string'` not `'array'` and the page falls into the legacy branch of every read path from then on. Re-running compounds nothing but never converges.

**Reachable input:** Two requests, both on a stock instance (no flags needed): 1. As any authenticated owner of a docs site, create a page whose content is a markdown string (the Zod validator accepts it: `content: z.union([z.string(), z.array(z.array(z.unknown()))]).default('')`, packages/schema/src/validators/docs.ts:20-23): POST /api/docs/handbook/pages {"title":"Legacy","content":"# Hello\n\nWorld"} The row is stored as a jsonb STRING. 2. As an instance admin (users.role === 'admin'; with features.rbac=false this is the only role that passes `requirePermission(event,'content.editorial')`), with a session cookie and CSRF header: POST /api/docs/migrate-content (empty body) Outcome, both halves verified against the code: the "Legacy" page is read back as the JS string "# Hello\n\nWorld", converted to blocks, `JSON.stringify`-ed at line 67 and written at line 71, where drizzle stringifies again — the row ends as `jsonb_typeof(content) = 'string'` instead of `'array'`. In the same run, every existing BlockTuple page comes back as a JS array, throws `md.trim is not a function` inside the try at line 66, and is counted in `errored`, so the response reports errors for the whole site and says "Fix errors and re-run". No pre-existing legacy DB state is required — step 1 constructs the vulnerable row through a supported, currently-live API.

**Suggested fix:** Pass the array through unstringified (`.set({ content: blocks })`), branch on `Array.isArray(content)` to skip already-migrated rows instead of `typeof === 'string'`, and raise the gate to an admin-level permission given the route rewrites every site's pages.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The write-side mechanism is real and I verified every link personally. 1. `docsPages.content` is `jsonb('content').notNull()` (packages/schema/src/docs.ts:48) and the baseline migration already creates it as jsonb (packages/schema/migrations/0000_session128_baseline.sql:325) — the ALTER in the docstring is historical, not pending. 2. drizzle 0.45.2's `PgJsonb.mapToDriverValue(value) { return JSON.stringify(value); }` (node_modules/.pnpm/drizzle-orm@0.45.2*/pg-core/columns/jsonb.cjs). So `.set({ content: JSON.stringify(blocks) })` at migrate-content.post.ts:71 (and the `JSON.stringify([['paragraph',{html:''}]])` at :50) stringifies twice and lands a jsonb STRING. This is exactly what `createDocsPage` warns against in its comment (packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:379-386, "Manually JSON.stringify-ing here would double-encode … breaks any SQL that uses jsonb_typeof / jsonb_array_elements") and what migration 0001_docs_content_unstringify.sql exists to clean up. The session-129 fix was applied to createDocsPage/updateDocsPage but not to this endpoint. No TS guard catches it: drizzle types a bare `jsonb()` as `unknown`. 3. The skip logic is also dead. `mapFromDriverValue` JSON.parses strings, and node-postgres already parses jsonb, so a BlockTuple row comes back as a JS ARRAY. `typeof content === 'string'` (:34) is false, `!content` (:45) is false for an array, and `markdownToBlockTuples(content as string)` hits `if (!md || !md.trim())` (packages/editor/src/markdown/parser.ts:71) → `md.trim is not a function` → caught → `errored++`. Every already-migrated page is reported as an error, so the response always ends "Some pages failed conversion. Fix errors and re-run." and never converges. Guards I looked for and did NOT find: no `requireFeature('docs')` on the handler, and layers/base/server/middleware/features.ts explicitly skips `/api`, so the docs flag does not gate it; no DB constraint; no test pins the write shape (layers/base/server/api/__tests__/privileged-route-keys.test.ts only asserts the permission KEY); no UI caller, but a Nitro route needs none. TWO CORRECTIONS to the claim, which is why I am cutting the severity to P3: (a) The "an editor, not an admin" framing is WRONG under shipped config. `features.rbac` defaults to false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:136, layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:138), and with the flag off `resolveUserPermissions` returns an EMPTY grant set for every non-admin (packages/server/src/rbac/resolver.ts:80). `hasPermissionPure` then passes only on the admin floor (`primaryRole === 'admin'`). So on any instance with rbac off this is admin-only; a staff/custom role can reach it only if an operator turns rbac ON and grants `content.editorial`. (b) The "falls into the legacy branch of every read path" impact is OVERSTATED. The double-encoded shape is tolerated on both read paths I checked: drizzle's `mapFromDriverValue` JSON.parses it back into an array for the app, and `searchDocsPages` has a dedicated `jsonb_typeof(dp.content) = 'string' AND substr(... ,1,1) IN ('[','{')` branch that parses and walks it (packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:606-621). It is the only raw SQL touching `docs_pages.content` in the repo (grep). So no user-visible breakage results today — it violates the documented invariant and re-creates rows that migration 0001 was written to clean up, and it silently breaks any future jsonb_array_elements SQL. Net: a genuine, reachable write-side defect in an obsolete admin-only endpoint, with no data loss and no current read-path breakage. P3, not P2.

</details>

#### [P3] Duplicating the same docs page twice raises an uncaught unique violation — 500 instead of a second copy

`packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:474`

`duplicateDocsPage` hardcodes `const copySlug = `${original.slug}-copy`` with no uniqueness resolution, while `docsPages` carries `unique('docs_pages_version_slug').on(t.versionId, t.slug)` (packages/schema/src/docs.ts:57). The route only translates the string 'Not authorized' into a 404 and rethrows everything else (layers/base/server/api/docs/[siteSlug]/pages/[pageId]/duplicate.post.ts:10-15). The same gap exists on create: `createDocsPage` uses `input.slug || generateSlug(input.title)` with no dedupe (docs.ts:369), and layers/base/server/api/docs/[siteSlug]/pages/index.post.ts has no catch either.

**Failure:** An author duplicates the 'Getting started' page to draft a variant, keeps the copy, then duplicates 'Getting started' again a week later for a second variant. The second POST tries to insert slug `getting-started-copy` into the same version, hits `docs_pages_version_slug`, and Nitro returns a raw 500 with no indication that the cause is a name clash. Identically, creating two docs pages titled 'FAQ' in one version 500s on the second.

**Reachable input:** As a signed-in user who owns docs site `handbook` (created via `POST /api/docs {"slug":"handbook","name":"Handbook"}`, requireAuth only, no admin needed), with page `P` titled "Getting started" (slug `getting-started`) in the default version: (1) `POST /api/docs/handbook/pages/P/duplicate` -> 200, creates slug `getting-started-copy`; (2) keep that copy and issue the identical request again, `POST /api/docs/handbook/pages/P/duplicate` -> 500 Internal Server Error, because the insert of `getting-started-copy` hits docs_pages_version_slug and duplicate.post.ts rethrows any error whose message is not exactly 'Not authorized'. In the UI this is the page-tree context menu "Duplicate" clicked twice on the same page (DocsPageTree.vue:151 -> useDocsPageTree.ts:96), which surfaces only the generic toast "Failed to duplicate page". The create path is reachable the same way: `POST /api/docs/handbook/pages {"title":"FAQ","content":[]}` twice -> the second 500s, since slug is optional in createDocsPageSchema and both resolve to `faq` in the same default version.

**Suggested fix:** Use the existing `ensureUniqueSlugFor` helper scoped to `versionId` for both `createDocsPage` and `duplicateDocsPage` (it is already imported in this file and used by `createDocsSite` at docs.ts:100), producing `getting-started-copy-2` on the second duplicate.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the full function and reproducing the constraint violation on the live dev DB. duplicateDocsPage (packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:448-495) hardcodes `copySlug = `${original.slug}-copy`` and does a bare `.insert(docsPages)` with no onConflict, no probe, no suffix resolution, while docs_pages carries a live UNIQUE(version_id, slug) constraint (verified via `\d docs_pages`, defined at packages/schema/src/docs.ts:57, migrated in 0000_session128_baseline.sql:331). The route (layers/base/server/api/docs/[siteSlug]/pages/[pageId]/duplicate.post.ts:11-14) catches ONLY the literal string 'Not authorized' and rethrows everything else, so the 23505 surfaces as an unmapped Nitro 500. I searched for a guard and found none: no global error hook or middleware maps driver errors (the repo maps 23505 explicitly elsewhere — notification.ts:179-186, persona/consent.ts:918-927, federation/mirroring.ts:284 — so the omission here is real, not centrally handled); the `docs` feature flag defaults to true (packages/config/src/schema.ts:29); auth is only requireAuth plus an ownerId scope, which the triggering user satisfies by definition; there is no server-side test directory for docs at all (packages/server/src/docs/__tests__ does not exist), so nothing pins the correct behaviour. Git blame shows the function was added whole in 7bffcef5 with no dedupe, i.e. an omission rather than a deliberate design. The create path has the identical gap: createDocsPageSchema makes slug optional (packages/schema/src/validators/docs.ts:17) so createDocsPage falls back to generateSlug(title) at docs.ts:369 with no dedupe, and index.post.ts has no try/catch whatsoever. Empirical repro on commonpub_verify running the exact insert shape the function emits: first copy inserts, second returns `ERROR: duplicate key value violates unique constraint "docs_pages_version_slug" DETAIL: Key (version_id, slug)=(aae9a131-..., getting-started-copy) already exists.` Severity stays P3: it is owner-scoped to your own docs site, loses no data, exposes nothing cross-tenant, and the client catches the rejection into a generic toast (useDocsPageTree.ts:105); the defect is a bare 500 where a 409 or an auto-suffixed slug belongs.

</details>

### Denormalized counters and background jobs

#### [P2] GET /api/videos/:id increments view_count on every anonymous read with no dedup, unlike the content path

`layers/base/server/api/videos/[id].get.ts:9`

The video detail GET calls `incrementVideoViewCount(db, id)` unconditionally on every request. The sibling counter for content is a separate POST route (layers/base/server/api/content/[id]/view.post.ts) that keeps an in-process IP+content map with a 5-minute cooldown specifically to stop this: 'the previous leftmost-token read let any anonymous caller rotate X-Forwarded-For: random to inflate view counts'. The video route has no dedup, no auth, no method restriction (it is a GET, so it is also a write on a cacheable/prefetchable verb), and lands in the `api` rate-limit tier (packages/infra/src/security.ts:252, 60 req/min/IP).

**Failure:** An anonymous caller runs `while true; do curl -s https://<instance>/api/videos/<uuid> >/dev/null; done`. Each request commits `UPDATE videos SET view_count = view_count + 1`, capped only by the 60/min IP tier — 86,400 views/day from one IP versus a hard ceiling of 288/day per IP on the content equivalent. The inflated number is what `layers/base/pages/videos/[id].vue:69` prints as "N views", what `?sort=viewed` orders by (video.ts:69), and what `GET /api/public/v1/videos` serializes (serializers.ts:590). Ordinary link-preview bots and browser prefetch inflate it too, and because the write is on the GET no HTTP/CDN cache can ever be put in front of the route.

**Reachable input:** Unauthenticated, no headers, no body: GET https://commonpub.io/api/videos/f60fce27-580f-4c9d-9fbc-bc339ce62d00 I ran this live three times against the production instance and read the count back from GET /api/videos?limit=1 before and after: viewCount went 84 -> 87. One request = one committed `UPDATE videos SET view_count = view_count + 1`. No session cookie, no CSRF token, no dedup key, no cooldown. Repeating it in a loop is bounded only by the /api/ rate-limit tier (60 req/min/IP), i.e. ~86,400 increments/day from a single IP, versus 288/day/IP for the content equivalent. Note the video flag does NOT guard this: layers/base/server/middleware/features.ts explicitly returns early for any path starting with /api ("API routes handle their own gating via requireFeature() in each handler"), and grep for `requireFeature` across layers/base/server/api/videos/ returns zero matches. deveco.io has video:false yet GET /api/videos?limit=1 still answers 200 with a JSON body — the flag only 404s the /videos page routes.

**Suggested fix:** Move the increment behind the same dedup the content path uses (shared helper keyed on trusted client IP + resource id), or split it into a POST the page calls once, matching content/[id]/view.post.ts.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED — I tried to kill this one and could not. Code personally read, whole unit not just the cited line. layers/base/server/api/videos/[id].get.ts is 18 lines total: `parseParams(event, { id: 'uuid' })`, `getVideoById`, 404 if missing, then `await incrementVideoViewCount(db, id)` unconditionally before the return. There is no auth call, no ownership check, no dedup map, no cooldown, no `requireFeature`. packages/server/src/video/video.ts:280 is a bare `db.update(videos).set({ viewCount: sql\`${videos.viewCount} + 1\` }).where(eq(videos.id, id))` — no WHERE on viewer, no ON CONFLICT, no rate window. The sibling contrast is real and deliberate, not invented by the reporter. layers/base/server/api/content/[id]/view.post.ts is a POST that keeps a module-level `recentViews` Map keyed `${getClientIp(event)}:${id}`, a `VIEW_COOLDOWN_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000` early-return, and an unref'd 2-minute sweeper. Its comment explicitly names the abuse it was written to stop: "the previous leftmost-token read let any anonymous caller rotate `X-Forwarded-For: random` to inflate view counts past the 5-min cap." So the codebase already decided what correct looks like for this exact counter class; the video path predates and never adopted it. Guards I hunted for and eliminated, one by one: 1. Feature flag off everywhere — FALSE on both counts. commonpub.io reports video:true (curl /api/features). And even where it is false (deveco.io), features.ts short-circuits on /api paths, so the route stays live; deveco returns 200 for GET /api/videos. 2. requireFeature in the handler — zero matches anywhere under layers/base/server/api/videos/. 3. Auth/CSRF middleware — the csrf middleware cannot apply to a GET, and there is no auth requirement in the handler; verified by the anonymous curl succeeding and committing the write. 4. Nitro routeRules caching or swr in front of /api/videos — no matches in either nuxt.config. 5. A test pinning current behaviour as intended — packages/server/src/__tests__/video.test.ts:126-128 calls incrementVideoViewCount three times and asserts the count is 3. That test pins the pure DB helper's arithmetic, which is correct; it says nothing about route-level dedup, so it is not a "this is intended" signal. 6. Rate limiting as mitigation — it exists but does not refute. packages/infra/src/security.ts DEFAULT_TIERS.api = { limit: 60, windowMs: 60_000 } and getTierForPath falls through to the api tier for /api/videos/*. That is a ceiling 300x looser than the content path's 5-minute-per-IP-per-item cap, and layers/base/server/middleware/security.ts skips rate limiting entirely when NODE_ENV !== 'production'. The consequence chain the reporter cites also checks out: the inflated number is what the videos detail page renders as "N views", what `?sort=viewed` orders by, and what the public v1 serializer emits. Because the mutation rides a GET, ordinary link-preview bots, browser prefetch and any future CDN/HTTP cache in front of the route all corrupt or defeat it. Severity: P2 stands, and I considered pushing it to P3. Against P3: it is anonymous, unauthenticated, requires no crafting, is verified live on a production instance, and lets any visitor drive `?sort=viewed` ranking. Against P1: the blast radius is metric integrity only — no PII, no privilege escalation, no data loss, no availability impact, and the one production instance with real membership (deveco) currently has zero video rows. P2 is the right bucket. Only nuance worth flagging to the parent: the reporter's "86,400 views/day" assumes a single IP saturating the 60/min tier for 24h; that arithmetic is right, but a Redis-backed store is used when NUXT_REDIS_URL is set, so the tier is enforced across instances rather than per-process. That tightens nothing about the finding — it just means the 60/min figure is the real ceiling, not an optimistic one.

</details>

#### [P3] videos.like_count and videos.comment_count have zero writers; video likes/comments bump content_items instead

`packages/server/src/social/social.ts:126`

`updateLikeCount` switches on `targetType` with cases only for `comment` and `post`; everything else falls to `default:`, which does `UPDATE content_items SET like_count = like_count + 1 WHERE id = <targetId>`. `likeTargetTypeSchema` (packages/schema/src/validators/social.ts:5-13) accepts `'video'`, and a video id is a `videos.id`, never a `content_items.id` — so the statement matches 0 rows. `createComment` has the identical shape at social.ts:383: `targetType==='post'` bumps `hub_posts.reply_count`, everything else (including `'video'` and `'lesson'`) bumps `content_items.comment_count` by a videos/lessons id. Grep confirms the ONLY production write to the `videos` table in the whole repo is `viewCount + 1` (packages/server/src/video/video.ts:282); `packages/server/src/__tests__/video.test.ts:154-156` sets `likeCount` by hand, which is why the sort test is green. Neither column is in scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs, so nothing repairs them either.

**Failure:** A member opens /videos/<id> and clicks the heart. `POST /api/social/like {targetType:'video', targetId}` inserts the `likes` row, `updateLikeCount` runs `UPDATE content_items ... WHERE id = <videoId>` → 0 rows. The heart fills (state comes from `isLiked`) but `layers/base/pages/videos/[id].vue:80` renders `video.likeCount ?? 0` → still 0, forever, for every video on the instance. They then comment; `CommentSection target-type="video"` shows the comment, but line 81 keeps rendering `commentCount` 0. Worse, `layers/base/pages/videos/index.vue:102` offers "Sort: Most Liked", which reaches `listVideos` → `videos.likeCount` (video.ts:69-72) — every row is 0, so the order collapses to the `desc(videos.id)` tiebreaker and the sort silently does nothing. `features.video` defaults to true (packages/config/src/schema.ts:30), so this is live on every instance.

**Reachable input:** As any authenticated member on an instance with the default flags (`features.social` and `features.video` both default true), against any row in `videos` (e.g. created via `POST /api/videos` and visible at `/videos/<uuid>`): 1. `POST /api/social/like` with session cookie, body `{"targetType":"video","targetId":"<videos.id uuid>"}` → 200 `{"liked":true}`; a `likes` row is written, then `UPDATE content_items SET like_count = like_count + 1 WHERE id = '<videos.id uuid>'` matches 0 rows. `GET /api/videos/<uuid>` still returns `"likeCount": 0`, and `GET /api/videos?sort=liked` orders purely by `desc(videos.id)`. 2. `POST /api/social/comments` with session cookie, body `{"targetType":"video","targetId":"<videos.id uuid>","content":"nice build"}` → 200 with the comment; `canAccessCommentTarget` returns true for `'video'`, the `comments` row is written, then `UPDATE content_items SET comment_count = comment_count + 1 WHERE id = '<videos.id uuid>'` matches 0 rows. `GET /api/videos/<uuid>` still returns `"commentCount": 0`. Equivalent through the UI: open `/videos/<id>`, click the heart in the EngagementBar, then hard-reload the page — the heart stays filled (`isLiked` reads the `likes` table) but the number is 0 again.

**Suggested fix:** Add `case 'video':` branches to `updateLikeCount` and to `createComment`/`deleteComment`'s count update that target the `videos` table, and add `videos.like_count` / `videos.comment_count` tasks to reconcile-counters.mjs to repair the existing zeros.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading every link in the chain. 1. Route reachable: `layers/base/server/api/social/like.post.ts` gates only on `requireFeature('social')` (default true, packages/config/src/schema.ts:27) + `requireAuth`, then validates the body with `likeTargetTypeSchema`, which includes `'video'` (packages/schema/src/validators/social.ts:5-13). No existence check, no target-type/table cross-check. 2. `toggleLike` (social.ts:30-95) inserts the `likes` row, then calls `updateLikeCount`. The switch (social.ts:103-137) has cases only for `'comment'` and `'post'`; `default:` runs `UPDATE content_items SET like_count = like_count + 1 WHERE id = $targetId`. A video id is a `videos.id` uuid; `contentTypeEnum` (packages/schema/src/enums.ts:10-15) is only project/article/blog/explainer and `packages/server/src/video/video.ts:179` inserts into `videos` only, never mirroring into `content_items` — so the UPDATE matches 0 rows. No error, no data corruption, just a lost increment. 3. Same shape in `createComment` (social.ts:377-388): `targetType === 'post'` bumps `hub_posts.reply_count`, `else` bumps `content_items.comment_count` — with a `videos.id`. `canAccessCommentTarget` returns `true` unconditionally for `'video'` (social.ts:236, comment at :191 says "video/default has no privacy concept and is served") without verifying the row exists, so nothing blocks it. 4. Hunted for the guard and found none: `videos.likeCount` / `videos.commentCount` are declared NOT NULL default 0 (packages/schema/src/video.ts:69-70); `grep -rn "update(videos)"` over packages/layers/apps returns exactly one production site, `incrementVideoViewCount` at video.ts:280-284 (viewCount). The only other hits are `packages/server/src/__tests__/video.test.ts:154-156`, which sets `likeCount` by hand — that is why the "Most Liked" sort test is green while the write path is broken. No DB trigger, no reconcile entry: `scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs` covers only hub_posts.like_count, comments.like_count and content_items.like_count, and its content_items recompute explicitly filters `target_type IN ('project','article','blog','explainer')` — i.e. it deliberately excludes `'video'`, confirming video likes were never meant to land in content_items and confirming nothing repairs `videos.*`. 5. User-visible: `layers/base/pages/videos/[id].vue:80-81` binds `:like-count="video.likeCount ?? 0"` / `:comment-count="video.commentCount ?? 0"`, and `getVideo` returns those straight from the `videos` row (video.ts:150-151). `useEngagement` optimistically bumps the local ref (composables/useEngagement.ts:114-116) so the number moves until reload, then reverts to 0 permanently. `listVideos` sorts on `videos.likeCount` (video.ts:69-72) for the "Sort: Most Liked" option rendered at layers/base/pages/videos/index.vue:102 — with every row 0 the order collapses to the `desc(videos.id)` tiebreaker and the control silently does nothing. Partial correction to the finding's blast radius: the comment thread's own header count is `comments.length` (CommentSection.vue:142), so comments themselves display correctly; only the EngagementBar counter is stuck at 0. Likes are not lost either — the `likes` row persists and `isLiked` renders the filled heart correctly, so the state is recomputable at any time. That is why I am lowering P2 to P3: no security impact, no data loss, no failing request, no cross-table corruption (the stray UPDATE matches zero rows rather than incrementing a wrong row), and the whole feature is instance-local and never federated. The real damage is two permanently-zero display counters and one inert sort control on a default-on feature.

</details>

#### [P3] joinHub returns success and burns an invite use for a pending requester, but leaves them pending and uncounted

`packages/server/src/hub/members.ts:78`

`hub_members` has PRIMARY KEY (hub_id, user_id) and a `status` column defaulting to 'active' (packages/schema/src/hub.ts:65-79). `requestToJoinHub` inserts a row with `status:'pending'` and deliberately does not bump `memberCount`. `joinHub`'s success path inserts `{hubId, userId, role:'member'}` with `.onConflictDoNothing()` and, when `inserted.length === 0`, returns `{ joined: true }` without touching `status` and without bumping `memberCount`. A pending row conflicts on that PK, so the 'already a member, nothing to do' branch fires for someone who is not a member at all. `validateAndUseInvite` (packages/server/src/hub/moderation.ts:252-262) has already committed `useCount + 1` before this point.

**Failure:** Hub H has joinPolicy 'approval'. Alice hits Join, gets a pending row (members.ts:132-136). An admin, wanting to skip the queue, sends her an invite link. Alice POSTs /api/hubs/H/join with `{inviteToken}`; members.ts:60-62 validates the token — consuming one of a `maxUses: 1` invite's uses permanently — sets `inviteBypass`, and falls into the transaction. The insert conflicts with her pending row, `inserted.length === 0`, and the route returns `{joined: true}` so the UI shows her as a member. In the database she is still `status:'pending'`: `listMembers` filters `status='active'` (members.ts:359), `getMember` filters `status='active'` (members.ts:337) so every membership/authz check still refuses her, `memberCount` is unchanged, and she is still sitting in the owner's join-request queue. The same silent failure occurs whenever an owner flips joinPolicy from 'approval' to 'open' and a prior requester clicks Join again.

**Reachable input:** As an authenticated user Alice who already has a pending row on approval-policy hub H (created by an earlier `POST /api/hubs/H/join` with no body): `POST /api/hubs/H/join` with header Cookie: <Alice's session> and body `{"inviteToken":"<token from createInvite for hub H, maxUses 1>"}`. Route layers/base/server/api/hubs/[slug]/join.post.ts:14-16 passes the token to joinHub; members.ts:60-62 consumes the invite use and sets inviteBypass; the insert at 72-76 conflicts with her pending row; 78-80 returns {joined:true}. Post-state: hub_members row for (H, Alice) still status='pending', hub_invites.use_count incremented to its max so the invite is dead, and the caller was told she joined.

**Suggested fix:** On conflict, read the existing row's status: if it is 'pending' and the caller is entitled to join (open policy or a valid invite bypass), UPDATE it to 'active' inside the same transaction and bump memberCount; if it is already 'active', return joined:true as today. Also move the invite consumption after the membership decision so a no-op join doesn't burn a use.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The logic gap is real and I read it: joinHub's transaction (packages/server/src/hub/members.ts:71-80) inserts with .onConflictDoNothing() on the (hub_id, user_id) PK (packages/schema/src/hub.ts:78) and treats every conflict as "already an active member", returning {joined:true}. requestToJoinHub (members.ts:132-136) writes a row on that exact PK with status:'pending', so a pending requester conflicts and gets a false success with no status flip. validateAndUseInvite (packages/server/src/hub/moderation.ts:239-262) is an atomic UPDATE ... useCount + 1 ... RETURNING executed at members.ts:61 BEFORE any membership read, so the use is permanently consumed. createInvite (moderation.ts:192-206) has no join-policy check, so an approval hub can legitimately issue invites. No test in packages/server/src/__tests__/hub.integration.test.ts covers pending + invite bypass (the approval-workflow block at 296-380 only exercises the no-token path). Two corrections to the claim, which is why I cut the severity: (1) the "uncounted" framing is wrong — memberCount tracks active members by design and is bumped at approve time (members.ts:227), so not bumping it for a still-pending row is correct behaviour, not the bug; (2) the reference UI blocks the browser path — layers/base/pages/hubs/[slug]/index.vue:324 hides both "Join Hub" and "Accept invite" when hub.joinRequestPending is true, and packages/server/src/hub/hub.ts:287-289 sets that flag from row status regardless of join policy, so the described click-through (including the approval-to-open policy-flip variant) does not happen through the reference app. The defect is reached by a direct authenticated API call, or by a fork whose UI differs (the layer is a published package). Real damage is bounded: one permanently burned invite use plus a {joined:true} response the client toasts as "Joined hub!" while the user stays pending. No authz consequence (getMember at 337 and listMembers at 359 correctly still exclude them) and fully recoverable, since the row remains in the owner's queue and approveJoinRequest still activates it.

</details>

#### [P3] Federation activity cleanup never runs on an instance that redeploys more often than every 24 hours

`layers/base/server/plugins/federation-delivery.ts:54`

The daily cleanup is scheduled by an in-process tick counter: `cleanupCounter` starts at 0 on plugin init (line 12), increments once per delivery tick (line 53), and fires `cleanupDeliveredActivities` only when it reaches `Math.max(1, Math.round((24*60*60*1000) / (30_000)))` = 2880 (lines 54-61). Two problems. First, the divisor is the hardcoded literal `30_000`, not the `intervalMs` actually in use (computed at line 27 from `federation.deliveryIntervalMs`). Second, the counter is process-local with no persisted marker, so it resets to 0 on every boot.

**Failure:** An instance deploys, restarts, or gets OOM-killed more often than once every 2,880 delivery ticks. With the default 30s interval that is 24 hours of unbroken uptime — commonpub.io and deveco.io both deploy more often than that. `cleanupCounter` never reaches 2880, `cleanupDeliveredActivities` is never called, and `federation.activityRetentionDays` (default 90) is never enforced: every delivered/failed/dead-lettered outbound activity row, each carrying its full AP payload jsonb, accumulates in `activities` indefinitely. Separately, an operator who sets `deliveryIntervalMs: 300_000` to be gentle on remote hosts gets cleanup every 2880 x 5min = 10 days instead of daily; one who sets 5_000 gets it every 4 hours.

**Reachable input:** Not an HTTP input — a background job reachable via DB plus process state. Concretely: an instance with `features.federation: true` (as shipped in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:31 and apps/shell/commonpub.config.ts:20) that has `activities` rows with `status IN ('delivered','failed')` or `dead_lettered_at IS NOT NULL` and `created_at < now() - 90 days`, whose Nitro process is restarted (deploy, container replace, OOM kill) before completing 2,880 uninterrupted 30s delivery ticks. Those rows are never deleted: `cleanupCounter` resets to 0 at plugin init and nothing runs cleanup on boot. Second, interval-independent path with no uptime assumption: an operator sets `federation.deliveryIntervalMs: 300_000` in commonpub.config.ts; the timer at line 36 uses 300s but line 54 still computes `cleanupEvery = 2880`, so cleanup runs every 10 days instead of daily (5_000 gives every 4 hours). No live config in this repo sets a non-default `deliveryIntervalMs`, so today only the restart path is live.
