**Suggested fix:** Divide by `intervalMs` rather than the literal, and gate the cleanup on wall-clock time (a `lastCleanupAt` timestamp compared against `Date.now()`, or a persisted marker row) so it survives a restart instead of counting ticks.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Both halves verified by reading the code. (1) Line 54 divides 24h by the hardcoded literal `30_000` rather than the `intervalMs` resolved at line 27 from `federation.deliveryIntervalMs` (a real knob, `packages/config/src/schema.ts:273`), so the "daily" cadence is wrong for any non-default interval. (2) `cleanupCounter` is plugin-scope (line 12) with no persisted marker and, unlike every sibling worker in the same directory, no run-at-startup: `disclosure-purge.ts` does `void runPurge()` before `setInterval(..., 24h)` and `metrics-rollup.ts` does `runRollup()` before its interval, while federation-delivery's cleanup only fires after 2880 consecutive in-process ticks. No guard refutes it: `cleanupDeliveredActivities` has exactly one non-test caller (grep confirms), `features.federation: true` in both shipped configs, no test pins the counter (no hits for `cleanupEvery`/`2880` outside the source line), and the only other `delete(activities)` is a single-row Undo-Like handler at `packages/server/src/federation/inboxHandlers.ts:375`, so nothing else prunes the table against 50 insert sites. `git log -L 42,62` shows the block unchanged since its introducing commit 00790b98. Two claims in the finding I did not confirm and would drop: the deploy cadence of the live instances is asserted, not measured, so "never runs" is conditional on sub-24h uptime rather than absolute; and the cleanup covers inbound rows too, not only outbound. Neither weakens the defect.

</details>

#### [P3] learning_paths.completion_count is certificate-gated, never decremented, and reconciled from a different population

`packages/server/src/learning/learning.ts:783`

`completionCount` is incremented only inside the `existingCert.length === 0` branch of `markLessonComplete` — i.e. exactly once per (user, path), the first time a certificate is minted. `unenroll` (learning.ts:630-646) deletes the enrollment row and decrements `enrollmentCount` but never `completionCount`, and never removes the certificate. `scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs:103-108` recomputes the column as `count(enrollments WHERE completed_at IS NOT NULL)` — a third, different population — and the script is documented as 'Safe to run on production'.

**Failure:** A member finishes a path: `completedAt` set, certificate issued, `learning_paths.completion_count` = 1. They then call `POST /api/learn/:slug/unenroll` (layers/base/server/api/learn/[slug]/unenroll.post.ts). The enrollment row is deleted; `completion_count` stays 1 while zero enrollments carry a `completed_at`, so the path card advertises a completion that no longer exists. An operator runs `node scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs`, which rewrites it to 0 — discarding the certificate that is still in the `certificates` table. If the member re-enrols and re-completes, `existingCert.length !== 0` so the increment is skipped, yet `enrollments.completed_at` is set again, so the app value and the reconciler value now disagree in the opposite direction and every `--check` run reports permanent drift on that row.

**Reachable input:** 1) Authenticated user U (session cookie) enrolled in published path P (`POST /api/learn/{slug}/enroll`) marks every lesson complete via `POST /api/learn/{slug}/{lessonSlug}/complete` — certificate row created, `learning_paths.completion_count = 1`, `enrollments.completed_at` set. 2) Same user clicks Unenroll, i.e. `POST /api/learn/{slug}/unenroll` with no body as the enrolled learner (route: layers/base/server/api/learn/[slug]/unenroll.post.ts, requireAuth only) — enrollment row deleted, `completion_count` still 1, zero enrollments carry `completed_at`, certificate still present. 3) Operator follows docs/STATUS.md step 4 and runs `docker compose exec -T app node scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs --check` → reports drift on `learning_paths.completion_count`; running it without `--check` rewrites the column to 0, erasing a completion whose certificate is still in the `certificates` table and reducing the public `completions` metric.

**Suggested fix:** Pick one definition. Simplest: decrement `completionCount` in `unenroll` when the deleted row had a non-null `completedAt`, and move the increment out of the certificate branch to fire whenever `completedAt` transitions from null (guarded by the returning of that UPDATE), so the app and the reconciler agree.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified in source. `markLessonComplete` (packages/server/src/learning/learning.ts:770-787) increments `learning_paths.completion_count` only inside the `existingCert.length === 0` branch, while `enrollments.completed_at` is set unconditionally at 757-765 on completion — two different populations from the start. `unenroll` (learning.ts:628-646) deletes the enrollment and decrements only `enrollment_count`; the `certificates` row has no FK to `enrollments` (packages/schema/src/learning.ts: certificates references users + learning_paths only), so it survives and blocks any future increment. `scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs:103-108` recomputes the column as `count(enrollments WHERE completed_at IS NOT NULL)` — a third population — and the script is documented "Safe to run on production" and is step 4 of the deploy checklist in docs/STATUS.md:758-759, with `--check` telling the operator to "Run without --check to fix." Result: after a completed learner unenrolls, the stored count overstates; a reconcile run then rewrites it to 0, discarding a completion that still has a valid certificate, and the value is user-visible (layers/base/pages/learn/[slug]/index.vue:233) and publicly reported (packages/server/src/publicApi/metrics.ts:358 sums it as `completions`). No guard exists: the route is plain `requireAuth`, the UI exposes an Unenroll button (index.vue:151), `learning` is `true` in both apps/reference and apps/shell configs, no DB constraint or trigger touches the column, and the only unenroll test (packages/server/src/__tests__/learning.integration.test.ts:160-190) asserts nothing about counters. This is the same defect class the repo already fixed for likes in commit 4aab8302 ("so reconcile can't wipe fediverse likes"). One part of the claim is overstated and I do not endorse it: `--check` does not report "permanent drift" forever — the fixer is idempotent and converges each run; drift only reappears on the next app write. Severity is correctly P3: counter/metric inaccuracy only, no data loss (the certificate row persists), and it needs the learner's own unenroll.

</details>

#### [P3] Digest scheduler skips a whole day when the process restarts during hour 8 UTC

`layers/base/server/plugins/notification-email.ts:90`

The digest is driven purely by `setInterval(..., 60*60*1000)` registered 5 s after boot; `runDigest` is never called immediately the way `federation-delivery`, `metrics-rollup`, `persona-rollup`, `contest-reminders` and `disclosure-purge` all call their sweep once at startup. `runDigest` returns immediately unless `now.getUTCHours() === 8` (line 118/121). Ticks therefore land at a fixed offset within each hour, and hour 8 for the current day is only reached if a tick falls in the 08:00-08:59 UTC window after the process started.

**Failure:** A deploy (or a crash-restart) puts the process up at 08:30 UTC. The startup timer fires at 08:30:05 but only registers the interval; the first `runDigest` call is at 09:30:05, where `hour === 9` and the function returns at line 121. No other tick lands in hour 8 that day, so no `digest_runs` row for `todayKey` is ever attempted, no digest is enqueued for any user, and there is no catch-up: the next opportunity is 08:xx the following day. Every daily-digest subscriber silently loses that day's mail, and because the claim row was never inserted there is nothing in the database to tell an operator it happened. Cumulative timer lateness over long uptimes produces the same skip when the phase crosses an hour boundary.

**Reachable input:** On an instance with features.emailNotifications true (deveco today): a signed-in user saves PUT /api/profile {"emailNotifications":{"digest":"daily","likes":true,"comments":true,"follows":true,"mentions":true}} from /settings/notifications, producing a users row with email_notifications.digest = 'daily' and email_verified = true. Then any deploy or container restart (e.g. a push to main firing .github/workflows/deploy.yml) that brings the Nitro process up between 08:00:00 and 08:59:54 UTC. The startup timer fires at 08:xx:05 and only registers the interval; the first runDigest runs at 09:xx:05 where hour === 9 and returns at line 121. No tick lands in hour 8 for that date, so no digest_runs row for that todayKey is inserted and no digest email is enqueued for any subscriber that day, with no catch-up and no operator-visible record.

**Suggested fix:** Call `runDigest(...)` once immediately after registering the interval (the claim in `digest_runs` already makes it idempotent and multi-replica safe), and/or run the sweep every ~10 minutes with the hour check widened to 'hour >= 8 and not yet claimed for todayKey' so a missed tick still catches the day.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the whole plugin and every sibling worker. notification-email.ts:90-94 arms the hourly interval at boot+5s with no immediate runDigest() call, and runDigest returns at line 121 unless getUTCHours()===8, with the digest_runs claim (line 127) sitting downstream of that gate. Interval ticks are phase-locked to boot+5s, so a process whose boot+5s lands in [08:00:00,08:59:54] UTC has its first runDigest at hour 9 and never hits hour 8 that day. No catch-up exists: grep for digestRuns/digest_runs across the repo finds only this plugin, packages/schema/src/social.ts:138 and one concurrency test that only pins claim uniqueness; there is no admin trigger route, no .github cron, no external scheduler. Because the gate precedes the claim, no digest_runs row is written, so the skip is invisible to operators. The skipped day's notifications are also permanently excluded from the next digest, since line 161-166 sets since = now - 1 day and line 174 filters createdAt >= since. Every other worker does run its sweep once at startup (federation-delivery.ts:34, metrics-rollup.ts:25, persona-rollup.ts:53, contest-reminders.ts:31, disclosure-purge.ts:142), and nothing in the comments or git history marks this omission as deliberate. No guard refutes it: features.emailNotifications is on at deveco, the preference is user-settable, and the digest_runs unique claim would make an immediate startup run safely idempotent. Severity stays P3: it needs an opt-in preference, the flag on, and a restart landing in a 1-in-24 hour window, and the consequence is one missed day of digest email (notifications remain visible in-app), not data loss or a security issue.

</details>

#### [P3] Undo(Like) has no federated_hub_posts branch, so a remote unlike permanently inflates local_like_count

`packages/server/src/federation/inboxHandlers.ts:1303`

`onLike` has six increment targets, one of which is `federatedHubPosts.localLikeCount` matched by `objectUri` (1303-1308). `onUndo`'s Like branch (366-455) tries, in order: local hub post by URI pattern, local content by UUID, local content by author-scoped slug, an outbound Announce's wrapped Note, and finally `federatedContent` by `objectUri` — it never touches `federatedHubPosts`. A grep for `federatedHubPosts` in the file returns only lines 938-970, 1123-1127 and 1305-1308; nothing in the Undo path.

**Failure:** With `features.federateHubs` on, remote actor R sends Like(object = <mirrored remote hub post's objectUri>). No local hub post, no local content, no matching Announce match, so flow reaches 1303 and `federated_hub_posts.local_like_count` goes to 1. R then sends Undo(Like). onUndo deletes the `activities` ledger row (375-380) — permanently clearing the re-Like idempotency marker — walks every branch, matches none, and falls through to `UPDATE federated_content SET local_like_count = GREATEST(local_like_count - 1, 0) WHERE object_uri = <hub post uri>`, which matches zero rows. The mirrored post's like count is stuck at 1 with no like behind it, and because the ledger row was deleted, a subsequent re-Like from R increments it to 2. Neither column is in reconcile-counters.mjs, so nothing repairs it.

**Reachable input:** Precondition: instance A has features.federation and features.federateHubs on and mirrors hub H from instance B, so federated_hub_posts holds a row with object_uri U = https://b.example/hubs/h/posts/3f1c... (created by mirrorHubPost, hubMirroring.ts:555). Any remote actor R = https://r.example/users/r (self-hosted, no allowlist to pass) does: (1) POST https://a.example/inbox, HTTP-Signature signed by R's key, fresh Date, body {"@context":"https://www.w3.org/ns/activitystreams","id":"https://r.example/act/1","type":"Like","actor":"https://r.example/users/r","object":"https://b.example/hubs/h/posts/3f1c..."} -> onLike falls through to :1303 and federated_hub_posts.local_like_count goes 0 -> 1, plus an inbound Like ledger row. (2) POST the same inbox {"id":"https://r.example/act/2","type":"Undo","actor":"https://r.example/users/r","object":{"type":"Like","id":"https://b.example/hubs/h/posts/3f1c..."}} -> onUndo deletes the ledger row, matchHubPostUri matches the remote path, updates hub_posts by a uuid that does not exist locally (zero rows), sets unliked = true, and returns; local_like_count stays 1. Repeating step 1 with a new activity id (id must differ to clear recordActivitySeen replay dedup) raises it to 2, 3, ... unbounded. Even without the crafted inner id, an honest remote Like followed by an honest Undo leaves the count stuck at 1, displayed to viewers of /federated-hubs/{id}/posts/{postId} as likeCount = local_like_count + remote_like_count, with zero federated_hub_post_likes rows behind it.

**Suggested fix:** Add a `federatedHubPosts` decrement branch to onUndo, mirroring onLike's ordering (attempt it before the federatedContent fallback and set `unliked` from its `.returning()` length).

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The asymmetry is real and unguarded. onLike increments federatedHubPosts.localLikeCount by objectUri (inboxHandlers.ts:1300-1308) and onUndo's Like branch (:366-455) contains no federatedHubPosts statement — grep confirms the only references in the file are 938-970, 1123-1127, 1305-1308. Nothing blocks it: the shared inbox (layers/base/server/routes/inbox.ts) requires only features.federation plus a valid HTTP signature from any resolvable remote actor — there is no domain allowlist or blocklist anywhere (grep for blocklist/blockedDomain returns nothing), onLike has no feature-flag or federationConfig gate, and federateHubs is ON on the live instances per hubFederation.ts:91 and hub-privacy-p2.integration.test.ts:124. The drifted column is user-visible (federated-hubs/[id]/posts/[postId].vue:27 renders localLikeCount + remoteLikeCount) and is not in scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs (11 tasks, none on federated_hub_posts, though hub_posts.like_count IS reconciled), so nothing repairs it. Git blame shows the increment landed in 787c5695 with no Undo counterpart, and the exact same class of bug was fixed for content in the session-204 audit (inbox-handlers.integration.test.ts:366 comment: "was a no-op ... permanently inflating the count"), so this is oversight, not intent. No test pins the Undo behaviour for this table. Two corrections to the reported mechanism: (1) the Undo does NOT normally reach the federatedContent fallback — matchHubPostUri (inboxParsing.ts:46-59) is host-agnostic and, unlike onLike:1257, is called at onUndo:386 WITHOUT the isLocalUri gate, so a mirrored URI of shape /hubs/{slug}/posts/{uuid} matches, updates hub_posts by a non-local uuid (zero rows) and sets unliked = true, short-circuiting the rest; same outcome, different path. (2) processInboxActivity (packages/protocol/src/inbox.ts:66-77) passes inner.id — the Like ACTIVITY id — as objectId, and hubMirroring.ts:870-895 builds outbound Undos with object: likeAct.payload whose id is `${localActorUri}/likes/<uuid>`, so a well-behaved CommonPub peer's Undo(Like) matches no ledger row and does nothing at all; the count still stays permanently inflated, but the reporter's "re-Like climbs to 2" escalation needs a sender that puts the liked object URI in the inner `id`, which is sender-controlled and passes every inbox check but is not what an honest peer emits.

</details>

### The write surface

#### [P2] Saving notification preferences silently reverses a one-click email unsubscribe

`packages/server/src/profile/profile.ts:183`

`updateUserProfile` REPLACES the whole `users.email_notifications` jsonb (`updates.emailNotifications = input.emailNotifications`) rather than merging. `/settings/notifications` posts only five keys — `likes, comments, follows, mentions, digest` — and never `unsubscribedAll` (notifications.vue:32-38). `unsubscribedAll` is the single global suppression flag: `emailPrefs.ts:52` and `:90` return false/null on it, `broadcast.ts:35` and `contest/reminders.ts:204` filter on it. The unsubscribe route itself is careful to merge ("Merge so other notification toggles aren't clobbered", unsubscribe.post.ts:37-42); the profile PUT undoes that.

**Failure:** A member clicks List-Unsubscribe in a contest reminder. `POST /api/unsubscribe` stores `{digest:'none', unsubscribedAll:true}`. Weeks later they open /settings/notifications to check their settings. The page seeds from the stored object, but `likes/comments/follows/mentions` are absent there, so the four toggles render at their `ref(true)` defaults (notifications.vue:9-23) and nothing on the page indicates they are unsubscribed from everything. They press Save. The PUT body is `{emailNotifications:{likes:true,comments:true,follows:true,mentions:true,digest:'none'}}`, which replaces the column — `unsubscribedAll` is gone. The member is now re-subscribed to every instant notification email and to admin broadcasts, having taken an action they would read as 'confirming my settings'. Same result for any member who edits one toggle.

**Reachable input:** Authenticated member M on an instance with `features.emailNotifications` on and a real (non-console) email adapter. 1. M receives any instance email and clicks its RFC 8058 one-click link: `POST /api/unsubscribe?token=<valid HMAC over M's user id>` (no body). unsubscribe.post.ts:42 writes `users.email_notifications = {digest:'none', unsubscribedAll:true}`. 2. Later, M opens `/settings/notifications` (auth middleware, own session) and presses Save. The page issues, with M's own session cookie: PUT /api/profile {"emailNotifications":{"likes":true,"comments":true,"follows":true,"mentions":true,"digest":"none"}} 3. profile.put.ts passes it straight to `updateUserProfile`, which at profile.ts:183 assigns that literal over the whole jsonb column. `unsubscribedAll` no longer exists, so `emailPrefs.ts:52/:90`, `broadcast.ts:35`, `contest/reminders.ts` and `notification-email.ts:158` all treat M as mailable again. The same PUT can be issued directly with curl; no admin rights, no second user, no flag on the write path itself.

**Suggested fix:** Merge in `updateUserProfile`: `updates.emailNotifications = { ...current, ...input.emailNotifications }`, reading the current row first. Surface the unsubscribed-all state on /settings/notifications with an explicit 'Resubscribe' control so clearing it is always a deliberate act.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Survives, with severity corrected to P2. What I verified by reading the code: - `packages/server/src/profile/profile.ts:183` is exactly `if (input.emailNotifications !== undefined) updates.emailNotifications = input.emailNotifications;`, inside a field-by-field `updates` builder that ends in a single `db.update(users).set(updates)`. `users.email_notifications` is a nullable jsonb, so Drizzle replaces the whole document. No read-modify-write anywhere in the function. - Its declared param type (profile.ts:151-157) lists only `digest/likes/comments/follows/mentions` — it does not even model `unsubscribedAll`, so nothing in the function is aware there is a key to preserve. - The only caller is `layers/base/server/api/profile.put.ts`: `requireAuth(event)` then `parseBody(event, updateProfileSchema)` then `updateUserProfile(db, user.id, input)`. Owner-only, no feature flag, no other guard. The validator (`packages/schema/src/validators/auth.ts:83-92`) does accept `unsubscribedAll` as an optional boolean, so it is droppable-not-required, exactly as claimed. - `layers/base/pages/settings/notifications.vue` seeds from `/api/profile` but its inline DTO type omits `unsubscribedAll`, there is no UI control for it, and `handleSave` (lines 32-38) reconstructs a fresh 5-key literal. Confirmed verbatim. - The setter side is careful the other way: `unsubscribe.post.ts:37-42` spreads `...current` with the comment "Merge so other notification toggles aren't clobbered", and `unsubscribe-route.test.ts:24` pins `unsubscribedAll: true`. So the profile PUT genuinely undoes a deliberate merge. - Consumers of the flag are real and unguarded downstream: `emailPrefs.ts:52` (`if (prefs.unsubscribedAll) return false`), `emailPrefs.ts:90` (returns null from `getNotificationEmailTarget`, documented as "the single mailability gate"), `comms/broadcast.ts:35` raw-SQL `->> 'unsubscribedAll' IS DISTINCT FROM 'true'`, `contest/reminders.ts` claim SQL, `plugins/notification-email.ts:158`. Losing the key re-enables every one of them. Guards I hunted for and did NOT find: no merge helper, no DB trigger, no jsonb `||` update, no zod refinement requiring the key, no test anywhere asserting a profile save preserves it (`grep updateUserProfile` across all `*.test.ts` returns nothing; there is no `packages/server/src/profile/__tests__/`). Git history shows the assignment predates the unsubscribe feature (session 227 added `unsubscribedAll` to an existing whole-object write), so this is an oversight, not a deliberate design. Not new knowledge, but still unfixed: `docs/plans/persona-customization-and-audience-analytics.md:50` already logs this exact defect and line 1463 lists "fix the emailNotifications whole-object clobber ... to a merge" as pending work. Documented-and-unfixed is still a defect. Why P2, not P1: - The actor is the account owner acting on their own row. No privilege escalation, no cross-user reach, no data disclosure — the harm is that the instance resumes mailing someone who opted out (a compliance/CAN-SPAM/GDPR problem, not a security one). - The precondition `unsubscribedAll === true` is only reachable by presenting a valid HMAC token, which only exists inside a delivered email (`comms/unsubscribe.ts:38` builds the `List-Unsubscribe` header). `features.emailNotifications` defaults false (`packages/config/src/schema.ts:99`) and is ON only on deveco, where `useEmailAdapter()` (layers/base/server/utils/email.ts) resolves to `ConsoleEmailAdapter` unless `NUXT_EMAIL_ADAPTER` is set — so on today's instances no member holds a token and the state cannot yet be reached. That is deployment configuration, not a code guard, and it flips the moment an operator sets SMTP/Resend. - Two minor corrections to the finding's wording, neither fatal: the cited line is 183 at HEAD (the plan doc's 169 is stale), and `/api/profile` does return `unsubscribedAll` in the raw JSON (profile.ts:100 casts the whole jsonb) — it is the page's narrower inline type and its 5-key rebuild that drop it, not the API.

</details>

#### [P2] No content metadata field can ever be cleared: the editor rewrites every emptied field to undefined

`layers/base/composables/useContentSave.ts:89`

`buildSaveBody` runs `if (body[key] === '' || body[key] === null) body[key] = undefined;` over the whole metadata object before every save. `undefined` is dropped by JSON serialization, so an emptied field is indistinguishable from an unmentioned one. `updateContent` applies a field only when it is `!== undefined` (content.ts:788-808 and `applyContentPassthroughUpdates` at :716-722). The server-side schema cannot rescue it either: `coverImageUrl`/`bannerUrl` are `optionalUrl()` and `seoDescription`/`estimatedMinutes` have preprocesses that map `''`/`null` to `undefined` (validators/content.ts:34-43). Net effect: `description`, `subtitle`, `coverImageUrl`, `bannerUrl`, `seoDescription`, `series`, `licenseType`, `buildTime`, `estimatedCost`, `category`, `difficulty`, `categoryId`, `estimatedMinutes` are write-once-per-value and can only be replaced, never emptied. The in-code comment shows this was a deliberate workaround for `null` producing an opaque 400 — the workaround traded a visible error for a silent no-op.

**Failure:** An author publishes a project with the wrong cover image. `ProjectEditor.vue:250` (and `ArticleEditor.vue:475`) offer a trash "Remove" button that calls `removeCover()` → `updateMeta('coverImageUrl','')`. The editor preview clears, Save reports success, and the PUT body simply has no `coverImageUrl` key. Reload the page and the wrong cover is back — on the public page, in feed cards, and in the federated Article's attachment. Identically, an author who wants to drop a stale SEO description or an inaccurate 'estimated cost' cannot; the only escape is to overwrite with a placeholder string.

**Reachable input:** Signed-in author of an existing project (any instance, no flag involved). Go to /u/{username}/project/{slug}/edit, click the trash "Remove" button under the cover image (ProjectEditor.vue:250 -> removeCover() -> updateMeta('coverImageUrl','')), then click Save. The browser issues: PUT /api/content/{content-uuid} {"type":"project","title":"My build","content":[["paragraph",{"html":"hi"}]], ...} with NO coverImageUrl key at all — buildSaveBody (useContentSave.ts:89) maps '' -> undefined and ofetch's JSON.stringify drops undefined keys. The route returns 200 and updateContent's applyContentPassthroughUpdates (packages/server/src/content/content.ts:716-722) skips the field because it is undefined, so the row keeps the old cover. Reload the page or view the public/feed/federated version and the old cover is still there. There is no request body that clears it: PUT /api/content/{id} {"coverImageUrl":""} is preprocessed to undefined by optionalUrl() (validators/_shared.ts:21-26) and skipped identically, and PUT /api/content/{id} {"coverImageUrl":null} is rejected 400 by updateContentSchema (no field is .nullable()). Same for bannerUrl, seoDescription, estimatedMinutes, and (via the client rewrite alone) description, subtitle, buildTime, estimatedCost, series, licenseType, category.

**Suggested fix:** Make the clearable content fields `.nullable()` in `updateContentSchema`, have `buildSaveBody` map `''` → `null` (not `undefined`) for those fields, and have `updateContent` write `null` through. Keep the `undefined` coercion only for fields that genuinely have no empty state.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified end to end by reading the code, not the summary. useContentSave.ts:75-99 rewrites '' and null to undefined over the entire body (git 4d486147 added the null half in session 251; the '' half dates to the layer's first commit 00790b98). ofetch JSON-serializes, dropping those keys. layers/base/server/api/content/[id]/index.put.ts does a straight parseBody(updateContentSchema) -> updateContent with no patch/merge semantics. updateContent applies passthrough fields only when !== undefined (content.ts:716-722, CONTENT_PASSTHROUGH_FIELDS at :700-703). No server-side rescue exists: optionalUrl() (validators/_shared.ts:21-26) maps '' to undefined itself, seoDescription/estimatedMinutes have the same preprocess (validators/content.ts:34-43), and no content field is .nullable(), so null 400s. I searched for a guard and found none: no alternate cover-removal endpoint (layers/base/server/api/content/[id]/ has no such route), no feature flag, no DB constraint, no server test covering clearing (packages/server/src/__tests__/content.integration.test.ts has zero coverImageUrl assertions). The only relevant test, layers/base/composables/__tests__/useContentSave.test.ts, pins the drop behaviour ("coverImageUrl must not be sent") — it protects the session-251 400 fix and vacuously locks in the no-op. The trash Remove buttons are real and wired: ProjectEditor.vue:116-117/136-137 with buttons at :250, :339, :368; ArticleEditor.vue:216-217/233-234 with buttons at :475, :504. Two corrections to the finding: (a) the affected-field list is slightly off — `category` and `visibility` also go through the client rewrite but were omitted, while `tags: []` and numeric `estimatedMinutes` genuinely survive; (b) removing the client line alone fixes only description/subtitle/buildTime/estimatedCost/series/licenseType/category — coverImageUrl/bannerUrl/seoDescription/estimatedMinutes additionally need the server schemas made .nullable(), because their preprocesses strip '' independently. Severity corrected P1 -> P2: it is a user-visible control that reports success and silently does nothing, on every instance with no flag gate, and it does reach the public page and the federated Article attachment — but it requires authenticated ownership, destroys no data, exposes nothing, crashes nothing, and has a workaround (overwrite the field with a different value).

</details>

#### [P2] Dynamic OAuth client registration hands an existing client's client_secret to any unauthenticated caller

`packages/server/src/federation/oauth.ts:415`

`POST /api/auth/oauth2/register` is deliberately unauthenticated ("zero-friction instance-to-instance setup"). `processDynamicRegistration` treats `instance_domain` from the request BODY as an idempotency key: it selects the existing `oauth_clients` row for that domain and, if one exists, returns `{ clientId: existing.clientId, clientSecret: existing.clientSecret }`. There is no proof that the caller controls the domain — no signature, no callback to the domain, no shared secret, nothing. `instance_domain` is validated only as `z.string().min(3).max(255)` (register.post.ts:8), i.e. an arbitrary string. The secret it hands back is the credential `validateTokenRequest` timing-safe-compares at the token endpoint (packages/protocol/src/oauth.ts:90), so it is the relying party's password.

**Failure:** Federation is enabled on commonpub.io/deveco.io. An attacker sends `POST /api/auth/oauth2/register` with `{"client_name":"x","redirect_uris":["https://attacker.example/cb"],"instance_domain":"deveco.io"}` — a domain already registered as an SSO partner (partner domains are public: they appear in the federation UI and in nodeinfo peers). The 200 response body contains deveco.io's real `client_id` AND `client_secret`. The attacker now authenticates as that relying party at `POST /api/auth/oauth2/token`, so any authorization code they can observe or induce (the code is delivered to one of the partner's already-registered redirect URIs) is exchangeable for `{access_token, user}` — the victim's identity record including email. Secondary defect on the same line: a legitimate instance that re-registers to ADD a redirect URI gets the old row back and its new `redirect_uris` are silently discarded, so its new callback never works and nothing says why.

**Reachable input:** Unauthenticated, no cookie, no session, against any instance with `features.federation: true` (verified true on commonpub.io) whose `oauth_clients` table already holds a row for the named domain (created either by a prior dynamic registration or by an admin `POST /api/admin/federation/clients`): POST https://commonpub.io/api/auth/oauth2/register Content-Type: application/json {"client_name":"x","redirect_uris":["https://attacker.example/cb"],"instance_domain":"deveco.io"} -> 200 {"client_id":"cpub_<deveco.io's real id>","client_secret":"cpubs_<deveco.io's real secret>","registration_type":"dynamic"} I confirmed the route is live and ungated by sending the same shape with a too-short domain and getting the zod 400 ("instance_domain: Too small: expected string to have >=3 characters"), i.e. the request reached the handler body with no auth. I deliberately did not fire the real payload, because on the not-yet-registered path it INSERTS a junk row into the production DB. The DB precondition (a row for the guessed domain) is the only thing I could not verify from outside; with no such row the same request instead silently squats that domain, and the legitimate instance later gets back attacker-known credentials plus its own redirect URIs discarded.

**Suggested fix:** Never return a secret for a pre-existing client. On a repeat registration either 409, or rotate and return the secret only over a channel that proves domain control (e.g. require the caller to first serve a nonce at `https://<instance_domain>/.well-known/...` and verify it server-side before returning credentials). Separately, apply the submitted `redirect_uris` on re-registration instead of dropping them.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The mechanism is real and I could not find any guard. `processDynamicRegistration` (packages/server/src/federation/oauth.ts:401-422) selects `oauth_clients` by `eq(oauthClients.instanceDomain, request.instanceDomain)` and returns `{ clientId: existing.clientId, clientSecret: existing.clientSecret }` with no proof of domain control; the route (layers/base/server/api/auth/oauth2/register.post.ts) echoes both into the 200 body as `client_id` / `client_secret`, has only `requireFeature('federation')` and no `requireAuth`, and the auth middleware explicitly classifies `/api/auth/oauth2/` as a custom route it does not gate (layers/base/server/middleware/auth.ts:201). `validateDynamicRegistration` (packages/protocol/src/oauth.ts:120-148) checks only clientName length, redirect-URI scheme and `instanceDomain.length >= 3`. `isDomainTrusted` exists but is wired only into the OUTBOUND flow (layers/base/server/api/auth/federated/login.post.ts:22), never into registration. `instance_domain` has no unique constraint (packages/schema/src/auth.ts:135-142), so the row is also squattable. The returned secret is exactly what `validateTokenRequest` timing-safe-compares (packages/protocol/src/oauth.ts:88-92). I verified reachability against production: `federation: true` in commonpub.io's `/api/features`, and an unauthenticated `POST https://commonpub.io/api/auth/oauth2/register` reached the zod validator (400 "instance_domain: Too small") rather than any auth/CSRF/rate gate. The existing test only pins that the same clientId comes back; nothing pins that the secret must not be re-disclosed. Two corrections to the claim, which is why I downgrade severity. (1) The token endpoint returns `{id, username, displayName, avatarUrl, actorUri}` (oauth.ts:154-165) — there is NO email in the payload, so "the victim's identity record including email" is false. (2) The claimed takeover needs a further step the finding hand-waves: an authorization code is bound to clientId + a redirect_uri already on the row (processTokenExchange -> consumeAuthCode, oauth.ts:134), and the idempotent path DISCARDS the caller's new redirect_uris, so the attacker cannot add their own callback to an existing partner's row. Codes therefore still land on the legitimate partner's callback; the stolen secret alone yields client impersonation at the token endpoint, and identity theft only if a code also leaks (referrer, logs, an open redirect on the partner). The secondary defect (a partner re-registering to add a redirect URI silently gets the old row and its new URIs dropped, with no error) is likewise real on the same line. So: an unauthenticated internet caller who guesses a partner domain gets that relying party's long-lived password. Credential disclosure, not direct account takeover — P2.

</details>

#### [P2] POST /api/hubs writes an unauthorized parentHubId, attaching a hub under someone else's company hub

`packages/server/src/hub/hub.ts:392`

`createHubSchema.parentHubId` is `z.string().uuid().optional()` and `createHub` writes it verbatim (`parentHubId: input.parentHubId ?? null`). Nothing checks that the parent exists, that the caller has any role in it, or that the parent is a hub type for which children are meaningful. Hub creation is open to every authenticated user (`hubs/index.post.ts` is `requireAuth` only; there is no creation policy in the config schema). The value is load-bearing: for a `company` hub, the product gallery aggregates products from every child hub (`product.ts:690-696` selects `hubs.parentHubId === hubId` and unions the product ids). `updateHubSchema` also accepts `parentHubId` but `updateHub` (hub.ts:449-461) never applies it, so an owner cannot even detach a child that was attached under them.

**Failure:** An attacker reads a company hub's id from `GET /api/hubs/<company-slug>` and posts `{"name":"Free Crypto Kits","parentHubId":"<company-hub-id>"}`. Their hub is now a child of the victim company hub, and every product they add surfaces in that company's `GET /api/hubs/<company-slug>/gallery`, attributed to the company's own surface. The victim's owner has no route to remove it: `updateHub` ignores `parentHubId`. Separately, `{"parentHubId":"00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000"}` is shape-valid but violates the self-referencing FK (`hub.ts:47 references(() => hubs.id)`), so the insert throws an unhandled error and the caller gets a bare 500 rather than a 400 naming the field.

**Reachable input:** Any authenticated user with an ordinary account (session cookie, plus `Origin: https://<host>` to satisfy layers/base/server/middleware/csrf.ts — the attacker sets it themselves, it is not a barrier for a deliberate actor). No feature flag gates POST /api/hubs (layers/base/server/middleware/features.ts maps only page prefixes, and /hubs is not among them; the route itself has no requireFeature). Concrete sequence against a public `company` hub `acme-corp`: 1. `GET /api/hubs/acme-corp` -> returns real `id` (getHubBySlug redacts the id to REDACTED_HUB_ID only for `privacy === 'private'` non-members; a public company hub returns the real uuid). Call it `ACME`. 2. `POST /api/hubs` `{"name":"Free Crypto Kits","hubType":"product","parentHubId":"<ACME>"}` -> 200. Route is `requireAuth` + `parseBody(createHubSchema)` only; `createHubSchema.parentHubId` is `z.string().uuid().optional()` (packages/schema/src/validators/hub.ts:20) and createHub writes `parentHubId: input.parentHubId ?? null` (packages/server/src/hub/hub.ts:392) with no existence check, no role check on the parent, no hubType check. The attacker is auto-inserted as `owner` of the new hub. 3. `POST /api/hubs/free-crypto-kits/products` `{"name":"Kit"}` -> allowed; createProduct (packages/server/src/product/product.ts:64-90) only requires an active membership in that hub, which the attacker owns. 4. `POST /api/content/<attacker's published content id>/products` `{"productId":"<kit id>"}` -> allowed; the route checks only that the caller authored the content. 5. `GET /api/hubs/acme-corp/gallery` (public, unauthenticated) now returns the attacker's content: listHubGallery's company branch selects `hubs where parentHubId = hubId` with no filter on hubType, privacy or ownership, unions the products of those child hubs, and lists the linked content (product.ts:687-700). Remediation gap also confirmed: `updateHub` (hub.ts:407-461) neither accepts nor applies `parentHubId`, and grep shows `parentHubId` appears in exactly five non-test files repo-wide — no other route, no admin route, and no UI writes it (layers/base/pages/hubs/create.vue posts only name/description/iconUrl/bannerUrl/hubType/joinPolicy). So the victim owner, and even a platform admin using the admin hub-edit path, cannot detach the child; only direct DB access or the attacker deleting their own hub clears it. Secondary claim also holds: `POST /api/hubs {"name":"x","parentHubId":"00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000"}` is shape-valid, hits the self-referencing FK (packages/schema/src/hub.ts:47), and nothing maps Postgres 23503 anywhere in layers/base/server or packages/server, so the caller gets a bare 500 instead of a 400 naming the field.

**Suggested fix:** Before writing `parentHubId`, load the parent and require the creator to hold `editHub` in it (or drop the field from the public create schema entirely and expose parenting through an owner-side endpoint on the PARENT hub). Add the corresponding branch to `updateHub` so a parent can detach a child.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

I read the whole createHub/updateHub unit, both routes, the validator, the FK declaration, the gallery consumer, and the git history, and found no guard anywhere on the path. The route (layers/base/server/api/hubs/index.post.ts) is `requireAuth` + `parseBody` with no flag and no permission check; the validator accepts any uuid; createHub writes it verbatim. Git history shows the write was added incidentally in b46c1e7a ("persist dropped create/update fields", a sweep for fields the UI sends) — but the UI never sends parentHubId, so this is an unintended API-only write, not deliberate design. `grep -rn parentHubId --include=*.test.ts` returns nothing: no test pins any behaviour here, in either direction. The single consumer, listHubGallery's company branch, trusts the edge completely (no hubType/privacy/ownership filter on children), so the write is load-bearing and the injected content surfaces on a public unauthenticated endpoint. Severity stays P2, not higher: the impact is integrity/spoofing on one listing surface (the attacker's own already-public content appears under someone else's company gallery), not disclosure of victim data and not privilege escalation — the attacker gains no role in the parent hub, and the parent's own products/members are untouched. It needs an account, several steps, and a `company`-type hub to exist on the instance. What keeps it at P2 rather than P3 is that there is no in-product removal path: `updateHub` ignores the field, so cleanup requires direct DB access. The bare-500 FK case is real but on its own would be P3.

</details>

#### [P3] PUT /api/events/:slug accepts isFeatured from the event's creator, so any member can feature their own event

`packages/server/src/events/events.ts:252`

The route computes `isAdmin = hasPermission(event, 'event.manage')` and passes the parsed body straight to `updateEvent`, whose authorization is `if (!isAdmin && existing[0].createdById !== userId) return null` — i.e. the plain owner passes. `isFeatured` is then applied with no further check: `if (data.isFeatured !== undefined) updates.isFeatured = data.isFeatured;`. `isFeatured` is an editorial placement flag: `listEvents` filters on it (`events.ts:110`), `/events?featured=true` is a first-class tab (events/index.vue:85), and `EventCard.vue:52` renders a "Featured" badge. Event creation itself is open to every authenticated user by explicit design (the long comment at events/index.post.ts:28-39).

**Failure:** Any member creates an event, then sends `PUT /api/events/<their-slug>` with `{"isFeatured": true}` — or simply ticks the unconditional "Featured Event" checkbox the edit page shows every owner (events/[slug]/edit.vue:138), which posts the same field at :62. Their event now appears in the Featured tab with a Featured badge, indistinguishable from operator-curated placement. Repeat per event to occupy the whole featured rail.

**Reachable input:** On an instance where the operator has enabled `features.events` (admin Features toggle; default false), as any ordinary authenticated member with no roles: 1) `POST /api/events` with `{"title":"My Meetup","startDate":"2026-09-01T18:00:00.000Z","endDate":"2026-09-01T20:00:00.000Z"}` — permitted for every logged-in user by explicit design (index.post.ts:28-39) — returns the created event with slug `my-meetup`. 2) `PUT /api/events/my-meetup` with `{"isFeatured": true}` as that same member. `hasPermission(event,'event.manage')` is false, but `existing[0].createdById === userId`, so the owner branch passes and `updates.isFeatured = true` is written. 3) `GET /api/events?featured=true` (unauthenticated) now returns that event, and it renders with the star "Featured" badge on /events and on the event page. Step 2 also happens with no crafted request at all: the edit page at /events/my-meetup/edit shows every owner an ungated "Featured Event" checkbox that posts the same field.

**Suggested fix:** Gate the field, not the row: `if (isAdmin && data.isFeatured !== undefined) updates.isFeatured = data.isFeatured;` and hide the checkbox behind `isAdmin` in edit.vue. Mirror the pattern already used for `contentItems.isFeatured`, which is only settable through the permission-gated `admin/content/bulk-editorial.post.ts`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The code path is exactly as claimed and I found no guard anywhere. The PUT zod schema accepts `isFeatured`, the route forwards the whole parsed body, and `updateEvent`'s sole authorization check (`if (!isAdmin && existing[0]!.createdById !== userId) return null`) is satisfied by the plain creator, after which `if (data.isFeatured !== undefined) updates.isFeatured = data.isFeatured` runs with no admin condition. The value is publicly consequential: `listEvents` filters on it, `/api/events?featured=true` backs a first-class "Featured" tab, and two components render a Featured badge. The norm elsewhere in the codebase confirms featuring is an editorial act: content `isFeatured` is writable ONLY through `/api/admin/content/[id].patch.ts` and `/api/admin/content/bulk-editorial.post.ts`, and there is no admin events curation route at all, so the owner PUT is the only write path to `events.is_featured`. No test pins the behaviour (the events `__tests__` dir holds only `[slug].get.test.ts`), no DB constraint restricts it, and the schema column is a plain nullable-free boolean. Two corrections to the finding: (1) it omits that `features.events` defaults to FALSE in every in-repo config (`layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:119`, `composables/useFeatures.ts:133`, `packages/test-utils/src/mockConfig.ts:31`) and `requireFeature('events')` 404s the entire route family when off, so this only bites instances whose operator has turned events on; (2) intent is partly ambiguous — the owner-visible "Featured Event" checkbox at `pages/events/[slug]/edit.vue:138` was added in the same commit as the field (`09b47b4c`), so an operator could read owner-set featuring as designed. It still fails as shipped, because the public UI presents the Featured tab as curation while any member can place themselves in it. Severity drops to P3: flag off by default, authenticated-only, no data exposure, no privilege escalation, impact limited to placement spam in one tab.

</details>

#### [P3] Website, avatar and banner can never be removed from a profile: the route coerces the cleared value to undefined

`layers/base/server/api/profile.put.ts:12`

The route post-processes the parsed body with `avatarUrl: raw.avatarUrl || undefined, bannerUrl: raw.bannerUrl || undefined, website: raw.website || undefined` (comment: "avoids <img src=\"\">"). `updateUserProfile` applies a field only when it is `!== undefined` (profile.ts:174-176). The schema half already does the same thing: `optionalUrl()` preprocesses `''` to `undefined` (validators/_shared.ts:21-26). So there is no representable request that clears any of the three. Note the asymmetry that proves it is a bug rather than a policy: `socialLinks` is a whole-object replace, so clearing a social link DOES work — only the three scalar URL columns are stuck.

**Failure:** A member on /settings/profile/links clears the Website field (it is a plain editable text input, links.vue:145) and saves. `body.website` is `''` → schema preprocess → `undefined` → route `|| undefined` → skipped. The save reports success. On reload the old URL is back on their public profile and in their federated actor document. A member who wants their personal site removed from a public profile — a plausible erasure request under GDPR Art. 17, and one the UI appears to accept — has no way to do it, and no error tells them why.

**Reachable input:** Authenticated member (any session; no special role needed), from /settings/profile/links with the Website field emptied and Save clicked, which issues: PUT /api/profile Content-Type: application/json Cookie: <the member's own session> {"website": "", "socialLinks": {"github": "https://github.com/me"}} Response is 200 with the profile DTO still containing the OLD website, and the page shows "Links updated". Reload /settings/profile/links and the old URL is back; it also remains on the public profile and in `/api/public/v1/users/:username`. The same request with `{"avatarUrl": ""}` or `{"bannerUrl": ""}` is likewise a no-op (API-only — the Basics tab has no remove control for either image). `{"website": null}` is NOT an escape hatch: it fails `optionalUrl` and returns 400.

**Suggested fix:** Make `website`/`avatarUrl`/`bannerUrl` `.nullable()` in `updateProfileSchema`, drop the `|| undefined` coercions in the route, and let `updateUserProfile` write `null`. Keep the empty-string guard at RENDER time (`:src="url || undefined"`), which is where the `<img src="">` concern actually belongs.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The outcome is real and I verified every link in the chain by reading it. `optionalUrl` (packages/schema/src/validators/_shared.ts:21-26) maps `''`/whitespace to `undefined`, and rejects `null` outright (preprocess passes non-strings through to `z.string().url()`, which throws) — so no request body can express "clear". `updateUserProfile` (packages/server/src/profile/profile.ts:174-176) then applies each of website/avatarUrl/bannerUrl only when `!== undefined`. `updateUserProfile` is the sole writer of `users.website` (grepped all of packages/server/src; every other hit is a serializer, the export, or the unrelated `hub.website`), and `layers/base/server/api/profile/` holds only `theme.put.ts`, so there is no alternate clearing route. The save returns 200 and links.vue fires `toast.success('Links updated')`, so the failure is silent. Two corrections to the finding. (1) The cited cause is wrong: the route's `avatarUrl/bannerUrl/website: raw.X || undefined` at profile.put.ts:10-15 is REDUNDANT — the schema already produced `undefined` before the route ran. Deleting those three lines fixes nothing; the defect lives in the schema preprocess plus the `!== undefined` writer guard. (2) It is a documented, deliberate pre-existing limitation, not an oversight: layers/base/pages/settings/profile/basics.vue:18-22 states verbatim that clearing back to empty is impossible for bio, headline, location and pronouns by this exact mechanism and "is deliberately not fixed here". So the real class is wider than the three URL columns, and this was a known carry-over from the pre-split form. Severity down to P3, not P2. No security, authz, availability or data-loss impact; the member is authenticated and acting on their own row. The privacy framing is overstated: the member can overwrite the value with any http(s) URL, and disclosure of `website` to the recruiter/sponsor directory is already gated by `scope.links` (packages/server/src/persona/directory.ts:985), so there is a working withhold path. Avatar and banner are not even UI-reachable — basics.vue offers upload-only, with no remove control — leaving `website` on /settings/profile/links as the single member-facing instance.

</details>

#### [P3] An over-long upload filename 500s the request after the bytes are already stored, orphaning the object

`layers/base/server/api/files/upload.post.ts:99`

The multipart filename is taken raw from the part's Content-Disposition (`const filename = file.filename || ...`, line 37) and is never length-checked. It is inserted into `files.originalName`, declared `varchar('original_name', { length: 255 })` (packages/schema/src/files.ts:13). Postgres does not truncate on insert into `varchar(n)` — it raises 22001. There is a second overflow on the same insert: `generateStorageKey` derives the extension as `originalName.split('.').pop()` stripped to alphanumerics with NO length cap (packages/infra/src/storage.ts:39-45), so a long extension produces a multi-kilobyte `storageKey` that is written into `files.filename`, also `varchar(255)`. Both overflow AFTER the adapter has already written the object (lines 72 / 75 / 90) and neither is inside a try/catch.

**Failure:** `POST /api/files/upload` (auth required, any member) with a part whose filename is 300 characters, e.g. `('a'.repeat(295)) + '.png'`. Size and MIME pass `validateUpload`; `processImage` runs and writes the original plus four WebP variants to S3/disk; then the metadata insert raises `value too long for type character varying(255)`. The caller receives a bare 500 with no field named, no `files` row exists, and five objects are left in the bucket that no reference points to and no orphan sweep will find (the sweep matches `files.id`). The same request with `name.` + 300 letters overflows `files.filename` via the derived storage key, and on local storage additionally attempts an ENAMETOOLONG write.

**Reachable input:** As any authenticated member (valid better-auth.session_token cookie, no elevated role, no feature flag needed since purpose defaults to the unflagged `content`): POST /api/files/upload with a multipart body whose first part is Content-Disposition: form-data; name="file"; filename="aaaa...a.png" where the name is 295 'a' characters plus ".png" (299 chars total), Content-Type: image/png, and a small valid PNG payload. Concretely: curl -b "$SESSION" -F 'file=@small.png;filename='"$(python3 -c "print('a'*295+'.png')")" https://<instance>/api/files/upload — validateUpload passes (image/png allowed, size under the 10MB content cap), processImage writes the original plus up to four WebP variants through the adapter, then the insert at upload.post.ts:94 raises 22001 "value too long for type character varying(255)" on original_name and the caller gets a bare 500 with five unreferenced objects left in storage. The filename-column variant is the same request with filename="name.bbbb...b" (300 'b' characters after the dot), producing a 345-char storage key for files.filename.

**Suggested fix:** Clamp before use: `const filename = (file.filename || 'upload-' + Date.now()).slice(-200)` and cap the derived extension in `generateStorageKey` (e.g. `ext.slice(0, 12)`). Wrap the metadata insert so a failure deletes the just-written object(s) instead of orphaning them.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The defect is real and unguarded. I read upload.post.ts in full: the multipart filename at line 37 is taken raw, and the only checks before the write are requireAuth (any member), the purpose allowlist, and validateUpload (MIME + size only — packages/infra/src/storage.ts:413-428). No length check exists anywhere in the path; I grepped the route dir and packages/infra for truncation and found none, and no test pins the behaviour. The bytes are written at lines 72/75/90 and the metadata insert at 94-109 has no try/catch, so a 22001 surfaces as a bare Nitro 500 with the objects already in the bucket. Both columns are varchar(255) in the schema (packages/schema/src/files.ts:12-13) and in the applied DDL (migrations/0000_session128_baseline.sql:632); the only later ALTER TABLE "files" is 0043 adding `visibility`, so the live column widths match. Postgres raises 22001 on insert into varchar(n) rather than truncating. I reproduced both overflow arithmetics with the exact generateStorageKey logic: 'a'.repeat(295)+'.png' yields originalName length 299 (overflows original_name; key is a benign 48 chars), and 'name.'+'b'.repeat(300) yields a 345-char storage key written into files.filename. Orphaning is confirmed: layers/base/server/api/files/[id].delete.ts is the only storage-delete path and resolves the key from a files row by id, so an object with no row can never be swept. Two corrections to the report: storage_key is `text`, so only the `filename` column overflows on the long-extension case (the report says this correctly but the title implies otherwise), and this is not reachable through the UI — POSIX caps a filename component at 255 bytes, so the file picker cannot generate it; it requires a crafted request. Severity drops to P3: the impact is a 500 with no field named plus objects invisible to the operator's accounting. It is not storage exhaustion, since with no per-user upload quota the same member can consume identical bytes through successful uploads; the marginal harm is bookkeeping, not capacity, and there is no cross-user or privacy consequence.

</details>

#### [P3] Unbounded integers in write bodies reach int4 columns and 500 a save the user can trigger by typing

`packages/schema/src/validators/product.ts:44`

Several numeric body fields are `z.number().int()` with a lower bound but no `.max()`, while the column is Postgres `integer` (max 2147483647). `addContentProductSchema.quantity` is `z.number().int().positive().default(1)` against `content_products.quantity integer` (packages/schema/src/product.ts:60). `createContentSchema.estimatedMinutes` is `z.number().int().positive()` against `content_items.estimated_minutes integer` (packages/schema/src/content.ts:81). The same pattern recurs on every uncapped `sortOrder` (`createContentCategorySchema`, `createDocsPageSchema`, `createHubResourceSchema`, `createVideoCategorySchema`). Zod accepts the value, Postgres raises 22003, nothing catches it. Contrast `events.capacity`, `estimatedHours` and `durationMinutes`, which all do carry a `.max()`.

**Failure:** The explainer editor's 'estimated minutes' control is `<input type="number" min="1">` with no max (ExplainerEditor.vue:345). An author types 999999999999, the value is coerced to a Number by the `@input` handler, and the next autosave PUTs it. Zod passes it (it is a positive integer), the UPDATE raises `integer out of range`, and the author gets a 500 with no field named — the whole save fails and the autosave keeps retrying the same poisoned body every 30 s. The BOM path is the same: `products-sync` with `quantity: 3000000000` 500s the save of the entire bill of materials. A related gap on the same route: `productsSyncSchema.items` (products-sync.post.ts:8) has no `.max()`, so the array is bounded only by the 10 MB body cap — ~50k rows deleted and re-inserted in one transaction.

**Reachable input:** Authenticated author of their own content item, one request: PUT /api/content/<uuid-of-content-they-own> Content-Type: application/json {"title":"My explainer","content":[],"estimatedMinutes":9999999999} Path verified end to end: - layers/base/server/api/content/[id]/index.put.ts calls requireAuth, parseParams, then `parseBody(event, updateContentSchema)` with NO try/catch around `updateContent`. - packages/schema/src/validators/content.ts:34-37 — `estimatedMinutes: z.preprocess(..., z.number().int().positive().optional())`. 9999999999 is a positive safe integer, so Zod accepts it. No `.max()`. - packages/server/src/content/content.ts:702 — `estimatedMinutes` is in `CONTENT_PASSTHROUGH_FIELDS`, so `applyContentPassthroughUpdates` copies the raw value straight onto the UPDATE. No clamp anywhere in between. - packages/schema/src/content.ts:81 — `estimatedMinutes: integer('estimated_minutes')` (int4, max 2147483647). Postgres raises 22003 "value out of range for type integer". I grepped for 22003 / "out of range" handling across layers/ and packages/: there is no error mapper, no Nitro error plugin, no catch. The error surfaces as an unhandled 500 with no field name — the same class the team already documented at packages/server/src/publicApi/metrics.ts:47. UI-reachable form of the same request: layers/base/components/editors/ExplainerEditor.vue:345 is `<input type="number" min="1">` with no `max`, and the handler does `Number(value)` with no bound. layers/base/pages/u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/edit.vue:224 copies it into `metadata` on any truthy value, useContentSave.ts:75-90 spreads `metadata` into the body (its sanitiser only rewrites `''` and `null`), and initAutoSave fires a PUT every 30s (AUTO_SAVE_DELAY = 30_000), so the poisoned body does keep retrying. The `quantity` half is reachable the same way at the route level (POST /api/content/<id>/products-sync with {"items":[{"productId":"<real-product-uuid>","quantity":3000000000}]} 500s), since addContentProductSchema.quantity is `z.number().int().positive().default(1)` against `integer('quantity')` at packages/schema/src/product.ts:60.

**Suggested fix:** Add `.max(2_147_483_647)` (or a domain-sensible ceiling — 100000 for quantity, 525600 for minutes) to every int field that reaches an `integer` column, and cap `productsSyncSchema.items` at the BOM ceiling the editor actually allows. A shared `intColumn = (max) => z.number().int().min(0).max(max)` helper keeps the two in step.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED on the primary path, with two parts of the write-up corrected. What I verified myself: the cited validators have no `.max()`; the columns are int4; nothing between Zod and the UPDATE clamps; `parseBody` (layers/base/server/utils/validate.ts) enforces only a 10MB body cap and Zod shape, and its own comment says the per-field `.max()` caps are "the real semantic bound within this envelope" — which is exactly the bound missing here; the PUT route has no try/catch; there is no 22003 handler anywhere in the repo; there is no test pinning a 400 for oversized integers; and git history shows the line was moved verbatim by the c412a17a validators split, not written deliberately. The contrast the claim draws is real: packages/schema/src/validators/learning.ts:11 and :37 do carry `.max(9999)` on the analogous fields. Guards I looked for and did not find: no feature flag (content editing is core, no requireFeature on this route), no DB CHECK constraint (plain `integer`), no upstream coercion, no numeric-bound helper in validators/_shared.ts (it only has URL helpers). Corrections to the claim: 1. The BOM failure scenario is wrong. edit.vue:272 calls products-sync as `$fetch(...).catch(() => {})`, so an overflowing `quantity` from the editor does NOT 500 the author's save — the BOM sync fails silently and the parts list quietly does not persist. That is a different (arguably worse-to-diagnose but lower-impact) bug than the one described. Only a hand-crafted POST 500s. 2. The "~50k rows deleted and re-inserted" sub-claim on the unbounded `items` array is not reachable as stated: content_products.productId is a FK to products.id and there is a uniqueIndex on (contentId, productId), so the array is bounded in practice by the number of distinct real product rows on the instance, and bogus UUIDs die on the FK long before row count matters. Severity: P3 is correct and I would not raise it. The only actor who can trigger it is the content's own author (updateContent filters by authorId), the blast radius is that one author's own save, there is no data loss, no privilege boundary crossed, and no effect on other users. It is a self-inflicted opaque 500 plus a retrying autosave — a real UX/robustness defect of the "validate domain not shape" class, not an availability or security issue. The right fix is a `.max()` on these fields (and a matching `max` on the number input), not anything structural.

</details>

#### [P3] Contest date invariants are enforced only in the editor; a partial PUT satisfies the server refine vacuously

`packages/schema/src/validators/contest.ts:451`

`createContestSchema` carries two cross-field refines: `endDate > startDate` and `judgingEndDate >= endDate`. `updateContestSchema`, hand-rebuilt because `.partial()` is unavailable on a refined object, re-applies only the FIRST one — and re-applies it as `!d.startDate || !d.endDate || ...`, so it passes vacuously whenever only one of the two dates is present in the body. `updateContest` compares nothing against the stored row (contest.ts:174-176 just assigns). The judging invariant exists only client-side, in `dateError` (useContestEditor.ts:243-251), which merely disables the Save button.

**Failure:** deveco.io's live contest runs 2026-08-01 → 2026-09-15. A contest owner or an `editor` stakeholder sends `PUT /api/contests/<slug>` with a body of exactly `{"endDate":"2026-07-01T00:00:00Z"}`. `startDate` is absent, so the refine short-circuits to true; the write lands. Submissions are now closed against a start date that is later than the end date, and every deadline/'time remaining' computation on the contest page derives from that pair. Independently, `{"judgingEndDate":"2026-08-02T00:00:00Z"}` sets judging to end six weeks before submissions close, with no server-side objection at all — the only thing that ever refused it was a disabled button.

**Reachable input:** As the contest owner (or an `editor` stakeholder / `contest.manage` holder) on deveco.io, with `features.contests` on: `PUT /api/contests/<slug>` with body exactly `{"judgingEndDate":"2026-08-02T00:00:00.000Z"}` on the live contest (2026-08-01 to 2026-09-15). No `endDate` in the body, no judging refine anywhere on the update path, no DB constraint — 200, and `judging_end_date` is stored six weeks before `end_date`. Equivalently `{"endDate":"2026-07-01T00:00:00.000Z"}` stores an end before the stored start, since the lone refine short-circuits on the absent `startDate`. Both are also reachable through the editor UI without hand-crafting a request: in edit mode the date fields are `:required="mode === 'create'"`, so clearing Start Date (or End Date) empties the ref, `dateError` short-circuits to '' and Save enables, and `buildPayload` omits that key — producing the same one-sided body.

**Suggested fix:** Move the invariant into `updateContest`, where the stored row is already loaded: merge `data` over `existing` and validate the resulting triple (start < end <= judgingEnd) before the write, returning a 400 that names the offending field. A schema-level refine cannot see the absent half.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The defect exists and is unguarded. `updateContestSchema` (packages/schema/src/validators/contest.ts:410-454) re-applies only the start/end refine, as `!d.startDate || !d.endDate || ...`, and omits the judgingEndDate refine entirely — so a body carrying only one date satisfies the first vacuously, and ANY body (even a complete one) can set judgingEndDate < endDate with no server objection. `updateContest` (packages/server/src/contest/contest.ts:174-176) assigns the dates without comparing against the fetched `existing` row. The PUT route (layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/index.put.ts) does auth only. No DB guard: the three columns are plain timestamps (packages/schema/src/contest.ts:440-442) and the only CHECK constraints in packages/schema/migrations are layout_sections_col_span_check and contest_agreements_one_scope. The judging invariant's message string exists in exactly two places — the create schema and useContestEditor.ts:248 (`dateError`, which only disables the Save button). The one test on updateContestSchema (validators.test.ts:1090) pins the judges-stripping behaviour, nothing about dates. The UI is not a backstop either: ContestEditor.vue:670-676 marks the date fields `:required="mode === 'create'"`, so in edit mode they are clearable, and buildPayload emits `startDate.value || undefined` — clearing a date makes the client `dateError` and the server refine vacuous at the same time, so the corrupt write is reachable through the real editor, not only through curl. However the claimed consequence is overstated: gating is by `status`, not dates. `currentStage` (packages/server/src/contest/stages.ts:36-53) derives from status ("`status` remains the behavioural source of truth for gating"), and submitStageArtifact refuses on `contestStatus !== 'active'`, so a backwards endDate does NOT close submissions; ContestSidebar.vue:97-102 already suppresses the backwards span. Real impact is corrupted countdown/deadline display, the nextContestDeadline fallback to a past endDate feeding the reminder sweep and email copy. Every actor who can reach the route (owner, `editor` stakeholder, `contest.manage` holder) is already authorized to set these dates, so this is self-inflicted misconfiguration of one's own contest, correctable by a second PUT — a genuine validation asymmetry against the create path, but P3, not P2.

</details>

### The migration chain and schema integrity

#### [P2] getContentBySlug ignores type though uniqueness is (author, type, slug), so a URL can serve the wrong item

`packages/server/src/content/content.ts:568`

`content_items` is unique on `(author_id, type, slug)` (packages/schema/src/content.ts:106) and `createContent` scopes its slug-uniqueness probe to the same three columns (content.ts:735-739 → query.ts:110-116). But the resolver builds its WHERE from `slug` + `deletedAt` plus an OPTIONAL `authorUsername`/`authorId` — `type` is never a condition — and finishes with `.limit(1)` and no ORDER BY. The URL layer supplies a type it then discards: `/u/:username/:type/:slug` reads `route.params.type` into `contentType` (pages/u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/index.vue:7) and never passes it to the fetch at line 14.

**Failure:** Alice publishes a project titled "Robot Arm" (slug `robot-arm`) and later a blog post titled "Robot Arm". The per-(author,type) uniqueness probe finds no clash, so the blog also gets slug `robot-arm` — both rows are legal under `content_items_author_type_slug`. Now `/u/alice/project/robot-arm` and `/u/alice/blog/robot-arm` both call `/api/content/robot-arm?author=alice`, which matches two rows and returns whichever Postgres hands back first (no ORDER BY, so this can flip after any UPDATE moves a heap tuple). One of the two pages renders the wrong item — and because the page selects its view component from `content.type`, the project URL renders the blog view. Separately, `/api/public/v1/content/:slug` makes `author` optional (querySchema at [slug].get.ts:4-6), so with no author the same query picks an arbitrary user's item whenever two authors share a slug (`hello-world`, `first-post`).

**Reachable input:** As authenticated user `alice`: (1) `POST /api/content {"type":"project","title":"Robot Arm"}` then publish it -> row slug `robot-arm`, type project, status published. (2) `POST /api/content {"type":"blog","title":"Robot Arm"}` -> the (author,type)-scoped probe finds no clash, so a second row gets slug `robot-arm`, type blog, status draft; both rows are legal under content_items_author_type_slug. (3) Anonymous `GET /api/content/robot-arm?author=alice` (issued by /u/alice/project/robot-arm at index.vue:14) matches both rows, returns the blog draft, which the status gate at content.ts:597 then nulls -> the published project's own URL 404s. With the blog also published, the same request returns the blog and /u/alice/project/robot-arm renders it inside ViewsProjectView. Also reproducible unauthenticated on the public API: `GET /api/public/v1/content/hello-world` with no `author` (querySchema makes it optional at [slug].get.ts:4-6) returns an arbitrary author's published item when two users share the slug.

**Suggested fix:** Add an optional `type` parameter to `getContentBySlug` and push `eq(contentItems.type, type)` into the conditions; have the `/api/content/:slug` route and the `[type]` page pass it, and make `author` required on the public v1 route (or resolve it and 409/redirect on ambiguity). Add a deterministic `orderBy` as a backstop so the choice is at least stable.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified by reading and by empirical reproduction. getContentBySlug (packages/server/src/content/content.ts:559-591) has no `type` parameter and no `type` condition; its WHERE is slug + deletedAt IS NULL + optional username/authorId, finished with .limit(1) and no ORDER BY. The ambiguous DB state is not hypothetical: it is deliberately produced by createContent's (authorId, type)-scoped probe (content.ts:735-739 -> query.ts:110-116) and explicitly pinned by url-restructure.integration.test.ts:122-136 ('allows same slug for different content types by same author'), which asserts a project and an article by one author both keep the clean slug. Neither route disambiguates (api/content/[id]/index.get.ts:13 passes only authorUsername; public/v1/content/[slug].get.ts:25 only an optional author), and the page never compares content.type to route type. I reproduced it against the running commonpub Postgres inside a rolled-back transaction: inserting a published `project` and a draft `blog` sharing author_id+slug, the resolver's exact WHERE returned the blog draft under BOTH the authorId shape and the username shape (plan: Index Scan using idx_content_items_author_status, Filter on deleted_at + slug). Because the returned draft then fails the status gate at content.ts:597, an anonymous visitor to the PUBLISHED project's URL gets a 404 instead of the project; when both are published, the project URL renders the blog's payload. Two corrections to the finding: (1) the page selects its view from the URL's `contentType` (index.vue:104-106), not content.type, so the wrong item renders under the URL's view rather than the item's; (2) unstated but confirmed additional impact - createContent returns getContentBySlug(item.slug, authorId, undefined, authorId) at content.ts:765, also type-blind, and useContentSave.ts:103-137 assigns that id to opts.contentId and PUTs every later autosave to it, so a new draft's body can be written into the pre-existing sibling row (same author, so the ownership check passes).

</details>

#### [P3] Deploy serves the new image before migrations run, so column-adding deploys 500 a live route

`.github/workflows/deploy.yml:103`

The deploy script runs `docker compose up -d --force-recreate` (line 103), `sleep 5` (line 104), and only then `exec -T app node scripts/db-migrate.mjs` (line 111). The container's `CMD` (Dockerfile:68) is `node .output/server/index.mjs` with no migrate step, so nothing applies DDL at boot. Nitro binds and Caddy routes traffic to the new code several seconds before the first migration statement executes, and the whole pending batch then runs inside one transaction (verified in drizzle-orm/pg-core/dialect.cjs:62-73 — `session.transaction` wraps the entire loop), so newly-created objects are invisible to the serving process until the batch commits.

**Failure:** Migration 0047 (`ALTER TABLE "api_keys" ADD COLUMN "recipient_id" varchar(40)`) was deployed this way. `authenticateApiKey` (packages/server/src/publicApi/auth.ts:48-51) does `db.select().from(apiKeys)`, which drizzle expands into an explicit column list that includes `recipient_id`. Between container start and migration commit, every request to any `/api/public/v1/*` route — the whole key-authenticated public API — throws `column apiKeys.recipient_id does not exist` and returns 500. The same window applies to 0048's `user_shared_links`/`user_statistics_objections` and the persona routes that read them. If the migration FAILS (a constraint violated by live data, or a lock timeout on a busy `content_items`), the workflow prints `::error` and exits 1 — but the incompatible image stays live indefinitely with no automatic rollback, turning a transient window into an outage on an instance running a live contest.

**Reachable input:** `GET /api/auth/export-data` with a valid session cookie from any ordinary logged-in member, issued in the window between Nitro binding on the freshly recreated container and the migration transaction committing, on the deploy that shipped migrations 0047/0048. The handler runs requireAuth only (no feature flag), and exportUserData issues `select ... from user_statistics_objections where user_id = $1` and `from user_shared_links` and `from disclosure_events`; against the pre-migration schema Postgres raises `relation "user_statistics_objections" does not exist` and the route returns 500. Note the finding's own stated input (`GET /api/public/v1/*` with `Authorization: Bearer cpub_...`) is NOT reachable: publicApi is false on the deployed instance and the middleware 404s before touching the DB.

**Suggested fix:** Run `db-migrate.mjs` against the DB before the new container is allowed to serve — either as a one-shot `docker compose run --rm app node scripts/db-migrate.mjs` on the NEW image prior to `up -d`, or as a `CMD`-level migrate-then-serve entrypoint (which the create-commonpub scaffolder already generates, per docs/llm/gotchas.md:143). Keep the existing hard-fail so a failed migration blocks the cutover instead of following it.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The ordering defect is real: deploy.yml does `up -d --force-recreate` -> `sleep 5` -> `exec db-migrate.mjs`, Dockerfile's CMD has no migrate step, and I confirmed in drizzle-orm/pg-core/dialect.cjs that `session.transaction` wraps the whole pending batch, so new DDL is invisible to the serving process until commit. However, the finding's flagship failure scenario is REFUTED. layers/base/server/middleware/public-api-auth.ts:31-34 throws 404 at `if (!config.features.publicApi)` before it ever calls useDB()/authenticateApiKey, and the live instance this workflow deploys reports `"publicApi":false` (GET https://commonpub.io/api/public/v1/ping returns 404, verified). The admin reader layers/base/server/api/admin/api-keys/index.get.ts also opens with `requireFeature('publicApi')`. So `db.select().from(apiKeys)` is unreachable on this instance and `apiKeys.recipient_id` can never be selected. The persona half is equally gated (`persona`, `memberDirectory`, `dataSharingConsents`, `personaAnalytics` all false). The defect survives only via a different, flag-independent route I found myself: layers/base/server/api/auth/export-data.get.ts is `requireAuth` only and carries an explicit comment that it is deliberately NOT gated on `features.persona` because a subject access request is a legal obligation; it calls exportUserData, which at packages/server/src/profile/export.ts:630-645 unconditionally selects from disclosureEvents, userStatisticsObjections and userSharedLinks. Severity drops from P2 to P3: the window is seconds long and self-heals on commit; `--force-recreate` on a single container already makes this deploy non-zero-downtime by design, so the marginal harm is a 500 instead of a 502 during a gap that already exists; and scripts/smoke.mjs:30 budgets 40x3s for Nuxt cold start, strong evidence the app usually has not bound within the 5s head start, so the migration typically wins the race. The no-automatic-rollback half is real but loud (`::error` + `exit 1`) and is an operational posture, not an active defect. Git history on deploy.yml (pipefail, in-container smoke, drizzle-kit push -> committed migrations) shows the ordering was never deliberated, so this is an unaddressed gap rather than an intended trade-off.

</details>

#### [P3] 34 declared indexes are exact prefixes of another index on the same table

`packages/schema/src/layout.ts:85`
