A UNIQUE constraint and a composite index on the same leading columns produce two btrees that serve the same lookups; the narrower one is dead weight on every INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE and in VACUUM. Comparing every index against every unique constraint and composite PK in the 0048 snapshot finds 34 such pairs. Three are exact full duplicates (identical column list AND order): `idx_layout_rows_layout` vs `layout_rows_position_unique` on `(layout_id, zone, position)`, `idx_layout_sections_row` vs `layout_sections_position_unique` on `(row_id, position)`, and `idx_layout_versions_layout` vs `layout_versions_version_unique` on `(layout_id, version)` — plus `idx_layouts_scope` vs `layouts_scope_unique` on `(scope_type, scope_key)`. The rest are single-column prefixes, e.g. `idx_notifications_user_id` (covered three times over by `idx_notifications_user_created`, `idx_notifications_user_read` and `uq_notif_user_type_actor_link`), `idx_fedcontent_object_uri` duplicating the UNIQUE on the same unbounded `text` column, and `idx_contest_entries_contest_id` covered by `contest_entries_user_content`.

**Failure:** Every write to `notifications` (the highest-insert-rate table: one row per like, comment, follow, contest event, plus the digest sweep) maintains four btrees on `user_id` where one composite would do. Every layout save deletes and re-inserts all rows and sections (packages/server/src/layout/layout.ts:392-461), doubling the index maintenance for those two tables. `federated_content` carries two full btrees over the same `text` object_uri, the widest key in the schema. Not a correctness bug, but a permanent, avoidable write-amplification on the hottest tables that compounds as tables are added.

**Reachable input:** Two concrete triggers, both live and unguarded: 1. `PUT /api/admin/layouts/{id}` (layers/base/server/api/admin/layouts/[id].put.ts → packages/server/src/layout/layout.ts:392-461). Any authenticated admin saving a layout runs `tx.delete(layoutRows).where(eq(layoutRows.layoutId, id))` then re-inserts every row and section. Each re-inserted `layout_rows` row maintains BOTH `layout_rows_position_unique` and `idx_layout_rows_layout`, which migrations/0005_wonderful_blue_marvel.sql:9 and :60 create with byte-identical key lists `(layout_id, zone, "position")`. Same for `layout_sections` on `(row_id, position)`. 2. Any inbound federated `Create` on the shared inbox → packages/server/src/federation/inboxHandlers.ts:810 `insert(federatedContent)`. migrations/0000_session128_baseline.sql:440 declares `CONSTRAINT "federated_content_object_uri_unique" UNIQUE("object_uri")` and line 1217 separately declares `CREATE INDEX "idx_fedcontent_object_uri" ON "federated_content" USING btree ("object_uri")` — two full btrees over the same unbounded `text` column, maintained on every remote post received. No feature flag, no guard: unauthenticated remote instances drive this path. No guard exists. I looked for one: there is no index-hygiene test, no comment justifying the pairs in packages/schema/src/layout.ts (I read all 185 lines), and git history shows the layout pairs landed in a single commit (69b1a0c0) with no rationale. The one nearby comment that DOES discuss drizzle-kit behaviour (packages/schema/src/social.ts:120-130 — pushSchema "silently drops table-level CONSTRAINT declarations") is not a justification for the plain duplicates: a non-unique index cannot substitute for a uniqueness constraint, and the team's actual fix for that problem was switching to `uniqueIndex`, not adding a parallel plain index.

**Suggested fix:** Drop the four exact duplicates first (`idx_layout_rows_layout`, `idx_layout_sections_row`, `idx_layout_versions_layout`, `idx_layouts_scope`) — they can never be chosen over the unique index — then the single-column prefixes on the write-heavy tables (`idx_notifications_user_id`, `idx_fedcontent_object_uri`, `idx_contest_entries_contest_id`, `idx_contest_registrations_contest_id`, `idx_bookmarks_user_id`, `idx_follows_follower_id`). Ship as one generated migration so the snapshot chain stays authoritative.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED, but the counts and the failure-scenario arithmetic need correcting. What I verified myself. I reproduced the analysis over packages/schema/migrations/meta/0048_snapshot.json and got 5 exact-duplicate pairs and 30 single-column-prefix pairs (35 total, vs the claimed 34 — the claim listed only 4 exact and filed `idx_fedcontent_object_uri` under "the rest", when it is in fact a 5th exact duplicate). Every specific index named in the claim exists exactly as described: packages/schema/src/layout.ts:56/57, :84/85, :121/122, :147/148; packages/schema/src/social.ts:114-131; packages/schema/src/federation.ts:95/146; packages/schema/src/contest.ts:577/578. I confirmed the DDL actually emits both objects in production (not deduped by drizzle-kit) by reading the migration SQL directly. The indisputable core is the 5 exact duplicates — identical column lists in identical order, so two byte-identical btrees per table serving no distinct query. That is pure dead weight with no defensible tradeoff. Corrections that shrink the impact story: - "notifications maintains four btrees on user_id where one composite would do" is wrong. Only `idx_notifications_user_id` is redundant. `idx_notifications_user_read` (user_id, read), `idx_notifications_user_created` (user_id, created_at DESC, id DESC) and `uq_notif_user_type_actor_link` (user_id, type, actor_id, link) each back a different query and one of them enforces the like/unlike dedup invariant. The fix removes ONE index from the hottest table, not three. - "doubling the index maintenance" for the layout tables is wrong. `layout_rows` carries 3 btrees (PK on id, the unique, the duplicate); dropping the duplicate is a one-third reduction, not a halving. Layout saves are also admin-gated and rare, so this is the least consequential of the three tables named, despite being the cited location. - The 30 prefix pairs are hygiene-grade rather than clear waste. A narrow leading-column index is smaller than the composite that covers it and can legitimately win for pure equality lookups; dropping them is standard DBA advice but is a judgement call, not a defect. The finding should not be sold as 34 equivalent problems. Severity holds at P3 as filed. Zero correctness impact, nothing user-visible, no route breaks. It is a permanent, avoidable write cost that is real and cheaply fixable — 5 `DROP INDEX` statements in one migration retire the undebatable half — which is exactly what P3 is for. It is not P2: the highest-volume affected path (federated_content inserts) pays one extra text-key btree per remote post, which is measurable but not a stability or capacity threat at any plausible instance size.

</details>

#### [P3] hub_members has no user_id index, and 36 FK columns are unindexed, so cascades sequential-scan

`packages/schema/src/hub.ts:78`

`hub_members` declares only `primaryKey({ columns: [t.hubId, t.userId] })` — I verified against a database built from the committed chain that the table has exactly one index, `hub_members_hub_id_user_id_pk` on `(hub_id, user_id)`. A btree keyed on `hub_id` first cannot serve a predicate on `user_id` alone. Systematically checking the 0048 snapshot, 36 single-column FKs have no index leading with the FK column, including `sessions.user_id`, `accounts.user_id`, `email_outbox.user_id`, `hub_members.user_id`, `content_versions.created_by_id`, `contest_agreement_acceptances.user_id`, `contest_reminder_sends.user_id`, `notifications.actor_id` and `contest_entries.content_id` — all with `ON DELETE cascade` or `set null`.

**Failure:** `listProducts` runs `select hubMembers.hubId ... where(and(eq(hubMembers.userId, requesterId), eq(hubMembers.status,'active')))` (packages/server/src/product/product.ts:343-346) on every authenticated product-list request once any private hub exists — a full scan of `hub_members` per request. The DSAR export (profile/export.ts:347) and admin user delete (admin/admin.ts:675) do the same user-only lookup. Independently, `DELETE FROM users WHERE id = …` forces Postgres to seq-scan each of those 36 child tables to enforce the referential action while holding row locks; `email_outbox` is never pruned (so it grows without bound) and `sessions`/`notifications` are the highest-churn tables in the schema, so account deletion degrades in proportion to total instance history rather than to the deleted user's own data.

**Reachable input:** Primary, no special privileges: `GET /api/products` (optionally `?q=foo`) sent with a valid ordinary-user session cookie, on any instance where at least one row in `hubs` has `privacy = 'private'`. The route layers/base/server/api/products/index.get.ts is unflagged, takes `getOptionalUser(event)` and threads `user?.id` into `searchProducts`, whose non-admin branch (packages/server/src/product/product.ts:341-346) then runs `select hub_id from hub_members where user_id = $requesterId and status = 'active'` on every such request. EXPLAIN on the live DB confirms this resolves to a full bitmap index scan of the composite PK, never a ranged seek. The `blocked.length > 0 && requesterId` condition means the only preconditions are "authenticated" and "one private hub exists" — both ordinary DB state, no crafting required. Secondary (admin-gated, drives the cascade): `DELETE` of a user via the admin user-delete path — packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts:675 first runs the same user-only `select ... from hub_members where user_id = ?`, then admin.ts:696 issues `tx.delete(users).where(eq(users.id, userId))`, whose RI triggers emit `DELETE FROM <child> WHERE <fk> = $1` against 36 child tables; for sessions, accounts, email_outbox and content_versions no index contains that column, so each is a genuine heap seq scan under row locks. Tertiary: the DSAR export at packages/server/src/profile/export.ts:347 runs `.from(hubMembers).where(eq(hubMembers.userId, userId))` for the requesting user's own export.

**Suggested fix:** Add `index('idx_hub_members_user').on(t.userId, t.status)` to hub_members (it serves both the product/hub membership lookups and the cascade), and add covering indexes for at least the high-churn cascade parents: `sessions.user_id`, `accounts.user_id`, `email_outbox.user_id`, `notifications.actor_id`, `content_versions.created_by_id`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Verified independently and empirically, not taken on trust. (1) Live DB check: `select indexname from pg_indexes where tablename='hub_members'` returns exactly one row, `hub_members_hub_id_user_id_pk ON (hub_id, user_id)`. (2) I wrote my own pg_constraint/pg_index query for single-column FKs whose column does not lead any index; it returns 36 — independently matching the claimed count, and a separate recomputation from 0048_snapshot.json produced the identical 36-column list including sessions.user_id, accounts.user_id, email_outbox.user_id, hub_members.user_id, content_versions.created_by_id, notifications.actor_id and contest_entries.content_id. (3) Per-table index dumps confirm sessions (only pkey + token_unique), accounts (only pkey), email_outbox (only pkey + (status,scheduled_at)) and content_versions (only pkey + content_id) have NO index containing the FK column, so those ON DELETE cascade branches are true heap sequential scans. (4) email_outbox is confirmed never pruned: `delete(emailOutbox)` appears only in three __tests__ teardowns, never in packages/server/src production code, so it grows without bound. Guard hunt found nothing. hub_members appears only in 0000_session128_baseline.sql across all migration SQL, so no raw-SQL migration adds an index outside the Drizzle snapshot (the live DB confirms). No feature flag gates the path. It is not deliberate: sibling tables in the SAME file all carry the equivalent index (packages/schema/src/hub.ts:145 idx_hub_post_likes_user_id, :165 idx_hub_bans_user_id, :107 idx_hub_posts_author_id) — hub_members is the lone membership table without one. docs/sessions/119-cleanup-version-fix-borders.md:171 records the project fixing exactly this class before (idx_bookmarks_user_id, idx_lesson_progress_user_id), so the convention is established, not waived. No test pins the absence. ONE CORRECTION to the claimed mechanism: for hub_members itself Postgres does not sequential-scan. EXPLAIN yields "Bitmap Heap Scan ... -> Bitmap Index Scan on hub_members_hub_id_user_id_pk, Index Cond: (user_id = ...)" — a full scan of the composite PK index with user_id non-leading. Still O(total memberships) with no ranged seek, so the asymptotic complaint holds, but the constant factor is meaningfully better than "seq scan of the table". Same nuance for notifications.actor_id, which is covered non-leading by uq_notif_user_type_actor_link (user_id,type,actor_id,link). The literal seq-scan characterization IS accurate for sessions/accounts/email_outbox/content_versions. Minor citation error, non-fatal: the query at product.ts:343-346 is in `searchProducts` (line 308), not `listProducts`; the line numbers and the code are exactly as quoted, and that is the function the public route calls. Severity stays P3. There is no correctness or security impact — the private-hub NOT-IN filter still returns the right rows, just via an O(n) index scan. Present-day cost is negligible (hub_members holds 15 rows on the dev instance). This is a scale-optimality defect that degrades with instance history, which is precisely the P3 band; not P2 because nothing fails today and there is no user-visible outage at current volumes.

</details>

## Is it clear how to submit a proposal?

**1. Is it clear how to submit a proposal? Not on your contest. On a correctly configured one, yes.**

The proposal path only renders when the current stage is `submissionMode: proposal` *and* its submission form has at least one field (`index.vue:253`, and `ContestProposalForm.vue:62` guards on `template.length` again). Your live Resilient America stage is proposal mode with an empty form. So the entire proposal path is off, silently — no form, no mention of the word anywhere an entrant can act on, and until this session's change nothing told you. `contestProposals` is already ON for deveco; the empty form is the only thing in the way.

**2. What an entrant actually walks through today**

They land on the hero: title, status pill, a stage chip reading "Proposals", a countdown, and "Log in to register". Nothing on the page says how to enter. After logging in they get "Register for this contest" and land on the register page — 42 fields, 20 required, including a shipping address for a dev kit they haven't won and a required "I confirm my country of residence is the United States" that a non-US maker cannot honestly tick. On save they return to Overview. The sidebar (desktop) now shows "Next: submit your project" with a button; on mobile the fixed bottom bar shows "Submit entry". Either one switches to the Entries tab, where — because proposal mode is inert — they read "Enter with an existing project. Submit one of your published projects, or start a new one." So they must go author and publish a project first, then come back and attach it. Six steps, and step five is a whole separate piece of work.

**3. The one change worth making**

Put fields on the Proposals stage form. It is a config edit in the stage editor, not code. It collapses the funnel from "register → go write and publish a project → come back → attach it" to "register → fill four boxes → done", which is what a proposal round is supposed to mean, and it is the difference between an entrant who bounces and one who is in your entries list tonight.

Two things to know before you do it. Name the first field exactly **Title**, not "Project name": the server titles the draft it creates from a field whose key is `title` and otherwise falls back to `"<Contest> proposal"` (`packages/server/src/contest/submissions.ts:459`), so the stock starter template would give you an entries list and a CSV where every row has the same name. And if an entrant leaves a required box empty, the form only fires a 3-second toast — no red field, no message under the input, no focus jump (`ContestProposalForm.vue:40`). On a long form that reads as a dead button. Worth a fix before you rely on it, but it does not block turning the form on.

**4. Leave alone**

The register-then-enter split and its new copy, the hero and mobile "Submit entry" buttons (they do switch tabs and scroll to the form), keeping the attach-a-project option visible alongside proposals, and the sidebar nudge. Those are working as designed and this session improved them.

## Systemic synthesis

# Round 2 synthesis

## 1. Round-2 findings that are further instances of the six known causes

### (1) The `server:false` zero-seed — **proven bigger, and mis-classified**

| Finding | What it adds |
|---|---|
| `useEngagement.ts:101` | Round 1 saw this as an SEO/crawler problem. It is on every project/blog/explainer page. |
| `ArticleView.vue:48` (`isFollowingAuthor` never produced) | The seed isn't zero — it doesn't exist. Same shape, one step worse. |
| `plugins/auth.ts:15` (RBAC perms never reach the client) | The pattern applied to **authorization state**: server knows the grant, client renders the unprivileged default. Delegated RBAC roles are effectively unusable in the admin UI. |
| `ContestRegistrationForm.vue:53` (saved answers wipe typed input) | The **destructive** variant: the late client fetch doesn't just correct a placeholder, it overwrites what the user typed. |
| `contests/[slug]/index.vue:14` (fake contest, HTTP 200) | Empty-seed-rendered-as-real, escalated to a wrong status code and a working Register button on a contest that doesn't exist. |

Verdict: bigger and differently shaped. Round 1 framed it as "a false number in the HTML." Round 2 shows the same call-shape destroys user input, hides authorization, and serves 200s for nonexistent resources. It is a correctness class, not a crawler class.

### (2) `FormField` has no identity independent of its label — **proven much bigger; round 1's fix is not a fix**

`ContestStageCard.vue:179`, `FormTemplateEditor.vue:187`, `FormTemplateEditor.vue:244`, `packages/schema/src/contest.ts:289`, `packages/server/src/contest/submissions.ts:210`.

Round 1 found this once, in the registration builder, and fixed it there. Round 2 finds **three producers of `FormField[]`** — the registration builder, the stage submission template, and the markdown importer — and the key lock was wired into exactly one. The markdown importer re-opens the identical bug that was declared fixed. `contest.ts:289` shows the same cause one level down: an **option value** also has no identity independent of its display text, so a whitespace variant validates as a condition and can never match. `submissions.ts:210` shows the PII consequence: an answer is bound to the form only by its key's presence in the template, so hiding a field abandons the stored answer rather than removing it.

This is the clearest case in the whole audit of a fix applied to a symptom's location rather than the cause's location. On deveco, with a live contest and real entrants, the unfixed producer is the one organizers touch mid-contest.

### (3) Ownerless denormalized counters — **bigger in kind**

`social.ts:126` (two video counters with **zero** writers), `videos/[id].get.ts:9` (increment with no dedup, unlike the content path), `members.ts:78`, `learning.ts:783` (increment-only, certificate-gated, reconciled from a different population), `inboxHandlers.ts:1303` (Undo(Like) has no `federated_hub_posts` branch, so remote unlikes inflate permanently).

Round 1 audited the reconciler and found the reconciler wrong. Round 2 shows the reconciler is the wrong unit of analysis: these columns are created alongside their table and nobody owns increment + decrement + reconcile + the federated mirror of each. Two of them have no writer at all, so no reconciler bug could have surfaced them.

### (4) Unbounded list reads — **bigger, and the wrong name**

`sitemap.xml:20`, `backfill.ts:249`, `inboxHandlers.ts:521`, `hubs/[slug]/products.ts:26`, `useMarkdownImport.ts:47`.

Two changes. First, `inboxHandlers.ts:521` is the **write** version driven by an unauthenticated remote party: one signed POST expands an attacker-supplied `to` array into thousands of full-size rows. That moves the class from "slow page" to "remote DoS."

Second, the name is wrong. `hubs/.../products.ts:26` truncates at 20 and reports the page length as `totalItems`; `useMarkdownImport.ts:47` silently stops rehosting after 10. Unbounded and silently-truncated are the same defect — **no list operation in this codebase has an explicit, honest bound.** Half are missing a cap, half are missing an admission that a cap was applied.

### (5) The SSR-date call sites are one bug — **unchanged**

Round 2 found no new instances. Round 1's inventory of ~31 is probably the true extent. This is the one cause that held its size, which is mild evidence the round-1 inventory method (grep the formatter, count call sites) works where the enumeration is mechanical.

### (6) A sanitizer that does not recurse into `children` — **proven bigger, and the diagnosis was too narrow**

`inboxHandlers.ts:770`, `BlockContentRenderer.vue:141`, `useSanitize.ts:104`.

`packages/protocol/src/sanitize.ts:2` says *"Critical security boundary — all federated HTML MUST pass through this."* It is an HTML-string sanitizer. The content model is a **block tree**. Remote `cpub:blocks` are therefore not unsanitized by oversight; they are unsanitized **by construction** — they were never HTML, so no path was ever going to route them through the HTML boundary. `BlockContentRenderer.vue:141` is the same input hitting the render function and 500-ing the mirror page.

Restate the cause: not "the sanitizer forgot to recurse," but **the trust boundary is typed over a format the payload stopped using.** And the input's trust level changed too: round 1's instance was locally-authored admin content passing through a permissive sanitizer. Round 2's is an unauthenticated remote actor reaching the *same* permissive sanitizer.

---

## 2. The new systemic cause: **the `undefined` merge — every write is a partial merge that trusts every field and can clear none**

Twelve files in `packages/server/src` contain **116** instances of:

```ts
if (input.x !== undefined) updates.x = input.x;
```

`contest.ts` (33), `profile.ts` (14), `events.ts` (13), `hub.ts` (9), `learning.ts` (9), `docs.ts` (8), `product.ts` (8), `categories.ts` (6), `content.ts` (5), `resources.ts` (5), `referral/links.ts` (4), `video.ts` (2).

This one shape produces two unrelated-looking families of defect, which is why round 1 filed its instances under three different dimensions and never joined them.

**Family A — nothing can ever be cleared.** The column is nullable; the API has no way to say so. Clients and routes independently coerce the empty case to `undefined`, which the merge then reads as "no change":

- `useContentSave.ts:89` — client rewrites `''` and `null` to `undefined` for *every* metadata key. No content field is clearable anywhere in the editor.
- `profile.put.ts:12` — the route does the same for `website`, `avatarUrl`, `bannerUrl`.
- `useContestEditor.ts:429` — "Remove banner" sends `undefined`; the banner stays and its focal point is destroyed.
- Round 1's *"Clearing a PII answer silently fails: the private-field write is skipped and the old value survives"* is this cause. Nobody attributed it.

The comment at `useContentSave.ts:82` documents the real trap: the validators are `.optional()` but not `.nullable()`, so `null` 400s with an opaque "Validation failed." The client's coercion is a rational workaround for a schema that has no vocabulary for *clear*. Fixing it at any one call site just moves the 400.

**Family B — passing the row-level check is treated as field-level authority.** The merge loop applies no per-field policy, so any field the Zod schema accepts is writable by anyone who can edit the row:

- `events.ts:252` — `isFeatured` sits in the same list as `title`, so the creator can pin their own event to the public Featured tab. (Round 2 found this twice, from two dimensions, which is itself a signal.)
- `hub.ts:392` — `parentHubId` is written straight from the body; a hub attaches under someone else's company hub.
- `role.put.ts:15` — only the literal value `'admin'` is guarded; `'staff'` rides through, and there is no self-target check.

**Family C — cross-field invariants evaporate.** `packages/schema/src/validators/contest.ts:451`: the date refine passes vacuously when the partial body omits one side of the comparison. A partial merge cannot enforce an invariant over fields it cannot see, and no route re-checks after the merge.

Why nobody named it: the three families surface as a UX bug, a privilege bug, and a validation bug, filed by three different reviewers. The shape is invisible unless you grep for the merge itself.

### Runner-up: **one guarded front door, N unguarded side doors**

Every resource has a canonical route that gates visibility and a set of *derived representations* that don't: `content/[id]/products.get.ts:8` (sub-resource JSON, no viewer parameter at all), `hub-post-ap.ts:56` (AP representation, not scoped to the hub in the URL), `sitemap.xml:40` (index representation ignores `profileVisibility`), `content-redirect.ts:43` (redirect representation leaks draft existence, author and type), `hubs/[slug]/feed.xml.get.ts:61` (per-viewer authz served with `Cache-Control: public` — the cache *becomes* the side door), `image-proxy.get.ts:45` (asset representation), `layouts/by-route.get.ts:90` (derived surface reads the draft row instead of the published snapshot).

The evidence that this is structural: `packages/server/src/content/visibility.ts:12` says `visibleContentWhere` *"MUST be composed into every non-owner content read."* The rule is written down and enforced by memory. Outside contests there is **no `canView*` function at all** — content, hub, product, docs, video and event visibility is re-derived inline at every call site, and a derived representation added later has nothing to forget to call.

This also explains four round-1 findings nobody grouped: `/api/users` enumeration, `GET /api/users/:username` on a private profile, `public/v1/events/{slug}` missing the private-hub gate, and contest vote/withdraw skipping `canViewContest`.

### Third, smaller: **copy is a second, unverified source of truth about behaviour**

`contest.ts:304` ("we'll email you" — status notifications skip registrants entirely), `templates.ts:137` (confirmation email promises reminders a `contestReminders`-off instance never sends), `settings/notifications.vue:30` (the settings screen's own save reverses a one-click unsubscribe). Three places where user-facing text asserts a behaviour gated by a flag or code path the text does not consult. Same root as the memory note *"derive, do not declare twice."*

---

## 3. Top 8, by blast radius × cheapness

**1. Hoist the form-field key lock out of the registration builder.** deveco is running a live contest with real entrants; an organizer editing a stage-form label right now orphans stored submission answers and consent rows. The lock (snapshot-at-hydrate of server-persisted keys) lives in the wrong component — move it into `FormTemplateEditor` so all three producers inherit it, and make markdown import reuse the existing key for any label that matches a locked field rather than regenerating.
`layers/base/components/contest/FormTemplateEditor.vue:187`, `ContestStageCard.vue:179`, `ContestRegistrationForm.vue`.

**2. Allowlist raster content types in the image proxy.** Unauthenticated stored XSS, same-origin, on a public route. Replace `contentType.startsWith('image/')` (`image-proxy.get.ts:45`) with an explicit set — `image/jpeg|png|gif|webp|avif` — and add `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` plus `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` to the response headers at line 49. Five lines, one file.

**3. Cap recipient expansion on the public inbox.** `inboxHandlers.ts:521`. De-dupe `to`/`cc`, reject or slice above a fixed N, and length-cap the stored body before the insert loop. One signed POST currently writes thousands of full-size rows on any federating instance, and it replays inside the 5-minute skew window.

**4. Stop the dynamic OAuth endpoint from returning an existing secret.** `packages/server/src/federation/oauth.ts:415` returns `existing.clientSecret` verbatim, and `layers/base/server/api/auth/oauth2/register.post.ts` is unauthenticated by design, gated only on `features.federation`. Any caller who names an already-registered `instance_domain` receives that instance's `client_secret`. Make the idempotent branch return `client_id` only (or 409), never the secret.

**5. Fix the three unauthenticated read leaks as one commit** — they are one cause, and each is 1–3 lines. Thread the viewer plus `visibleContentWhere` into `listContentProducts` (`product.ts` / `content/[id]/products.get.ts:8`); filter `sitemap.xml:40` on `profileVisibility = 'public'`; scope the `hub-post-ap.ts:56` Note lookup by the hub slug already in the URL. A private project's BOM and private hub posts are anonymously readable today.

**6. Remove the privileged-field ride-along in the three proven places.** Drop `isFeatured` out of the creator-writable merge in `updateEvent` (`events.ts:252`) into an admin-gated branch; require `admin.access` for **any** system role, not just the literal `'admin'`, and add a self-target guard (`role.put.ts:15`); authorize `parentHubId` against the caller's membership in `createHub` (`hub.ts:392`). Three small edits that close the demonstrated instances; the other 113 merge sites are the follow-up audit, not this commit.

**7. Fix the conditional-field save before it ships.** This is uncommitted work from the session running the audit: ticking "Only show this field when…" emits a shape the server's Zod rejects, so the whole contest save 400s (`FormTemplateEditor.vue:244`), and an option value with surrounding whitespace validates as a condition that can never match (`packages/schema/src/contest.ts:289`). Trim on both sides and make the editor emit the schema's exact `showWhen` shape. Free now, an incident after deploy.

**8. Bind the engagement seed and the permission seed to real SSR data.** `useEngagement.ts:101` — replace `ref(0)` with the count already present on the SSR'd DTO, fixing every content page's HTML. `plugins/auth.ts:15` — include the resolved permission set in the SSR payload so a non-admin `users.manage` grantee sees a populated sidebar instead of an empty one. Two files, both small, and together they make delegated RBAC actually usable.

Just below the line, in order: the docs `parentId` cycle (`docs.ts:422` — a two-request cycle loops `buildPagePath`, which is a request-hang, not just hidden pages); `feed.xml.ts:35` raw C0 control characters (one regex in the escape helper, but fatal to the entire feed for every subscriber when it fires); `layouts/by-route.get.ts:90` serving draft rows publicly. And `useContentSave.ts:89` is the single highest-blast-radius item in the whole audit — no user can clear any content field — but the fix is `.nullable()` across the validators plus `?? null` at 116 merge sites, so it is a scheduled piece of work, not a patch.

---

## 4. What round 2 still did not reach

**`packages/protocol/` — again.** This was the critic's headline complaint about round 1, and round 2 has **zero findings in a `packages/protocol/` file.** The "protocol-security" dimension audited `packages/server/src/federation/*` and `layers/base/server/utils/inbox.ts` — what happens *after* verification. Unreviewed: `keypairs.ts:54` `verifyHttpSignature` (signature-string parsing is hand-rolled over `split(',')`; there is a skew window but no nonce/seen-id cache, so replay inside 5 minutes is open), `actorResolver.ts` key caching and rotation, `webfinger.ts`, `ssrf.ts`, and `sanitize.ts` itself — the file that declares itself the critical boundary.

**Meilisearch.** Both rounds audited `/api/search` *query parameters* and neither opened the index. Nobody checked whether draft, `private` or `members` content is indexed, whether deletes and visibility changes propagate to the index, or whether results are filtered per viewer. The side-door cause predicts a leak here and it is untested.

**`packages/persona/`.** Zero findings, either round. It is the newest privacy-critical code in the repo, five days live, and deveco is the one instance with all four flags ON via DB override. The audit covered the persona *admin copy* and nothing else.

**Transactions as a class.** Round 1 found individual races. Nobody enumerated which multi-table writes run outside a transaction. `createHub` (`hub.ts:392`) is two unwrapped statements — a failure between them leaves a hub with `memberCount: 1` and no owner row, and it was read for a different finding without anyone noticing.

**Migration content.** The migrations dimension covered index declarations and deploy ordering. Nobody diffed the 48 migration files against `packages/schema/src/*` for drift, checked idempotency, or ran `drizzle-kit check`.

**Better Auth.** Session cookie flags, session invalidation on privilege change, reset-token entropy/expiry/single-use, email-change flow, and the `packages/auth` AP SSO linking beyond the one callback CSRF from round 1.

**Never opened at all:** `layers/base/pages/cert` + `layers/base/server/api/cert` (named as untouched by round 1's critic, still untouched), `tools/worker`, `packages/theme-studio`, `packages/explainer`, `packages/ui`, `test-site/`, and the referral attribution write path (one cookie finding, no audit of self-referral or the signup join).

**Method gaps.** Both rounds are 100% static reading. Nobody ran the app. Several findings assert "every call 500s" (`docs/[siteSlug]/versions.post.ts:10`) or "always returns empty" — each is one curl against a dev instance, and none were confirmed that way. Nobody read live flag state, though memory explicitly warns that flags drift per instance and deveco carries DB overrides, so every "the flag is off, therefore X" claim in both reviews is unverified against the boxes that matter. And the one reviewer who ran the layer's Vitest found it exits 1 on the touched suites — meaning the gate the whole session was trusting is red, and no other dimension noticed.
